# Shyland Architecture

> Authoritative technical reference as of commit 419a30a (v25.12 brief 1: the durability-posture invariant (#311/#312/#314) — one shared validator behind the admin form, the agent door, and the repo's first CheckConstraint; artifacts don't wear; durability is integral; the final hash lands the playtest-found bot-side schema mirror)
> Describes what is built. For design intent see the current GDD.
>
> **Version 25.12 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_12`: the durability-posture invariant (#311 founding ticket; #312, #314 dependencies).** Every bot-created artifact definition inherited the model-default pair `takes_durability_loss=True` + `durability_table=[]`, and `get_durability_penalty`'s no-band-matched fallback (1.0) treated pristine items as fully degraded — say-voice durability cells and fully-penalized strikes (#311); the manual repair path was itself blocked because the admin form refused legitimately-empty JSON lists (#312); and the door's `durability_current` instance edit accepted fractional values no seed band covers, silently drawing the same fallback (#314). The ruled invariant (GDD §6.5): **wear on requires a table covering every integer 0–100 (bands may touch or overlap — first-match-wins at read time is unchanged); wear off requires `[]`; nothing between the two postures saves, anywhere** — and the 1.0 fallback itself stays exactly as it was, deliberately: with the invariant enforced the state is unreachable, and softening it would mask future misconfiguration. One shared pure validator — `durability_posture_violations(takes_durability_loss, durability_table)`, module-level in `models.py` (placement avoids the `item_utils` import cycle), returning human-readable violation strings — behind three enforcement layers: `ItemDefinition.clean()` (new; `ValidationError` keyed to `durability_table` so the admin form renders errors in place), door-side validation on the new edit keys, and the repo's first `CheckConstraint` — `durability_posture_coherent`, `~Q(takes_durability_loss=True, durability_table=[])` in the new `ItemDefinition.Meta`, the cheap core (Django ≥ 5.1 `condition=` kwarg). **#312:** `blank=True` on exactly six fields — `ItemDefinition.valid_slots`/`.durability_table`/`.primary_stats`/`.secondary_stat_pool`, `ItemInstance.rolled_primary_stats`/`.rolled_secondary_stats` (`LootTableEntry.rarity_weights` deliberately stays required — never legitimately empty, must sum to 100). **Migrations, order load-bearing:** the authored data migration `0055_normalize_durability_posture` first (forward: broken-pair definitions flipped non-wearing, then any non-integral `durability_current` normalized to `float(int(round(v)))` with `is_broken` re-derived — one UTC-Z-stamped count line each; reverse noop; dev counts 0 and 0 — the operator's shell mitigation had already flipped the one affected row, `Ludicrous Speed`; prod counts land in the closeout tail's deploy output), then the generated `0056` (the six `blank=True` alters + `AddConstraint`). **The door:** `_create_artifact` authors `takes_durability_loss=False, durability_table=[]` explicitly — **artifacts don't wear**, and the artifact spec vocabulary gains no durability keys; `ARTIFACT_DEFINITION_EDIT_KEYS` gains `takes_durability_loss` (boolean) and `durability_table` (list), the coupling judged **on post-edit state** through the shared validator (the mystery-pairing structure), any violation ⇒ `bad-params` carrying the violation text — whole-request atomicity and the artifact-only gate ride `_edit_item`'s existing structure; **#314:** `durability_current` edits accept only integral values (`int`, or a float with `value.is_integer()` — JSON clients may deliver `42.0`), fractional ⇒ `bad-params` naming the integral rule, range check and `is_broken` invariant unchanged. Code + migrations only — no seed change, no client change. Suite 891 → 915 (`test_v25_12_brief1.py`: validator/clean()/constraint/door-posture/integral-rule/admin-re-save, 24 tests). **The recorded deviation (operator-approved in-session):** the brief's "zero modified tests" premise did not survive contact — ~139 existing tests built fixture definitions through the model-default broken pair (an `IntegrityError` under the new constraint, by design) and one door test pinned fractional acceptance; the repair authors an explicit all-worn band `[{'min': 0, 'max': 100, 'penalty': 1.0}]` on those fixtures — behavior-preserving: exactly the retired empty-table fallback they ran under — and converts the fractional pin to an integral-rule refusal with its nonzero-clears-broken intent preserved on a whole number. **The playtest-found gap (fixed in-session):** the brief's change surface was door-side only, but its playtest step 4 asks sudo to drive the new edit keys — and the bot's `edit_item` schema (which mirrors the door's param shapes by doctrine) had not grown them, so the model had no vocabulary for the edit and, worse, warned admins that items "cannot be exempt from durability" while the door was authoring exactly that; the fix mirrors the two keys into the bot's `edit_item` schema (coupled-pair descriptions), notes the integral rule on `durability_current`, and states in the `create_artifact` description and system prompt that artifacts are born non-wearing (never warn otherwise). Stamp 25.12; the header hash moves to 419a30a (architectural change: model constraint + door vocabulary; the final hash lands the playtest-found bot-side mirror). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**
>
> **Version 25.11 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_11`: the zero-tool bounce (#308 founding ticket, sole milestone member).** V25.10's playtest surfaced the marked fabrication's unmarked sibling: delivery-claim prose (*"Sent — full memory list delivered to your pane."*) on a turn with **zero tool calls** — no `[did:]` mark, no receipts, no machine-detectable signature. The ruled fix is signature-free machinery in `_handle_sudo`'s tool loop: when a **nonempty** would-be final answer arrives and **no tool call has executed at any point this turn** (success or error both count as executed), the answer is bounced **once** with a corrective `[game]`-register user message (mirroring the receiptless-`[did:]` gate's form); the redo's answer is then delivered without a second bounce — two per-request locals (`ran_tool`, `bounced`) carry the state. Gate order is fixed: the existing receiptless-`[did:]` gate evaluates first and is unchanged (loop-capped, multi-fire); the zero-tool gate sees only answers that pass it. Model silence (no text, no tool calls) stays sanctioned and is never bounced; the bounce consumes one `TOOL_LOOP_CAP` iteration, and cap exhaustion falls through to the strip guard and delivery path exactly as before. The accepted, operator-ruled cost: every genuinely conversational turn draws exactly one extra model round trip. Change surface `agents/sudo_bot.py` only (host-side; nothing in the Docker image beyond the version stamp); `StubBrain`'s canned flow always answers after an executed tool call, so the gate never fires on it — no stub edits. No new in-suite tests (the suite cannot see `agents/`; the scripted-brain driver pattern over the real dev door proved all four behaviors — precedent v25.9/v25.10). Suite steady at 891.
>
> **Version 25.10 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_10`: truthful delivery and the filing bot (#301 founding ticket; #304, #305, #306 riding the release). **The attach singleton (#304, operator-ruled):** one agent account = one attached connection — hard reject, **never takeover**, close **4409**. The claim registry (`ATTACHED`, agent username → `channel_name`) is a module-level in-process dict in `mc_consumer.py`, deliberately not Redis: a crash destroys the registry together with the connections it guards, making a stale lock structurally impossible (single Daphne process — no worker fan-out). Gate order in `connect` is fixed: auth → membership (4403) → kill switch (4503) → singleton (4409) — a killed door reports killed, not conflict; a non-member sees 4403 either way (the leak law). A duplicate is accepted, sent the `already-attached` error frame, closed 4409, and never claims or sets `_agent`; `disconnect` releases under the guarded-presence-delete discipline (only when the entry holds this connection's `channel_name`), so a rejected duplicate's teardown cannot disturb the holder. Freed-slot guarantee: clean stop or `kill -9` closes the socket → `disconnect` fires → slot freed; a half-dead peer is reaped by Daphne's default WS ping (20s/30s) within ~50s; a Django restart destroys registry and connections together. Bot-side `CLOSE_MEANINGS` gains 4409 — the existing capped backoff is the correct response: the refused bot waits patiently, then attaches. **The report family (#306):** `REPORT_KINDS` grows to (`inventory`, `waypoints`, `memories`, `memory`), `a_report` kind-dispatched — `inventory` byte-identical (the v25.8 tests pass zero-edit); `waypoints`/`memories` list the **calling agent's** store newest-first (cap `MEMORY_LIST_CAP`, **true total counted before the slice**; leader `sudo: {total} waypoints|memories` with a `(newest N shown)` suffix on truncation, the leader alone the report at total 0; lines `#{id} {name} — {path}` / `#{id} {kind} {name} — {summary}` with live paths, `(room no longer exists)` when dangling); `memory` renders one row's detail (`where:` live path, or the bundle's `{quantity}× {slug} Mk {mk_tier} {rarity}` lines, then `taught by {username}`/`(unknown)` and `created {ISO} / updated {ISO}`). Machinery composes, the game renders, model prose is commentary (the standing doctrine, GDD §10.11): the bot's `report` schema grows the kinds (`character` schema-optional, integer `id` added — the door validates authoritatively), `_compose_receipt` goes kind-aware (`report ({kind})[ on {character}] to {to}: {status}` — never `on None`), and the prefer-report standing order extends over the bot's own store. **The store remembers what the game confirmed (#305):** `_handle_sudo` computes `receipts = actions.receipts()` **before** the record call and folds them into the persisted answer as `[did: {receipt}]` lines (text absent ⇒ the lines alone; the `{'q','a'}` exchange shape unchanged); a new prompt sentence makes a past answer claiming an action without a `[did: …]` line worthless as license (the v25.9 standing orders stand beneath — defense-in-depth). **Sudo files the tickets (#301):** `file_issue` — the first `BOT_ACTIONS` member, executed bot-side in `_execute_tool` before the door round trip (never a door frame; **the door grows no filing vocabulary; the game box holds no GitHub credentials**); the confirm gate is behavioral by design this release (tool description + prompt: gather Q&A style, thin is correct, read the complete draft back verbatim, file only on an explicit yes) while the filing's honesty is structural — the `filed issue #{number}: {url}` receipt carries the API response's values, never model text (**no ledger harvest** — issue numbers belong to no door id-space, deliberately), riding the turn's `answer` delivery and persisting with the stored answer. Filed issues are thin: title + body + the provenance footer (`_Filed via sudo (in-game) by {actor}, {UTC}Z._`), assignee by machinery, no labels — triage fattens them. Missing token ⇒ legible `not-configured` refusal; the token lives on the bot host (`agents/.secrets/github-token.<name>`, injected by botctl as `GITHUB_TOKEN` child-environment-only; the startup log says only `filing=enabled|disabled`). **The central runtime directory (#304):** pid/log/convo state moves to `~/.shyland/` in both `sudo_bot.py` and `botctl.py` (created at start); `module` and `.secrets/` stay checkout-scoped — code belongs to the checkout it ships in; no state migration, old per-checkout files abandoned in place (a bot started by old code owns an old-location pidfile the new botctl cannot see — the door's 4409 is the backstop, the 25.9 incident shape now failing safe). **Code-only release: no model change, no migration, no seed, no client change.** Suite 877 → 891 (`test_v25_10_brief1.py`: 6 singleton + 8 report tests); live dev verification — the singleton drill (duplicate refused 4409 with the holder's log untouched, slot freed on stop, then admitted), state files under `~/.shyland/` only, a scripted-brain driver proving `[did: …]` persistence in the dev conversation store, the `not-configured` filing refusal (nothing filed), and captured pane frames matching the brief's table exactly. Stamp 25.10; the header hash moves to 66896ca (architectural changes: a new consumer-level invariant, new door report kinds, a new bot capability, the bot runtime relocation). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**
>
> **Version 25.9 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_9`: structural receipts (#302 founding ticket, sole milestone member): the sudo bot never invents a value it should have read from the database — every id it acts on and every action it claims traces to a tool receipt from the current turn, **enforced by machinery, never asked of the model** (the v25.8 prompt standing orders remain in force — defense-in-depth, no longer the only line). Three layers (operator-ruled 2026-08-28). **Door-side:** `move` gains a third mutually exclusive destination key, `waypoint` (the memory name) — exactly-one-of-three replaces the two-key XOR (`bad-params` naming all three); resolution is **lookup-and-act atomic in the door** against the calling agent's own store (the memory verbs' addressing law: `agent=_agent_user(agent_name)`, kind waypoint, `name__iexact`), so the bot never handles a room id for a taught place; no memory of the name ⇒ `not-found` naming the waypoint, a stored room since deleted ⇒ `not-found` naming both the waypoint and the vanished room id (the legible-refusal law); the result gains `waypoint` (the row's cased name) only when the waypoint form was used — `to_name`/`to_room_id` behavior and result shape byte-identical. **Delivery-side:** `answer` gains machinery-only `receipts` — a list of 1–20 non-empty strings ≤ 200 chars (`MAX_RECEIPTS`/`MAX_RECEIPT_LEN`, every violation a `bad-params` naming the rule broken); `text` becomes optional when receipts are present (unchanged rule when given; neither present ⇒ `bad-params`); online delivery is the `sudo: {text}` line exactly as before (skipped when no text) then **one `sudo did: {receipt}` line per receipt in list order**, all category `sudo`, each through `_send_player_line` → the audited send (receipts on the MC record like all door output) — the `sudo did:` prefix is door-composed, so model text can never occupy line-start position on a receipt line; offline `delivered: false` posture and result shape unchanged; **no client change** (category `sudo` renders since v25.5). **Bot-side (the typed ledger):** a per-request `ReceiptLedger` — one sudo request = one turn = one ledger — with four id-spaces (**room** int, **item** int instance ids, **memory** int, **stream** str); `_execute_tool` (the single choke point) refuses any checked argument (`move.to_room_id` room; `item`/`remove_item`/`edit_item`/`equip_item`/`unequip_item` `item_id` item; `memory`/`forget` `id` memory; `event.stream_id` stream) whose value no current-turn successful tool result produced **in the matching space** — an error tool_result `{'error': 'unreceipted-id', …}` the model recovers from inside the loop by looking the value up, the door never called; `move` with `waypoint` or `to_name` has no checked argument — nothing to invent. Harvest is a **per-tool result-path map** (never a generic integer sweep — **the typing is the point**: v25.8's playtest failure passed a receipted integer, a memory's own id, into the wrong id-space, and an untyped ledger would have licensed it), successful results only, missing keys skipped silently. Alongside it an `ActionLog`: every successful action except `answer` appends one composed receipt — bot machinery from the call's params and the door's result, never model text (`moved <name> from <path> to <path>` with the waypoint suffix, `remembered kind 'name' (result, id N)`, `forgot …`, `equipped/unequipped/removed/edited item N on <name> …`, `created artifact …`, `stripped`/`dressed <name>`, `report on … delivered`); **identical (slug, mk_tier, rarity, to) gifts aggregate to one `×N` receipt** (ids omitted when aggregated — per-instance ids live in the MC record; without this a 50-line bundle replay would flood the bound), receipts truncated bot-side to 200 chars and capped at 20 oldest-kept. `_deliver` sends `receipts` alongside `text`, and **delivery now happens when there is text OR receipts** — a turn whose model went silent but whose actions succeeded delivers receipts-only: model prose is commentary, receipts are the record. Schema/prompt tightening: the `move` schema gains `waypoint` ("for taught places, always prefer this over to_room_id") and exactly-one-of-three; every checked argument's description carries the "from a tool result in this turn; unreceipted ids are refused" contract; the system prompt's waypoint clause now passes the name to `move` directly (never resolving the room id bot-side), and one added sentence tells the model the game renders `sudo did:` lines itself — never enumerate receipts, summarize plainly. **Code-only release: no model change, no migration, no seed, no client, no new door kinds, no kill-switch change** (the new param and destination form ride existing choke points). Suite 865 → 877 (`test_v25_9_brief1.py`, 12 socket-driven door tests); a 16/16 deterministic driver run proved the bot machinery live on dev (scripted-brain SudoBot pipeline over the real door: unreceipted `to_room_id` refused with the door never called; the same id after a `rooms` lookup executing; a room id offered to `forget` refused — the typed-ledger proof; the waypoint move single-hop with the result carrying the name; `sudo did:` lines landing in a live admin pane, text + receipts and receipts-only both). Operator playtest successful (2026-08-28); two bot-side findings fixed in-session — the replay trap (the conversation store replays history as text only, so the model's own stripped transcript taught it that a text-only "Saved" answers a teach request; fixed as a standing order naming the trap) and its poisoned-history variant (the model's earlier false "Saved" claim persisted in the store as ground truth; remediated by resetting the dev conversation file) — with the structural findings filed design-lane: #304 (cross-checkout bot singleton), #305 (persist receipts with the stored answer), #306 (everything the bot delivers should render deterministically). Stamp 25.9; the header hash moves to a73fbb0 (architectural change: door vocabulary shapes and new bot machinery; the final hash lands the playtest-found replay-trap standing order). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**
>
> **Version 25.8 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_8`: bot memory and record search (#294 founding ticket; #290, #296, #299, #300 riding the release): permanence and reach for the sudo bot's first week of real use. **The store (#294):** `AgentMemory` (migration `0054`) — generic per-bot durable storage, one row per taught fact: direct `User` FK `agent` (agent accounts are Users, never a Character), `kind` (`waypoint`/`bundle` — kinds own their payload shapes, the door validates per kind at teach time), `name` (≤ 60, case-insensitively unique per (agent, kind) — the Character-name `Lower()` constraint precedent), JSON `data`, `SET_NULL` `taught_by` audit (the teaching admin's character name resolved to their User; unresolvable ⇒ null — audit, not authorization), indexed `created_at`. **The memory verbs:** `remember` — kind-aware teach validation (waypoint `data` exactly `{room_id}` with the room existing — **the room PK and nothing else**, no path snapshot, nothing duplicative that can go stale; bundle `data` exactly `{lines: [[slug, mk_tier, rarity, quantity], …]}`, 1–50 lines, every slug a real definition, the gift laws applied at teach time, artifact rarity refused — bundles replay `gift`), CI upsert by (agent, kind, name) returning `created`/`replaced` (replace takes the new casing), three distinct legible cap refusals, never silent truncation (`memory-full` 262,144 rows = 1 GiB / 4 KiB, checked on create only — a replace never grows the store; `payload-too-large` 4,096 serialized bytes; `too-many-lines` 50); `forget` — **by PK only, never by name** (read-before-delete, the door's mutation discipline); `memories` — newest-first, capped 50, optional kind/name-substring/since–until filters, each row carrying a **live-rendered summary** (waypoint → the current `Zone: Area: Room` path via one `in_bulk` joined query, `(room no longer exists)` when dangling; bundle → `<N> lines`); `memory` — the full row, bundle data rendered with labeled fields (stored positional, rendered legible). Every verb scopes to the requesting agent's own store — one bot never reads or forgets another's. **Bundle replay is not a door feature**: the bot replays a bundle as N ordinary `gift` calls, fresh generation each time (ruled). **Rooms (#290):** `rooms` — CI substring on `Room.name`, optional zone-substring narrowing, zone-then-room order, cap 50, `_room_dict` rows (duplicate names are simply multiple rows); and `a_move`'s receipt gains `from_room` (origin captured before the move) — "send them back" is a receipt read now. **Record search (#300):** `events` — the record is the journal: time-windowed MCEvent search, defaults `until` = now / `since` = `until` − 24h; filters `kind` (exact), `actor` (int id or CI name), `room_id`, and payload-substring `text`; **a `text` search must run inside a ≤ 7-day span** (legible refusal: "narrow the window and walk backwards"); indexed fields bound the scan (the composite `(kind, ts)`/`(actor_id, ts)`/`(room_id, ts)` indexes are the point), newest-first inside the window with `text` applied in that bounded walk, cap 50, 120-char serialized-payload gist; `event` — one full record by `stream_id`. **The rendered report (#296):** `report` (kind `inventory`, the only v1 kind) — the `a_answer` resolution and admin gate on `to`, offline ⇒ `ok: true, delivered: false`; delivery **privately to the recipient's pane only**: a door-composed leader `sudo: <Name> (<N> items total)` in the sudo voice (N = live total owned instances — the model never writes this line), then the equipped and carried sections **through the very compositions the player commands render with** — extracted from `SkylandConsumer` to `item_utils` on the 25.7 `equip_candidates` precedent (`table_lines`, `details_cell`, `slot_cell`, `equipment_doll_lines`, `inventory_table_lines`, with `SLOT_ORDER` and `STACKABLE_ITEM_TYPES` moving alongside; the consumer keeps thin delegates under its original names — player-facing output **byte-identical**, the pre-existing rendering tests passing with zero edits) — each section a structured-lines `player_message` in the player `report` category (the consumer's `player_message` handler gains the lines branch, rendered exactly like `send_report_lines` output). **Bot side:** eight new tools (schemas mirroring the door shapes; routing sets grown), and the system prompt's new standing orders — taught facts go in durable memory not conversation, `forget` looks the id up first, "what happened" questions search the record walking backwards from now, inventory questions prefer `report` over hand-written rosters. **Target-scoped state (#299):** `sudo_bot.py` pid/conversation files derive from a required `--target {dev,prod}` on `run`/`status`/`stop` (`.sudo_bot.<target>.pid`, `.sudo_bot_conversations.<target>.json`); `botctl.py`'s `BotPaths(name, target)` derives the `<target>`-suffixed log and passes the target through — **a dev stop is incapable of touching the prod pidfile by construction**; the gitignore conversation line widened to the suffixed forms; no state-file migration (stale unsuffixed files ignored; the operator restarts bots after deploy, the standing action). All new kinds inherit kill-switch gating and MC record emission from the consumer's frame handling with zero extra wiring (the 25.7 receipt, still true). Suite 840 → 865 (`test_v25_8_brief1.py`, 25 socket-driven tests); a 31-check deterministic driver run proved the full sequence live on dev (teach → list → detail → move-with-origin → bundle-replay gifts → forget; the just-emitted records searched back through the live persister; the report landing in a real player pane — leader, `Equipment...`, `Inventory (…)...`, in order); two-target coexistence proven without ever starting a prod-pointed bot. Operator playtest successful (2026-08-27), three bot-side findings fixed in-session — all variants of the model inventing values instead of reading them (a "saved" answered with no `remember` call; a move declined from stale "already there" belief with zero tool calls; waypoint moves teleporting through a guessed wrong room before the corrective hop) — each fixed as a system-prompt standing order (receipts-not-intentions; a move request is a location decision; `to_room_id` only from current-turn tool results), with the structural answer filed as #302 (make receipts machinery, not behavior). Stamp 25.8; the header hash moves to 7752dd7 (architectural change: new model, eight vocabulary kinds, the composition extraction, the scoped agents runtime; the final hash lands the three playtest-found prompt fixes). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**
>
> **Version 25.7 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_7`: granular item control (#287 founding ticket; #293, #288, #289, #292, #295 riding the release): the agent door's item vocabulary — two queries and four actions over existing fields. No model change, no migration, no seed data; every new kind inherits MC attribution and the kill switch from the consumer's frame handling with zero extra wiring (Section 4.20's machinery, unchanged). **Queries (#293):** `inventory` — the uncapped owner-wide roster, every instance the character owns, equipped included and flagged (deliberately unlike the player command's protective pools; `ITEMS_CAP` stays a catalog concern); `item` — one instance at full fidelity, **true state** (curse and identification flags exactly as stored — the no-leak rule governs world speech, which queries never touch), with owner/room holder context. **Actions:** shared write-path addressing (`name` + `item_id`; a missing instance is `not-found`, any other or no owner is `not-owner` naming the actual state; **no string-based item resolution on any write path** — the bot resolves via `inventory` first, a stale id is a refusal never a guess), one `transaction.atomic()` per mutation, narration through the audited send only when the target is online (category `system`; `item_ref` composed before any deletion). `remove_item` (#287): destruction never transfer, the `_strip` posture (no combat gate, no protective guards); **the curse ends with the item** (the death-teardown pattern applied to the one effect: components then the `EffectInstance`, `removed_by='item-removed'`); artifact removal deletes the definition — the CASCADE takes the instance and the unique name/slug free for re-authoring (the freed-name law, test-pinned by a create→remove→re-create round trip); bar-law rescale when the target was equipped, `refresh_status` riding only then. `edit_item` (#289): raw-set whitelists — instance-side on any owned instance (`mk_tier`, `rarity`, rolled stats through the artifact-creation validators, the damage pair both-or-neither-null, `durability_current` maintaining `is_broken == (durability == 0)`), definition-side on artifacts only (`not-artifact` for shared templates; renames re-run the unique-name law excluding self, `name-taken` on collision); **any unknown key refuses the whole request** — no partial application; values land exactly, nothing re-rolls, `item_type` deliberately not editable. `equip_item`/`unequip_item` (#288): the player's slot-resolution algorithm itself — **lifted verbatim from `cmd_equip` into `item_utils.equip_candidates`** (with `SLOT_CAPACITY` moving alongside; `cmd_equip` consumes the helper, player behavior byte-identical, zero test edits) — **structural rules always hold** (valid slots, capacity, two-hander geometry), **protective guards yield** (the `_strip` precedent: cursed comes off, capacity ignored); ambiguous displacement refuses with `ambiguous` naming each option (slot + item + id — structured for the bot to relay or retry with an explicit `slot`); equip re-soulbinds (byte-consistent with `equip_item`/`_dress`); `outfit_snapshot` never read, written, or consumed (sentinel-pinned). **Bot-side:** `sudo_bot.py` gains the six tools (schemas mirroring the door shapes; `_execute_tool` unchanged — verified generic) and the two prompt laws (resolve-before-write, explicit-target-only); `cmd_run` normalizes `--url` once (`rstrip('/')` — #292: a trailing slash composed a double-slash login POST whose 302 masqueraded as a credential failure; the refused-login warning now appends the `Location` header, the tell). **`agents/botctl.py`** (#295, Section 4.24): the stdlib-only, self-locating bot manager — `<prod|dev> <start|stop|restart|status|tail> [--bot NAME]`. Suite 840 green in-container (33 new door tests); driver round-trips live on dev with MC records attributed; the botctl dev cycle proven with honest exit codes. Stamp 25.7; the header hash moves to bf9c6a4 (architectural change: new door vocabulary, a new bot-side component). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**
>
> **Version 25.6 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_6`: the sudo bot (#262 founding ticket; #279 and #284 dependencies riding the release): the first live MC actor — the bot-side half of the first-actor arc, a standalone Python process **`agents/sudo_bot.py`** deliberately outside the Django image (a remote client of the game, exactly like a player's browser; Section 4.23). Game-side changes deliberately tiny: the version constant, one nginx template line (#284: `location /accounts/login/` `burst=3 → 10`, rate untouched at 5r/m, django-defender untouched), one gitignore growth (`*.pid`, the conversation file). The bot logs in once as `agent-sudo` and reuses the session cookie across every reconnect (#284's bot-side mitigation; re-auth only on a refused handshake), verifies `hello {protocol: 2}` (refuses to run otherwise), fetches the live verb list via `query commands` into the system prompt — the ruled decline mechanism ("you don't need sudo for that — `<verb>` does it" for existing commands, "I don't know how to do that" for the unmappable; live list, zero hardcoded command names) — then attaches and reacts to `cmd` records with `verb == 'sudo'` and non-empty args, pre-checking `query is_admin` (cost discipline only; the authoritative gate remains the door's `answer`). Parsing and composition run behind the provider-agnostic **Brain** interface: `ClaudeBrain` (official `anthropic` SDK; model from config, default `claude-sonnet-5`; no sampling parameters; per-request `usage` token logging; `--max-tokens` default 5000, operator-ruled up from the brief-table 1000 after Sonnet 5's default-on thinking starved a request into silence) and `StubBrain` (deterministic canned `where_is` → answer, no model, no key — the end-to-end proof minus the model); `ollama` reserved by interface. The tool loop lives in the bot (cap 8): tools = the door vocabulary minus `answer`/`is_admin` (bot machinery, never model choices), schemas mirroring `mc_door`'s param shapes exactly; every model tool call is a proposal the bot executes as a door frame, every result — the full `DoorError` code set included — fed back as a tool result, so the model never touches the game. Answers deliver via `action answer` (≤2000 chars ellipsis-truncated; model-written `sudo:` prefixes stripped — the door prepends its own). Conversations are bot-side only (ruled): keyed by admin character name, ≤20 exchanges, 600s quiet expiry (an expired thread is indistinguishable from never answering), JSON-persisted across restarts (gitignored); every request turn arrives stamped `[Name]` so first-person requests ("move Harley here") resolve against the requester. **Silence is never an error**: unable to parse, declining, killed, or crashed all look identical in-pane — echo, then nothing. Kill posture (GDD §10.11 standing invariant, checked game-side): on 4503 the bot retries quietly and indefinitely, capped backoff 2s → 60s, one log line per attempt cycle; SIGTERM = clean close, conversations saved, pidfile removed. Ops surface (#268 posture): `run`/`status`/`stop` subcommands through a pidfile, UTC-Z log stamps, pre-flight refusals logged (never just stderr). Verification: suite 807 green at 25.6-DEV (no count regression); secrets/pidfile/log/conversation file gitignore-pinned; stub-brain end-to-end against the dev stack 17/17 (cmd spotted → `is_admin` pre-check → door round trip → `delivered: true` → sudo-color pane line); the kill drill (sever on `mc kill`, quiet 4503 retry, unaided reattach on `mc restore`, fresh round trip) — re-proven after the venv moved to Python 3.14 (websockets 17, anthropic 1.0.0). Operator playtest successful (2026-08-24), nine bot-side findings fixed in-session (double sudo-prefix, location-bar grammar for locations, fresh-queries-over-stale-conversation-memory, speaker identity on every turn, origin capture before every move, UTC stamps, logged refusals, urllib3 import noise, the max-tokens ruling); door-vocabulary gaps found and filed for design as one capability set — #287 (complete item removal, artifacts included), #288 (targeted equip/unequip), #289 (item mutation), #290 (room-directory query). `agents/requirements.txt` gains `anthropic`; #279 closes — the in-repo agents harness proven against dev. Production `agent-sudo` provisioning (create user, set password, join `agents.shyland`) is a deploy-time action, executor the operator, in the closeout tail's window — no seed, no migration, a human act by design. Stamp 25.6; the header hash moves to 37deb96 (architectural change: a new top-level component — the first bot-side actor). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**
>
> **Version 25.5 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_5`: the agent door (#281 founding ticket; #273 dependency riding the release): the game-side half of the first-actor arc — the MC egress grows from a read-only tail into three inbound vocabularies on one authenticated connection (`MC_PROTOCOL` 1 → 2): **tail** (`attach`/`ping`, byte-identical to v25.3), **query**, and **action**, dispatched through the new **`mc_door.py`** (Sections 4.20 and 4.22). Six query kinds (`commands`, `who_online`, `where_is`, `character`, `items`, `is_admin`) and six action kinds (`answer`, `gift`, `create_artifact`, `strip`, `dress`, `move`), all server-validated, character names resolved `iexact`, frames processed serially per connection (the day-one rate discipline, recorded deliberately — #261/#268 own per-agent scopes and limits). The trust boundary does not move (session auth, live `agents.shyland` membership, no DB/Redis credentials for agents, no player door, no `Character` for agent accounts); **the kill switch covers the whole door** — every query/action frame checks fresh before processing, killed ⇒ close 4503 (fail closed, the v25.4 law); **everything on the record** — one `agent_query`/`agent_action` stream record per processed frame (actor_name = the agent's username, everything else empty), and every player-visible line the door causes rides the door's **audited send** (`out` record with the target-pk audience first, then the group send; no bare `group_send` in door code — the `mc_group_send` discipline mirrored). Transparency (#261): effect narration tells the truth (`An admin ...`) in the world's standard colors — move/strip/dress lines in `system`, giving lines in `reward`; bots talk in their talking color only — the `answer` action delivers `sudo: {text}` as the new stamped **`sudo`** category (`--sudo-color: #E24B4A` — error-red's hex under a separable name, no new palette literal; `.msg-sudo`), gated by the target's **live `admins.shyland` membership** (#273's delivery gate — `not-admin` otherwise; offline is `ok: true, delivered: false`, never an error). Soulbind law: `gift` is the first production `gift=True` caller of `generate_item_instance` (Mk-mismatch guard surfaces as `invalid-item`; capacity deliberately unchecked — an admin gift lands regardless of carry state); `create_artifact` hand-authors the definition + instance (never through generation; §5.4 spec validation, rarity-word name law enforced at runtime, name/slug collisions = `name-taken`, born soulbound, sell-guard-refused by rarity). `strip`/`dress` ride the new **`Character.outfit_snapshot`** (migration `0053`, nullable JSONField): snapshot written before any unequip, deliberately bypassing `_unequip_blocked_reason` (admin tool; the #275 over-capacity state accepted knowingly), always consumed by a dress attempt, one bar-law rescale per action. `move` refuses `in-combat`; online targets get the full arrival treatment through their own consumer (the new `moved` `player_message` branch, respawn-modeled — the door defers the visit to it so a first visit announces zone completion; a `refresh_status` sibling branch re-syncs the status pane after strip/dress); offline targets get DB + visit only, no broadcasts. Shared-machinery extraction, behavior byte-identical: `_rescale_bars_for_gear` → `combat_utils.rescale_bars_for_gear`, `record_room_visit` → `models.record_room_visit_sync` — consumer wrappers delegate, the door shares them. Name law: `RESERVED_BOT_NAMES = frozenset({'sudo', 'sirius'})` in `models.py`, refused by the creation form with the NPC-collision sentence (no-leak — bots and NPCs indistinguishable in refusal); no existing Character/NpcDefinition collision (test-pinned, dev-verified). The v25.3 `read-only` error string is superseded by `unknown-frame` (ruled; pins updated). Suite 768 → 807 (`test_mc_agent_door.py`, 39 tests: the frame contract, all twelve kinds happy + error paths, kill-switch sever on both frame types, MC capture, fill-fraction invariance, the moved branch end-to-end, name reservation, the sudo pins). Stamp 25.5; the header hash moves to 2a50579 (architectural change: new module, new protocol, new model field). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**
>
> **Version 25.4 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_4`: the MC kill switch (#266 founding ticket; #277 dependency riding the release): one lever that silences every AI actor at once, landing before any actor exists (25.5 ships the first). `MCKillSwitch` (migration `0052`) — a database singleton (row pk=1 always; absent row = alive — the designed default, no seeded row; Postgres is the only store whose state survives restarts and reseeds), **config, not history** (the append-only rule governs `MCEvent`, not this row). Every surface routes through one classmethod, `MCKillSwitch.flip` — THE flip choke point: every actual state change emits one `mc_kill` record (`data={killed, surface}`, the flipper as `actor_name`), a no-change flip emits nothing (there is no state change to record); `is_killed()` reads fresh — no cache, no TTL, no module state, by ruling — and raises on DB failure so enforcement sites fail closed. The emit rides the new **`mc_emit_sync`** — `mc_emit`'s sync twin in `mc.py` (byte-compatible record shape, same fire-and-forget law, short-lived sync `redis.Redis` client per call; flips are rare by definition). Enforcement is egress-only: `MCEgressConsumer` gains the connect gate (membership checked first, so a non-member sees 4403 whether or not the switch is engaged — the switch leaks nothing; a killed member draws accept-then-close **4503**, the new close code — killed is not not-authorized), the per-batch replay sever (a kill during a long catch-up never waits for replay to finish), and the live-loop sever at the top of every iteration (the loop wakes at least every `LIVE_BLOCK_MS`, so a hung or rogue agent is severed within ~2s without its cooperation); **any failure to read the switch = killed.** **Capture never checks the switch** (grep-enforced: zero switch references in the persister and ticker) — the persister is monitoring infrastructure inside the trust boundary, not an actor; its only change is **#277**: `BLOCK_MS` 5000 → 2000, mirroring the b812afb egress fix (a server-side BLOCK equal to redis-py ≥ 8's default 5s client `socket_timeout` is a coin-flip race every idle cycle), both block constants now test-pinned at 2000 and asserted < 5000. Three flip surfaces: the **`mc <status|kill|restore>`** admin command (`admins.shyland`-gated with the fn-18 stealth machinery extended, never reimplemented — `ADMIN_VERBS`, `COMMAND_TABLE`, `DYING_ALLOWED`, the members-only help row, membership-gated tab completion, and a prefix-matched closed subcommand vocabulary in `cmd_mc`; allowed while dying and in combat per GDD §9.1, voices per the three-layer doctrine — usage = CLI error, already-in-state = warn, actual flip = success, `status` = an unstamped Kind-1 report line), the **documented shell helper** (the helper IS the classmethod, via `make shell`), and the **Django admin** — registered **editable**, the editable-config exception beside the read-only `MCEvent` pattern, `save_model` routed through `flip` (no `super()` call — `flip` performs the write) so the no-change-no-emit rule holds even from the form, add permission only while no row exists, delete disabled. Suite 744 → 768 (`test_mc_kill_switch.py`: flip semantics, the egress connect/replay/live gates + fail-closed, stealth byte-identity, command voices and prefix matching, surface wiring, block-constant pins, and — from the operator-playtest-found admin fix — both admins' permission contracts pinned: `MCEvent` read-only, the switch editable-config, admin saves routing through `flip`). Dev-proven live (the throwaway pattern, real Redis and DB): engaged → attach 4503; restored → hello/attach/live flows; kill while attached → severed 4503; four `mc_kill` rows T/F/T/F with correct `surface`; an 11-minute idle soak with zero persister timeout lines (#277). Kill-switch mechanics in Section 4.21. Stamp 25.4; the header hash moves to 64a49bf (architectural change: new model, new command surface, egress enforcement; the final hash lands the playtest-found admin-registration fix — the kill-switch class had split `MCEventAdmin` from its read-only permission trio, flipping both admin surfaces). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**
>
> **Version 25.3 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_3`: the MC egress (#267 founding ticket, no dependencies): remote agents attach to the MC event stream through a read-only WebSocket endpoint, `wss://<host>/ws/shyland/mc/` — transport only, no actor, no actuation, no kill switch (25.4/#266's territory). New module `apps/shyland/mc_consumer.py` (`MCEgressConsumer`), one operator-approved routing line, and data migration `0051` creating the `agents.shyland` Group (the 0034 shape — idempotent, no members seeded; agent service accounts are provisioned operationally, never by code). The gate is the group, not a character (R1/R2): unauthenticated → handshake rejected pre-accept; authenticated non-member → accept-then-close 4403; members get the full stream — no scoped subscriptions, no filters, and the consumer never touches the character table (agent accounts have no Character and are never routed to the creator). Read-only by vocabulary (R4): inbound is exactly `attach` and `ping`; anything else — a second `attach` included — draws `{"type":"error","error":"read-only"}` and the connection stays open. Protocol: `hello {protocol: 1}` on accept → the client's `attach` (bare = live from now; `after: <id>` = hot-window replay, exclusive, `XRANGE` batches of 500) → live tail. Gaps announced, never silent (R5): a cursor predating the window draws one `gap {requested, oldest}` frame (oldest null on an empty window) then replay from oldest; id comparison is numeric on both dash parts. Agents own their cursors (R5): stateless `XRANGE`/`XREAD` only — consumer groups remain the persister's mechanism alone. The live tail reads from a **concrete tail id, never `XREAD $`** (the `$` cursor drops entries landing between calls; the "now" snapshot resolves inside attach handling, so a ping/pong fence after attach is a hard delivery guarantee — found by the live dev-stack check, wire contract unchanged). Backpressure never reaches the game: `mc_emit` and every game path untouched (zero `mc_emit` references in the consumer, grep-enforced); a slow agent is Daphne/nginx's problem. Capture boundary: egress connections are not stream events — attach/detach get `shyland.mc` logger lines carrying the agent username; fleet observability is #268's arc. `mc._get_client` deliberately reused (not renamed): existing `mock.patch.object(mc, '_get_client', ...)` sites govern egress reads too — zero patch sites moved (the V25.2 D1 lesson, avoided by construction). No model change; 0051 is data-only; settings, nginx (the `/ws/` prefix already proxies), compose, persister: untouched. Suite 731 → 744 (`test_mc_egress.py`: the three-outcome gate, read-only vocabulary survival, bare-attach live flow, ordered resume-by-id, both gap variants, `entry_to_frame` decode robustness — malformed json passes through raw, empty ids → null — and the migration's group). Dev-proven live against the genuine stream: hello/attach/live flow, resume-by-id in order, non-member 4403. Endpoint, gate, protocol, and cursor doctrine in Section 4.20. Stamp 25.3; the header hash moves to b812afb (architectural change — new consumer, new route, new group; the final hash lands the playtest-found `LIVE_BLOCK_MS` fix). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**

> **Version 25.2 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_2`: combat instrumentation (#33 founding ticket, no dependencies): seven `combat_*` kinds join `mc:events`, capturing what the engine rolls — including what no player ever sees (a graze renders as an ordinary hit; roll interiors were discarded until now). Capture is never load-bearing (every emission fire-and-forget through `mc_emit`); no combat mechanic, message, or outcome changed — the detailed helper variants make the same random calls in the same order as the plain names they absorbed, pinned by seeded equivalence tests asserting both the return value and `random.getstate()`. No model change, no migration — the persister is kind-agnostic and the longest kind (`combat_action`, 13) fits `MCEvent.kind`. `MC_STREAM_MAXLEN` default 100k → 250k (ruling 8 on #33). Family schemas, emission sites, and the ordering contract in Section 4.19. Dev-proven live: a real ticker fight to a win/kill with every kind in stream and rows, per-round internals-first ordering against the `out` records, and the volume data point (≈1.03 combat records/sec in-fight vs the 0.6/sec non-combat baseline). GDD lockstep: §10.11's combat-family passage landed with the design session (`40cba4f`, pending-implementation marker — the closeout sweeps it). Stamp 25.2; the header hash moves to 7b2c32b (architectural change). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**

> **Version 25.1 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_1`: the MC sink ships — "the firehose works" (#37 founding ticket; #271 dependency): every game event is captured at creation level in both processes through one choke point, `apps/shyland/mc.py`'s fire-and-forget `mc_emit` (whole body guarded — a sink failure of any kind drops the record and never raises into game code; at most one warning per 60s). Consumer taps: the command tap (every submitted command, accepted or rejected — no outcome field, rejections are the stream-adjacent `out` records that follow — plus the `complete` request), presence (`connect`/`disconnect`; the unattributable pre-entry paths emit nothing by rule), the personal-out helpers (`send_output`/`output`/`send_report_lines`), the direct creation sends (deleted-character redirect, no-exit warn, quit, room-render, map, fight, and the new tapped `send_status` that consolidated the 7 status sites), and the `mc_group_send` broadcast wrapper — after which `channel_layer.group_send` appears in `consumers.py` only inside the wrapper (grep-enforced), audiences resolved at fan-out time honoring all three exclude semantics, `token` stripped (never log secrets). Ticker taps live inside the two existing outbound helpers only (`send_to_player` = one record per call even though delivery unpacks up to three client messages, `actor_name='ticker'`; `broadcast_to_room` = presence-filtered room audience). Chrome excluded by ruling: `ping`/`pong`, the connect-time `verbs` list, the `complete` response. Hot tier: Redis Stream `mc:events` on db 0 (shared with the channel layer and presence keys), `XADD maxlen=MC_STREAM_MAXLEN` (default 100000, approximate), volumeless by ruling — Postgres is durability. Durable tier: `MCEvent` (migration `0050`) — loose integer `actor_id`/`room_id` plus denormalized `actor_name`, deliberately no FKs into live tables (reseeds delete rows; the record survives); the `mc-persister` sixth container (same image) drains via consumer group `persister` (`XREADGROUP` COUNT 500 BLOCK 5000, `XAUTOCLAIM` idle >60s crash recovery, ack only after a successful write, `bulk_create(ignore_conflicts=True)` idempotent replay, malformed entries stored raw and acked, SIGTERM-graceful). The read-only `MCEvent` admin — add/change/delete all disabled — introduces the repo's read-only-admin pattern. #271 fixed: the `REDIS_HOST` settings constant is born (with `MC_STREAM_MAXLEN`), the presence URL builds from it, and the two inline env reads re-point at it — zero behavior change at any current deployment. A second hardcode site found during implementation — the ticker's `_online_character_pks` presence reader — was filed as **emergent #272 on operator direction and fixed in the same session** (`345f09d`, the header hash): every presence client in both processes now builds from the constant. Suite 687 → 702 (`test_mc_sink.py`: XADD shape, fire-and-forget + throttle, end-to-end chrome/ordering/audience/no-token proofs over the real consumer; `test_mc_persister.py`: mapping, ts derivation from the stream id, idempotent replay, malformed survival, DB backoff-without-ack). Dev-proven live: stream and rows in lockstep, the `cmd` row precedes its `say` broadcast's `out` row, persister stop/start with zero-gap catch-up. Stamp 25.1; the header hash moves to 345f09d (architectural change). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**

> **Version 25.0 (major: Version 25, MC — Monitoring and Command) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_25_0`: the MC major opens (#269 founding ticket; #260/#264 milestone siblings): the design session's doc pass landed at `5da479f` (GDD §10.11 total-capture doctrine, the firehose→MC retheme across the docs including this doc's delivery-choke-point passage), and this brief ships the version start plus the five-reference comment/docstring firehose→MC sweep in the game code (`envelope.py`, `consumers.py`, `models.py`) — the two designated-tap claims corrected to the ruled architecture (the choke point is the envelope stamp; MC captures at creation level, #37/#33). No functional change of any kind — server behavior byte-identical; no model change, no migration, no seed data, no template/static change. Suite 687, unchanged, no test edited beyond the version pin. Stamp 25.0; the header hash does not move (a comment-only sweep is not an architectural change). Updated in place; the major-opening document mechanics (`GDD_MAJOR` → 25, monolith rename, this doc's own rename to the v25 era) are closeout work per the standing major-opening rule.** — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_31`: deploy-target build-exhaust sweep (#205 founding ticket, #255 dependency): the first release whose implementation is entirely in the `Makefile`. Every `make build` left exhaust on the daemon it ran against — orphaned (`<none>`) image records plus BuildKit cache entries — and nothing swept it: production's root volume climbed 20% → 58% across ten releases (~500MB each) and Emma's dev VM accrued ~2GB per build, both needing manual operator catch-ups. **Both deploy targets now sweep automatically**, image prune then builder prune, gated behind `PROD_BUILDER_PRUNE_FLAGS` / `DEV_BUILDER_PRUNE_FLAGS` (`:=`, deliberately not overridable from the environment). **The reconciliation that determined the fix:** the issue body ruled a dangling-image prune only, but `docker image prune` reclaimed ~0B on both daemons — the dangling images' layers were *also* held by build-cache references, so pruning the image records released records, not bytes; the builder prune releases the last references and the layers actually die. Both prunes ship, and the builder prune is the load-bearing one. **Eviction is size-capped rather than `-a`** — and the wording of that ruling was **corrected during implementation** (operator-directed, 2026-08-15): the original justification ("the next deploy's expensive layer (pip) is still a cache hit") was never true, because `make build` passes `docker compose build --no-cache` by design and no build ever reads the BuildKit cache. Verified by two consecutive bare `make build` runs with no prune between them — pip rebuilt both times while the cache grew. The cap is therefore kept as a conservative, free bound, not as a cache-preservation measure; **the `-a` prohibition that is genuinely load-bearing is on the *image* prune**, which stays dangling-only so tagged base images are never deleted and cold-re-pulled (the 2026-07-29 slowdown). **The release shipped a per-target flag divergence** (`--keep-storage` on prod, `--reserved-space` on dev) **on a false premise** — *corrected by ops, 2026-08-15, #257:* the claimed by-daemon mechanism does not exist, because prune flags are parsed **client-side** by the local CLI and `DOCKER_HOST` redirects only the API call; the tail's first prod run proved it (the CLI accepted `--keep-storage` as a hidden deprecated alias and swept 319.6MB of images / 0B of cache — the 0B correct, prod's cache sitting under the reserve). The ops correction removed the deprecated spelling and the prod cap entirely (prod's dedicated daemon holds only never-read cache — `--no-cache` builds) and hardened the `-` error-ignore prefixes into loud-but-non-fatal `|| echo WARNING` forms; §2.2 below describes the corrected sweep. On `deploy-prod` the sweep runs **last, after the posture restore**: both prunes pin their own `DOCKER_HOST` and neither reads `.env`, so not even a Ctrl-C during a long prune can strand production posture. Dev results: build cache 26.86GB → 5.85GB, **21.55GB reclaimed** on the first swept deploy, ~0.5–1GB per deploy at steady state, reclaimable held at 5.27GB against the 5GB (5 GiB) reserve, base image resolved with zero layer downloads on every subsequent build, `.env == .env.dev` after every run. **#255 rides this release** (ruled onto it rather than a separate ops session, since §2.2 rewrites the same table a split would have conflicted on): §2.2's target table documented a nonexistent `make reset` and omitted the entire deployment and guard surface — it is rewritten target-for-target against the Makefile, `reset` deleted (not renamed — `nuke` is materially different and gets its own row), the four posture-setting deployment targets and both check-only guards documented, and the sweep itself described below the table. No game code, no model change, no migration, no seed data, no GDD change (nothing player-facing changes, so this release produces no "(pending implementation)" markers). Suite **687**, unchanged, no test edited beyond the version pin. Stamp 24.31; the header hash moves to 45b9749 — infrastructure is architecture here, the deployment procedure itself changed (contrast v24.24, presentation-only, whose hash did not move). Updated in place per the point-release document rule.**

> **Version 24.30 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_30`: uniform config setters (#251 founding ticket, no dependencies): the four settings commands shared one helper but not one setter shape — `_set_brief_mode`, `_set_show_timestamps` and `_set_echo_mode` wrote only the DB row and left the cached `self.character` attribute to the calling `cmd_*`, so the cache was correct only by the caller's cooperation and **any direct call to a setter left it stale**. The ruling (operator, 2026-08-15): every config setter writes both, `_set_plunder_mode`'s v24.29 shape generalized. The consequence the design session ruled with it: the setter becomes the **single writer** of the cache, so the now-redundant caller-side assignments are **removed** rather than left in place — keeping them would preserve the caller-maintains-the-cache pattern the ruling exists to kill. `cmd_brief` and `cmd_plunder` lose their `if value is not None:` blocks entirely (the assignment was the whole body) and their discarded return values become bare awaits, matching `cmd_timestamps`' existing idiom; `cmd_echo` keeps its block for the fresh fetch and status payload inside it. **`cmd_timestamps` is unedited and is the point of the change:** its set path deliberately bypasses `_cmd_setting` (the status payload must precede the confirmation line so the confirmation renders under the new preference) and calls the setter directly — the third instance of the fragility, correct before this release only because the subsequent `get_character_fresh()` replaced `self.character` wholesale, and now correct by construction. **Zero player-visible behavior change, and no live staleness bug was fixed** — every gameplay read of the four settings was already fresh (`send_room_description` fresh-fetches before `brief_mode` is read, `_status_payload` reads its callers' fresh fetch, `loot_utils.get_plunder` re-reads by pk at combat end, the connect-time payload reads the connect-time object); what the release removes is a *latent class* of bug, exactly as #251 states. No model change, no migration, no seed data, no shared surface. Suite 683 → 687 (`tests/test_v24_30_brief1.py`: each setter called directly writes both row and cache — the issue's exact complaint pinned; each command leaves row and cache in step, `timestamps` pinning the direct-call path; and the bare/invalid paths still answer the v22 settings standard after the two conditionals were removed); no existing test required changing. Stamp 24.30; the header hash moves to 53c6da8 (runtime code change). Updated in place per the point-release document rule; GDD lockstep completes at the point-release closeout (no GDD change — the v22 settings standard is untouched).**

> **Version 24.29 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_29`: three things sharing a release and no code. **`plunder [on|off]` (#235 founding ticket)** — a settings command, default off, that runs the rights-scoped corpse sweep automatically at the moment combat ends. `Character.plunder_mode` (migration `0049`, one `AddField`) joins the settings family; the command goes through the shared `_cmd_setting` helper and occupies all five registration sites, deliberately staying out of `COMBAT_BLOCKED` so a mid-fight flip governs that same fight. **The load-bearing change is structural:** the sweep moved out of `SkylandConsumer._loot_sweep` into a new **`loot_utils.py`**, where `sweep_corpses` is synchronous, performs the mutations and **returns** the lines instead of sending them — the tick engine is a different process with no consumer, so a sweep that sends could not be shared. The consumer became a transport over it and its ORM wrappers now delegate, leaving one implementation of each; a move, not a redesign, with the existing loot suite passing unedited as the regression bar. **The trigger is anchored, not invented:** plunder fires wherever `Combat has ended.` is delivered and nowhere else — three sites in `run_tick_engine.py`, all on the same no-living-NPCs condition. Flee and death are excluded because the anchor excludes them, and deliberately carry no guard of their own; both exclusions are pinned by test. **The silence contract is ruled and tested:** plunder emits nothing whatsoever when it has nothing to take, and `cmd_loot`'s two refusals are never spoken on its behalf. No connected player is required. **The read-only verification family (#249 Part 2, riding this release per the ruling that a base class gets no dedicated release)** — `verification.py`'s `VerificationCommand`: a forced-rollback atomic block so no verification can write, exit code as the outcome signal (0 clean, nonzero findings), and report-never-repair; plus **`verify_ladder`**, the tier-material survey V24.28 Brief 1 specified and had no sanctioned path to run, which is what filed #248. Dev result: 0 mismatches out of 8 ladder rows. #249 stays open for its Part 3. **#250** — `_set_echo_mode` assigned `show_timestamps`; the stray line is deleted rather than corrected, since `cmd_echo` already maintains the cached attribute. Suite 683.**
>
> **Version 24.28 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_28`: the tier-material ladder (#211 founding ticket, #245 dependency): the ladder is **eight rungs, 96 definitions** — copper 1, silver 2, gold 3, platinum 4, rhodium 5, iridium 6, osmium 7, sphaerium 8 — twelve accessories each (six stats × ring/amulet). Rung membership becomes a data fact rather than a naming convention: `ItemDefinition.tier_material_mk_min` / `tier_material_mk_max` (migration `0048`, two nullable `AddField`s plus an `AlterField` for the corrected `suppress_mk_suffix` help text; existing rows take NULL, correct by construction since only the seed authors membership). **Sphaerium is the terminal, unbounded rung** (#245): `tier_material_mk_max` is NULL — the rung covers Mk 8 upward without limit — and it is therefore the one rung that does **not** suppress its Mk suffix, because a rung with no ceiling cannot say its tier by material name alone (a Mk 8 and a Mk 47 piece would otherwise render identically, the exact defect the release exists to remove). A sphaerium piece reads `Sphaerium Ring of Strength Mk 15`. **The guard:** `generate_item_instance` gains a range check as the first statement in its body — a ladder definition can no longer be instantiated outside its rung, the `ValueError` naming the slug, the bound (`Mk 3` bounded, `Mk 8+` unbounded) and the offending tier. Both live generation paths funnel through this function — the loot-drop roll and `do_buy`'s vendor path — so one guard binds them both plus every shell and admin caller; a **deliberate residual gap** remains open by ruling (direct ORM construction is unguarded — a save-time check would make any pre-existing mismatched instance unsavable, turning a naming defect into an unrepairable row). **`suppress_mk_suffix` is un-overloaded**: it is a display flag and *not* ladder membership, independent in both directions — the v19 freebie kit suppresses off the ladder, sphaerium joins the ladder without suppressing. **One curve for the whole ladder** (#211 ruling): every rung carries stat authorship byte-identical to its copper counterpart, and the engine's `midpoint = base + factor × mk_tier` does all the tier progression — 2.8 at copper's Mk 1, 4.9 at silver's Mk 2, 15.4 at osmium's Mk 7, 32.2 at sphaerium Mk 15. Per-rung midpoints were considered and rejected; so was pricing the rungs against the currency ladder — `base_value` is 30 at every rung and valuation's per-tier multiply does the rest. Seed: four module-level ladder tables plus `ladder_accessories()` generating the 84 new definitions from `LADDER_MATERIALS × LADDER_STATS` (so the one-curve invariant holds by construction), authored per-material prose (fourteen new openers, each material's own stat clauses), the copper twelve gaining their range, the `base_value` loop extended over all eight rungs, and five new verification assertions (rung counts, range shape, suffix shape, one-curve equality, loot/vendor entries inside their rung). **Rungs 2–8 ship with zero drop-table and zero vendor entries** — Z02 through Z08 do not exist yet; seeding the ladder complete now is exactly what the V24 new-zone-prep major is for, at near-zero marginal cost. Reconciliation on dev: ItemDefinition **84 created, 12 updated, 0 deleted**. Read-only mismatch survey on dev: **0 rows** outside their rung. Suite 653 in-container (15 new tests in `test_v24_28_brief1.py`: schema pin, the guard raising/passing/inert/across bounded rungs/at the unbounded rung, ladder completeness, range and suffix shape, one-curve equality, tier progression, display, suppression-is-not-membership, and zero drop/vendor exposure above copper); no existing test required changing. No shared-surface changes, no command-layer changes, no loot, vendor, combat, valuation or display code touched. Stamp 24.28; the commit hash moves (architectural: new model fields + new generation guard). Updated in place per the point-release document rule; GDD lockstep completes at the point-release closeout.**

> **Version 24.27 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_27`: character hard delete (#234 founding ticket, no dependencies): hard delete only, the Django admin console is the only deletion surface, and the entire inventory dies with the character. `ItemInstance.owner` `on_delete` `SET_NULL` → **`CASCADE`** (migration `0046`, an `AlterField` with every other attribute — `null=True, blank=True, related_name='inventory'` — unchanged; owner stays legitimately NULL for room items and corpse contents, so world drops and corpse property are unaffected by construction): a character delete now removes every held and equipped instance, bound and unbound, at the Django-collector level. The old `SET_NULL` orphaned the whole inventory as rows with owner, current_room, and corpse all NULL — outside the exactly-one-location invariant (the deletion collector bulk-updates around `save()`, so the #137 check never fired) and unreachable by any game path; pre-existing orphans are swept one-time by data migration `0047` (unconditional deletion of the all-NULL-location shape, always correct by the invariant's definition — the #137 logic; prints the deleted count, deliberate no-op reverse): **1 row deleted on dev**; expected 0 on prod (no character has ever been deleted there). `soulbound_to` stays `SET_NULL` by ruling — bound items can never leave inventory, so every row it references dies in the same owner cascade. Survivors: room drops and corpse contents (owner already NULL); corpses the character killed (`killed_by=NULL` — lootable-by-nobody, natural decay timer, `npc_name_snapshot` intact); the auth `User` (the cascade runs User→Character only); the freed name (case-insensitive constraint). Consumer: a deleted-while-connected character's next command raises `Character.DoesNotExist` out of `_dispatch`, now caught in `receive_json` ahead of the #20 catch-all guard and answered with the connect-time no-character routing verbatim — `'No character found. Create one to play.'` (error), the structured `redirect` envelope to `shyland:create_character`, socket close. Tick engine: **no code change** — the brief's zero-character session close was verified already implemented (the combat-v1 round-boundary participants guard: an empty character list closes the session through `close_session` — `is_active=False` plus `release_session_npcs` per #25 — within one combat round) and is now test-pinned; recorded as a deviation in the closeout report, the brief's premise having expected a gap. The stock admin delete flow is the designed surface — the confirmation page's cascade summary truthfully enumerating everything that dies is the point; no new UI, no commands, no seed changes, no shared-surface changes. Suite 638 in-container (8 new tests in `test_v24_27_brief1.py`: schema pin, cascade correctness, no-orphan shape, survivors, name reuse, 0047 predicate, zero-character session close, consumer guard). Stamp 24.27; the commit hash moves (architectural: schema change + consumer behavior). Updated in place per the point-release document rule; GDD lockstep completes at the point-release closeout.**

> **Version 24.26 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_26`: shard relay & attunement (#38 founding ticket, #30 dependency): one home concept, player-set. `Character.attuned_node` (nullable FK → `TravelNode`, `SET_NULL`, `related_name='attuned_characters'`; migration `0045`) replaces the removed `recall_room` — null means the founding node (the Heart of the Convergence, pinned by seed-verify as `travel_name='The Convergence', node_type='obelisk'`), so existing characters are unchanged by construction (no data migration). The **effective home** resolves through the shared sync helpers `resolve_home_node(character)` / `resolve_home_room(character)` in `models.py` — attuned node when bonded, founding node otherwise — consumed identically by the consumer (`get_home_room`, replacing `get_heart_room`) and the tick engine's death path. **`attune`** is a bare verb (chart fn 2): no node here → warn from `ATTUNE_NO_NODE_LINES`; the room's node equals the effective home (null compares as the Heart) → warn from `ATTUNE_ALREADY_LINES`; otherwise an atomic `.update(attuned_node=node)` and a pooled ceremony line ending in the stable exact parenthetical `(Home: {travel_name})` (success voice). No combat gate — structurally safe (every attunable room is a safe room); the central deny-by-default dying gate refuses it. **The relay (#30):** the sender gate's checkpoint refusal is deleted; `get_revealed_destinations` gains `spheres_only` (a `node_type='obelisk'` filter on the standing `RoomVisit` membership check) and `cmd_travel`/`_complete_travel` pass `spheres_only=(node.node_type != 'obelisk')` — a shard lists, completes, and sends to revealed spheres only, never to another shard; the no-node refusal and the listing opener/empty-pool lines gained shard-voice wording; listing format, `TravelMessage` pools, and the zone-lock gate are untouched. **`home` delivers to the bond**, resolved at countdown *completion* (a bond changed mid-fog lands at the new home); the already-home kindly refusal compares against the effective home room. **Death respawn follows the bond** (full bars, visit recorded consumer-side on the respawn event, unchanged message shape — the `'your recall point'` fallback string became `'your home'`). The stats sheet gains `Home: {travel_name}` directly under the Player line. Admin swaps `recall_room` → `attuned_node`; character creation and `seed_world` drop their recall backfills (expected seed deletions: 0); `recall_room` survives only in migration history. Suite 630 in-container (27 new tests in `test_v24_26_brief1.py`; the `test_room_visits.py` respawn test converted to the attuned-node shape with intent preserved; factory `recall_room` kwargs dropped across six test files). Stamp 24.26; the commit hash moves (architectural: Character schema change, new command, changed travel topology). Updated in place per the point-release document rule; GDD lockstep completes at the point-release closeout.**

> **Version 24.25 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_25`: zone locks & Convergence areas (#41 founding ticket, #95 dependency): locks are world data, keys are player data, and nothing derives from `danger_level`. `Zone.entry_requires_zone` (nullable self-FK, `SET_NULL`, `related_name='unlocked_by'`; migration `0043`) authors a zone's entry requirement; `ZoneCompletion` (`character`/`zone` FKs, `completed_at`, unique `(character, zone)`) is the permanent key — never revoked by any path, recorded for every zone whether or not a lock requires it. Keys mint inside `record_room_visit`, the fog-of-war choke point every arrival path already calls: on a first visit, the character's distinct visit count for the room's zone is compared to the zone's room count; equality mints the completion via `get_or_create`, and the arrival path announces one line from `ZONE_COMPLETE_LINES` (reward voice, after the room render, celebrating the completed zone by name and never naming what it unlocked) only when the row was newly created — pre-existing (grandfathered) rows suppress the announcement, and revisits cost nothing. The helper's return becomes `(first_visit, zone_completed)` across all six arrival paths (connect, move, travel, flee, home, respawn). Enforcement is one shared sync helper, `check_zone_lock(character, destination, current_zone_id)`, called from `cmd_move` (after exit resolution, before any state change or messaging) and `cmd_travel` (after match resolution, before departure messaging): intra-zone movement always passes — no character is ever ejected from a zone they stand in; a set lock with no key returns a refusal (warn voice) from the generic, door-agnostic `ZONE_LOCK_REFUSAL_LINES` — naming the required zone and the one Area of it holding the most rooms the character has not visited (ties broken by name; `ZONE_LOCK_REFUSAL_LINES_NO_AREA` when no unvisited room carries an Area), never counts. The travel listing keeps locked destinations listed and matchable (revelation and tab completion untouched): a locked zone's destination rows render through the existing `muted` seg voice (`get_completed_zone_ids` supplies the key ring) while the zone heading keeps its theme color — no new color literal; the chart set-equality test stays green. Migration `0044` grandfathers every character existing at deploy time: the Convergence key unconditionally plus honestly computed completions for every zone their `RoomVisit` records already cover; the reverse migration is a deliberate no-op (keys survive rollback); no-ops gracefully on an empty database. Seed: two new Convergence areas — The Everround (`the-everround`, `#C9AE7A`, the 40 ring rooms) and Morra's Smithy (`morras-smithy`, `#C0855C`, both smithy rooms) — the Heart stays deliberately area-free; the Verdant Reach's `entry_requires_zone` is authored to The Convergence (the only lock in the world); verification asserts the membership table (Everround 40, Smithy 2, Wisteria 4, Bamboo 4, Basalt 5, Fern 4, area-free 1, total 60), both theme colors, and lock exclusivity. WCAG contrast vs the output-pane background `#0D0D0F`: `#C9AE7A` 9.08:1, `#C0855C` 6.24:1 — both above 4.5:1. Suite 603 in-container (14 new tests in `test_zone_locks.py`; `test_room_visits.py` adjusted to the tuple return). Stamp 24.25; the commit hash moves (architectural: new model, new gate machinery). Updated in place per the point-release document rule; GDD lockstep completes at the point-release closeout.**

Type scale (#222): each template keeps exactly **one** px type declaration — the root `html, body`, raised 14px → **16px**, the single future tuning knob — and every other type site converts to `rem` at the exact former ratios (value = former px ÷ 14, four decimals): `game.html` `#loc-bar`/`#prompt` 15px → 1.0714rem, `#cmd` 14px → 1rem, `#side`/`#stats-name`/`#bars`/`#send-btn` 12px → 0.8571rem, `.fight-nums`/`#conn-ms` 11px → 0.7857rem; `character_create.html` `h1` 18px → 1.2857rem, `select`/`input[type="text"]`/`button[type="submit"]` 14px → 1rem. Deliberately excluded: `.map-updown` stays **byte-identical** (`font:` shorthand at bold 14px Courier — the U/D badge offsets are derived from that ink per the tuck rule below, and px is naturally immune to the root change), the right pane stays fixed at 300px, and the map SVG (fixed 300×300, all internal geometry) is untouched — the map deliberately does not scale. Contrast (#221): the creation screen's `--muted` moves `#6b6b80` → **`#85859c`** — 5.01:1 on the `#16161a` surface, above the WCAG AA 4.5:1 minimum (the old value computed 3.47:1); variable-level only, every usage site (subtitle, helper text, `.desc`, back link) inherits, no other palette value changes, and the creation screen's palette remains self-contained — zero color-doctrine impact, the game client's colors unchanged. Operator playtest on dev approved the starting values as final (zero tuning iterations). Verification: one px `font-size` per template, all else rem; `.map-updown` diff-free; suite 589 in-container (count unchanged from the 24.23 stamp). Stamp 24.24; the commit hash does not move (presentation-only change, not architectural). Updated in place per the point-release document rule; GDD lockstep completes at the point-release closeout.**

> **Version 24.23 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_23`: percentage bags (#215 founding ticket, no dependencies): the equipped-bag contribution to carry capacity changes from the flat additive `carry_bonus` to a percentage of the STR-derived base — `capacity = floor(effective_STR × 10 × (100 + Σ bag_pct) / 100)`, integer math (`* ... // 100`), where each equipped bag contributes `carry_pct_base + carry_pct_per_mk × mk_tier` percentage points at the **instance's** Mk tier — deterministic, no rarity roll, bags still roll no stats — and percentages from multiple equipped bags **sum into one multiplier, never compound** (two 20% bags → ×1.40, not ×1.44; pinned for the future hip slot — only BACK ships today). Model: `ItemDefinition.carry_bonus` → `carry_pct_base` (`RenameField`, migration `0042`) plus new `carry_pct_per_mk` (`IntegerField(default=0)`); the admin Carry fieldset follows. One helper — `item_utils.carry_capacity(character, equipped_items)` — answers every site; the four former inline sums (`cmd_inventory`, `_unequip_blocked_reason` — called with the reduced equipped list, the guard's "capacity without this bag" question — `get_carry_capacity`, `get_carry_counts`) all call it, no site keeps a private copy; `bag_pct(definition, mk_tier)` feeds the displays — the bag info suffix and examine's Carry bonus row show `+{pct}%` at the instance's Mk (unidentified bags still show nothing, #80). Seed: satchel `carry_pct_base=10`/`carry_pct_per_mk=5` (Mk 1 = 15%, Mk 2 = 20%), patchwork-satchel `5`/`3` (8%/11%); reconcile-in-place, 0 rows deleted. Unchanged mechanics: the cannot-unequip-a-bag-over-limit guard's behavior, BACK slot, bags never stack, the flat-pool inventory, no off-body storage. Reference case pinned in tests and verified live: STR 47 + Mk 2 Satchel = 470 × 120 // 100 = 564 (+94 versus +20 flat). Tests: new `tests/test_v24_23_brief1.py` (floor, Mk ladder, sum-never-compound, reference case, unequip guard); the flat-bonus pins in `tests/test_v24_16_brief1.py` and `tests/test_v24_11_brief1.py` converted to the percentage form with intent preserved (`test_gear_combat.py`'s bagless carry test needed no change — bagless capacity is invariant under the formula); suite 589 in-container. Stamp 24.23; the header hash moved to fb60a59 (model + runtime code changes). Updated in place per the point-release document rule; GDD lockstep completes at the point-release closeout.**
