> **Version 24.19 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_19`: zombie combat session reaping (#218 founding ticket, no dependencies — orphan-session hygiene only; #220's multiplayer combat model explicitly out of scope): `CombatSession.npcs` is M2M — one `NpcInstance` can belong to multiple players' sessions — but the kill path removed a dead NPC from the killer's session only, so every other session holding it kept the dead row, could never fire its own last-kill deactivation, and its members sat stuck behind the in-combat gate (loot refused) while the stale sweep couldn't reap the zombie because `update_session_tick` refreshed `last_tick_at` unconditionally before any liveness check (root cause confirmed by deliberate live reproduction on prod, 2026-08-08). Two-part fix, both in `run_tick_engine.py`: **(A)** the kill path goes M2M-wide — the dead NPC is removed from every active session holding it; each other session left with zero living NPCs closes on the standard end pattern within the same engine round (`is_active=False`, `focus_npc=None`, `release_session_npcs`, "Combat has ended." at `reward`, fight-clear payload, fresh status), and a session that retains living NPCs but was focused on the dead one reassigns focus to its next living NPC (canonical `(spawned_at, pk)` order) with the standard "You turn your attacks on {name}." announcement, ordinal-composed against that session's own remaining live NPCs; the killer's session keeps its existing flow untouched. **(B)** the loop-head self-heal — before `update_session_tick`, the loop re-reads the session's current DB state (never the prefetched cache, stale for sessions Part A closed earlier in the same tick): no longer active → silent skip; active with zero living NPCs (dead rows attached or none) → immediate close on the same pattern, and `update_session_tick` never runs for it, so a zombie never refreshes its own `last_tick_at` and the stale sweep is restored as backstop by construction — the stuck window for any leak path, present or future, caps at one engine tick. `release_session_npcs` unchanged by ruling: its `is_alive=True` filter and other-active-session guard mean dead NPCs are never restored and a living shared NPC never snaps to full — both pinned. Test-pinned in `tests/test_zombie_sessions.py` (engine-harness style: cross-session kill closes the bystander same-round with the loot gate's own predicate re-checked, cross-session focus reassignment, self-heal with a dead row attached asserting `last_tick_at` unchanged, self-heal with zero rows, dead-stays-dead through both close paths, damaged shared living NPC keeps its vitality; suite 566 → 572, zero existing-test conversions). Runtime code only — `run_tick_engine.py`; no models, no migration, no seed data, no data actions. Stamp 24.19; the header hash moved to 4f30bdf (architectural point release — tick-engine session-lifecycle change). Updated in place per the point-release document rule; GDD lockstep completes at the point-release closeout (no GDD content change — mechanical defect, GDD §5 already describes intended behavior).**
>
> **Version 24.18 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_18`: in-combat acuity drift pause (#142 founding ticket, no dependencies — finishes the acuity design's last unruled corner): tick Phase 2 (passive acuity drift, `get_characters_needing_drift` in `process_effects`) drifted every off-baseline character toward baseline at `ACUITY_DRIFT_RATE` (0.01) per tick regardless of combat state; the ruling — nothing passively recovers in combat, now uniform across all three bars — adds the in-combat exclusion to the candidate queryset: `.exclude(combat_sessions__is_active=True)`, exactly Phase 4 regen's combat-membership predicate (active `CombatSession` membership) expressed at queryset level and shared by construction, so a future membership-semantics change (#218) moves both phases together. Shift-active (#133 — a running shift owns the value) and in-combat are independent pause conditions: the `acuity_shift_types` exclusion stands verbatim, and a character with both is paused under each. Post-combat resume is ordinary — when the character's last active session ends, drift resumes at 0.01 per tick, no burst correction, no refill, no catch-up; the snap-within-rate rule, the 2-decimal rounding, the `ACUITY_FLOOR`/`ACUITY_CEILING` clamp, and drift silence (no output message) are all untouched. Test-pinned in `tests/test_acuity_drift_pause.py` (engine-harness style: drift law both directions, snap rule, in-combat pause, ordinary resume, shift independence with and without combat; suite 559 → 566). Runtime code only — `run_tick_engine.py`; no models, no migration, no seed data, no data actions. Stamp 24.18; the header hash moved to c3c21c8 (architectural point release — tick-engine runtime change). > **Version 24.17 (point release) — Brief 1 applied on branch `version_24_17`: DEX curve rounding parity (#105 founding ticket, no dependencies — Phase 3, Mk 2 balance, of the V24 series plan #139): `get_npc_stats` derived NPC DEX — the difficulty dial for all opposed rolls — via `round(NPC_CONTEST_BASE + NPC_CONTEST_STEP × (L − 1))`, and Python's built-in `round()` is banker's rounding: the `.5`-boundary levels that round UP (L4/L8 within every band — Mk-band lifts add an integer 25, so the pattern repeats identically per band) overstated NPC DEX by 1 pip against the calibration's reference player, who accrues the floor-share of 2.5 DEX per level (5 points split across two primaries — attainable primary `18 + floor(2.5 × (L − 1))`); 1 DEX = one d20 pip = 5% hit, so the blessed-targets-exact claim was overstated by −5% at exactly those levels (survey finding G5, #89). Now `curve = NPC_CONTEST_BASE + math.floor(NPC_CONTEST_STEP × (L − 1))` — the floor applied to the growth term, mirroring the player's own floor-share accrual, so NPC DEX equals the reference build's attainable at-level primary at every level of every band and the blessed at-level hit targets (55% normal / 45% elite / 45% boss) are exact everywhere. Sentinels: elite `scaling_factor` 4.0 Mk 1 DEX 28 → 27, 8.0 Mk 1 38 → 37; aligned levels byte-identical (odd levels and the `.5`-down evens — normal L2 = 20, L6 = 30, L10 = 40). The STR/PER/INT `growth = round(NPC_CONTEST_STEP × (L − 1))` line is deliberately untouched — its banker's ±1 artifacts were ratified by v19 Brief 7 Amendment 1 (damage-side noise, no hit% effect); `npc_max_vitality`'s half-up rounding (#104) and all four contest constants untouched. Test-pinned in `tests/test_dex_parity.py` (the curve law across Mk 1–3 × within-band 1–10 × all three tiers, player parity at effective levels 1–30, blessed-targets-exact thresholds 10/12/12, both sentinels, aligned-level pins; suite 553 → 559). Runtime code only — `combat_utils.py`; no models, no migration, no seed data, no data actions. Stamp 24.17; the header hash moved to d83fcdf (architectural point release — contest-math change). Updated in place per the point-release document rule; GDD lockstep completes at the point-release closeout.**

The equipped-items query stays alive: capacity is unchanged — effective STR (base + gear, #100) × 10 + equipped-bag `carry_bonus` — the equipped set keeps informing the numbers without being displayed. ---

## 1. Overview

Shyland is a free, browser-based Multi-User Dungeon. The primary interface is text: players connect via WebSocket, type commands, and read descriptive output. A minimal visual chrome (status bar, side panel) supplements the text pane. As of this commit, v16 adds the in-game character creator: a player with no `Character` who visits `/shyland/play/` is redirected to a creation form where they choose an Origin, an Archetype, and a Name, then spawn into The Convergence. Supporting changes: `Character.name` is now a real database field (previously a read-only property over the gamer tag), `Origin` and `Archetype` gained attire flavor-text fields and real seeded descriptions, and the `better-profanity` dependency was added for name filtering. v17 adds the Infinity City world seed — a data-only change (no models, no migrations) that replaces the 5-room placeholder starter zone in `seed_world.py` with the full first-version map of The Convergence: 4 park-path areas (Wisteria Walk, Bamboo Run, Basalt Way, Fern Boards), 54 rooms (obelisk hub, four park paths, a 35-room ring street, and Morra's Smithy), and 9 non-combat NPC definitions placed via `RoomSpawn`. v18 (in progress across multiple briefs) begins The Verdant Reach series. Brief 1 ships the Mk 1 item kit: 23 fantasy `ItemDefinition` seed rows (22 net-new plus the legacy Copper Ring absorbed as Copper Ring of Wisdom) covering a leather armor set, a wooden shield, four weapons, and twelve copper accessories; a `suppress_mk_suffix` display field on `ItemDefinition` (migration `0018`); a central `get_display_name_with_tier()` helper in `item_utils.py`; and a rewritten `equip` command implementing a general exchange rule (one-for-one auto-swap, refusals on multi-item or ambiguous displacement). Brief 1 contains no zone content — Verdant Reach rooms, NPCs, and drop tables come in later v18 briefs. Brief 2 ships the Obelisk Network, Shyland's fast-travel system (GDD 2.11): the superseded `ZoneGate` model is removed and replaced by `TravelNode` (one node per room, obelisk or checkpoint type) and `TravelMessage` (three seeded flavor pools), the `travel` command is implemented in the consumer (list + go forms, revelation derived from `RoomVisit`), the Primordial Sphere NPC is placed at the Heart of the Convergence, and the Heart is registered as the network's first node ("The Convergence", obelisk). Brief 2 also contains no Verdant Reach content. Brief 3 ships four zone-agnostic engine mechanics for battle-zone encounter design (migration `0020`, one `AddField` per model): boss-gated spawns (`RoomSpawn.requires_living_npc` + a tick-engine gate in `process_npc_respawn`), guaranteed-group loot (`LootTableEntry.guaranteed_group` + a rewritten `generate_loot_from_table`), per-NPC death messages (`NpcDefinition.death_message`, broadcast once to the room at death), and outleveled XP reduction (`xp_for_kill` pays −20% per level over the NPC's Mk band, floored at 10% of base and never below 1 XP). Brief 3 contains no zone content either — every mechanic is data-driven; Verdant Reach rooms, NPCs, spawns, and loot tables come in later briefs. Brief 4 ships the commerce loop and one combat quality-of-life change (migration `0021`): item valuation (`ItemDefinition.base_value` plus four helpers in `item_utils.py` — value, sale price, repair cost, repair success chance), the `material` item type with two seeded materials (Animal Hide, Insect Carapace) and a `base_value` back-fill across all definitions, the `list`/`buy`/`sell` commands routed to a living vendor in the room (`VendorEntry.sold_count` tracks finite-stock exhaustion), the `repair` command routed to a living repairer (`NpcDefinition.is_repairer`), and targetless `attack`/`kill` that auto-targets the earliest-engaged living NPC while the player has aggro. Brief 4 also ships no zone content — vendor and repairer NPCs arrive with the Verdant Reach world seed. Brief 5 is the first content payload of the series: the partial world seed for The Verdant Reach (Z01, levels 1–10) — the zone row and 6 areas, 69 rooms (the Tree Arch entrance, Fernwater Vale with the villages Reedmere, the ancient stair, The Sagewind Flats with Windhome, and four caves: Spinner's Hollow, the Silken Cleft, the Whistling Sink, the Drone Pit), the opened Verdant gate on the ring street, six species unarmed-message pools, 29 NPC definitions (surface creatures, villagers, the first vendors Essa/Sona and repairers Maro/Tavik, the Verdant Shard, cave insects, three bosses with death messages, three boss-gated minion definitions), 7 loot tables (three with guaranteed groups for boss drops), 57 room spawns, vendor stock, and the network's first checkpoint nodes (Fordwatch, Stairhead). Brief 5 also fixes a tick-engine respawn bug: dead instances now hold their spawn slot until their timer clears them, so `respawn_minutes` and boss-minion gating actually take effect. Brief 6, the final brief of the series, completes the zone: The Viridian Ridge (51 rooms — the switchback climb from Cragfoot through the villages Stonestep, Highfold, and Lastlight, four warned-about aggro offshoots, and the summit garden of the Verdant Crown), the three mountain delves (The Undercrag 9 rooms, Chitterdeep 10, Hollowcrown 11), vr-f18's north exit wired at last, 20 more NPC definitions (including the three delve bosses — the Undercrag Weaver, the Chittering King, and the Crowned Devourer — with death messages and boss-gated minions, plus the unique Verdant Sphere at the summit), three ridge unarmed pools, 5 loot tables (the Devourer's guaranteeing an Epic accessory), 72 spawns, Ridda's vendor stock and Old Brammel's repair bench at Cragfoot, and two travel nodes — Cragfoot (checkpoint) and The Verdant Crown, the Obelisk Network's second source node. Z01 stands complete at 150 rooms and 10 areas. v19 (in progress across multiple briefs) is a fix-and-feature pass from v18 play-testing; Brief 1 ships the presence-ownership fix, the wallet display (`wallet` command + `inventory` section), and the buy/sell vendor-check reorder. Brief 2 closes the client state synchronization gap found by v18 play-testing: `send_room_description` now fresh-fetches the character before building its status payload (fixing the jump-and-snap-back bar bug on `look`/`move`/`travel`/login), a level-up that ends a kill now pushes the refilled Vitality/Longevity bars to the client, and death respawn synchronizes the consumer's room-group membership, `last_direction`, and dying-command gate with the tick engine's server-side state change instead of leaving the client stranded in the old room. Brief 3 fixes combat narration found by v18 play-testing: NPC unarmed attacks no longer speak in the player's second-person voice (`get_unarmed_message` gained an `attacker_name` substitution and a `fallback_slug` parameter — NPCs fall back to a new `npc-default` pool, never the player-perspective `default` pool; eight Verdant surface animals gained third-person species pools), `cmd_attack` now distinguishes a genuine new engagement (`You move to attack …!`) from a no-op repeat against an already-engaged target (`You're already fighting …`), same-name NPCs in a room are disambiguated with positional ordinals (`npc_display_name`), and the tick engine's `broadcast_to_room` gained an `exclude_pk` parameter so a character's own kill and fall announcements no longer duplicate the personal line they already received. Brief 4 ships the complete death-and-dying presentation: falling now clears the fallen player's output pane (a new engine/consumer-initiated `clear` event, implemented client-side as an ARIA-safe content wipe of the existing live region — never a rebuild) and replaces it with a red fatal-blow line; every active effect on the faller is canceled (`removed_by='dying'`) and their own queued/future attacks are discarded from the moment of falling, so a character who drops mid-round can no longer land a posthumous kill and no combat output of any kind (hit lines, kill lines, "Combat has ended.") reaches a dying player; a lore-only escalation ladder (no numerals, no time units) plays out over the 30-second window from an in-memory per-tick-engine scheduler; `use`-ing a vitality-restoring item while dying revives the character with exactly the healed amount (no bonus, no forced full heal) and resumes combat naturally; and expiry now announces `"The darkness takes you."` before the existing death/respawn flow. Brief 4 Amendment 1 removes a room-title line from between the `clear` event and the fatal-blow line, found redundant with the client's persistent room-name chrome during brief 4 play-testing. Brief 5 re-baselines combat feel: `resolve_hit`'s v18 d100-band formula, under which miss and graze were mathematically unreachable for a well-built attacker, is replaced with a contested d20 to-hit plus an independent critical roll, so Z01's authored NPC stats now mean what they say. Brief 6 makes attack focus player-controlled: `CombatSession.focus_npc` (migration `0022`) is the single field driving both the auto-attack target and the Acuity single-target bonus, replacing the old implicit "always `npcs[0]`" behavior; `cmd_attack` gains a three-way outcome (engage steals focus, repeat against the current focus is a no-op, naming an in-session non-focused target refocuses with a personal-only message); the tick engine auto-shifts focus with an announcement when the focused NPC dies and others remain, and clears it when the session ends; and `npc_display_name`/`ORDINALS` move from `consumers.py` to `combat_utils.py` so the engine's shift announcement can reuse the same ordinal logic as the consumer. Brief 7 corrects two combat-math defects found in v19 play-testing. First, NPC contest stats (DEX for to-hit, STR/PER/INT alongside) scaled multiplicatively (`base × scaling_factor × mk_tier`) against additively-growing player stats, so a d20 contest — able to bridge at most ~20 points — went permanently unreachable past the early game; 26 of Z01's 42 combat-relevant NPCs, including every giant, elder, and all six bosses, sat at 0% hit chance for an at-level player. `get_npc_stats` is rewritten so contest stats grow additively on the same curve players climb (`npc_level = scaling_factor + 10 × (mk_tier − 1)`; DEX is derived purely from level + a per-tier offset so normal/elite/boss land at blessed 55%/40%/25% at-level hit chances forever; STR/PER/INT keep authored species bases and grow at the player slope), and the six Z01 bosses' inflated `scaling_factor` values (data migration `0023`) are corrected to their intended level-band placements. Second, the Acuity damage modifier compared `acuity_current` against a hard-coded 1.0 instead of the character's Origin band, and 1-decimal rounding silently zeroed out several Origins' baselines; `acuity_damage_modifier` replaces the inline computation with a band-relative, deviation-based formula (neutral inside the Origin's band, bonus/penalty scaled by distance beyond `acuity_band_high`/`acuity_band_low`, no rounding). Brief 7 Amendment 1 ratifies the STR/PER/INT growth formula (`base + round(NPC_CONTEST_STEP × (level − 1))`, uniform per level) as authoritative after a stop-and-flag surfaced that the brief's own worked table had computed a handful of values by rounding the *sum* rather than the *growth term*, producing inconsistent ±1 artifacts at five levels — the formula stands, table corrected. v20 (in progress across multiple briefs) opens with Brief 1, the map system: `Room.coord_x/y/z` are promoted to the map's positional source of truth (per-zone map-space; z is a plane index, not elevation), four cardinal boundary-flag booleans are added to `Room` (migration `0026`), seed verification permanently enforces the geometry invariant (every unflagged intra-zone cardinal exit lands grid-adjacent at the same z) plus cell uniqueness, flag symmetry, and flag inventory, and the world is re-laid to satisfy it: the Convergence ring street grows to a true 40-room right-angle circuit (five new corner rooms plus BR-4 and two street-cart vendors, issue #43), and the Verdant Reach surface flattens to a single z=0 plane with Stonestep re-hung west of the spine and Bear's Hollow north of Highfold (issue #44) and the checkpoint sphere→shard wording fixes (issues #46, #49 — Fordwatch, Stairhead, Cragfoot, and the shard entity), and Brief 1 Amendment 2 decouples fog-of-war from rendering (issue #50): `RoomVisit` recording moves out of `_resolve_room_rendering` into every arrival path (move, travel, flee, respawn, connect), so aggro-ambush entries that skip the room description no longer skip the visit. On top of the clean geometry, the server derives per-character MapFrags on demand and pushes a new `map` message on connect and every room change (issue #35), and the client renders it as an SVG node-and-line map in a re-geometried fixed-width right pane (issue #36). Brief 3 rebuilds the command layer (issues #22, #3, #21, #19, #20, #23, #45, #48): a single authoritative reference resolver (`command_grammar.py`) replaces every ad-hoc item/NPC matcher and gives all object commands one grammar (`<verb> [all|N] [rarity] [noun]` plus the retained `N.noun` index form) with token-subsequence prefix matching, plural fallbacks, rarity filtering, cross-definition disambiguation refusal, and per-verb selection ordering; `loot all` and `sell all/N` ship on it, and `buy N` becomes an atomic all-or-nothing purchase. The verb dispatch moves to a table wrapped in a catch-all guard (a crashing handler logs its traceback and tells the player one error line instead of killing the socket), directional movement and `travel` refuse during active combat, `Character.show_timestamps` (migration `0027`) backs a `timestamps on|off` preference, rarity leaves composed item names for a trailing `[<Rarity>, Bound|Droppable]` flag block (bind word since renamed to Unbound in v21 brief 1) built by one shared composition helper (with a new On-the-ground room listing), and server-authoritative tab completion arrives as a connect-time verb list plus a `complete` request/reply pair computed from the resolver's own candidate providers. See Section 4.14. v21 opens with Brief 1, an output-and-display sweep (#55, #60, #81, #84, #85, #86, #90, #91, #92 — no model changes, no migrations): the palette gains a named key-color/value-color vocabulary and a structured key/value `report` form adopted by `inv`/`stats`/`wallet`, the item bind flag is renamed Unbound, who's-here/what's-here lines become bare noun phrases, aggro-room arrival renders the full room before the engagement lines in both movement and flee paths, the five pane borders take the zone theme color, area and room prose are colorized to match the location bar (the room-render payload now carries `area_text`/`area_color`/`room_text`), and the player help is rewritten around a single `<item selection>` grammar section. See Sections 4.6, 4.14, 4.15, and 4.16. Brief 2 is the B3 balance retune (#68, #101, #102, #103 — data plus two tier-offset constants; no model changes, no migrations): the NPC tier dodge offsets flatten from 0/+3/+6 to 0/+2/+2 (blessed at-level hit targets 55%/45%/45% — boss difficulty now lives in HP, damage, and escorts, not the miss rate), the six-boss ladder and five heavy elites are retuned per the ruling on #101 (derivation in `Shyland_V21_B3_Retune_Proposal.md` and the #89 kill-feasibility survey), delve escorts drop from 3 to 2 making "boss + 2 escorts" the ladder-wide pattern, the four broken proc-stat curves are raised to the working family's 0.5/0.2, a one-time `fix_zero_secondary_stats` management command re-rolls stored zero-value secondaries, the two ×3 aggro grounds gain authored direction-neutral approach warnings, and the four placeholder roster NPCs (Aldric, Info Prime, Seris, Veris) become unattackable. See Sections 4.5, 4.6, and 4.8. Brief 3 is the B2 combat-state pass (#52, #64, #17 — no model changes, no migrations): consumer-side bar restores become atomic database updates with a standing never-read-modify-write invariant on bar fields (the #52 lost-update race resurrected tick-engine damage on every in-combat heal), `(spawned_at, pk)` becomes the single canonical NPC order across the Who's-here listing, resolver default pick, `N.noun` index, and message ordinals (ordinals render only among same-name duplicates in the encounter), and aggressive NPCs now engage present players on their spawn tick inside the batched respawn path. See Sections 4.4, 4.5, and 4.9. v22 opens with Brief 1 (Maps V2), the map vocabulary and payload redesign (#82, #115; #53 absorbed) — see Section 4.12. v23 Brief 2 (B2 Data Integrity, #137/#117/#18) ships corpse-content CASCADE with the exactly-one location invariant and the one-time `purge_orphaned_items` command (Sections 4.1, 4.6, 4.9), deletes the stub `tests.py` restoring whole-app test discovery, and extends inventory stacking to all wear-free types with soulbound state in the grouping key (Section 4.3). v23 Brief 1 (B1 Flee & Disengagement, #143/#25) puts the flee contest's NPC side on effective stats — `flee_contest_npc_side()`, the session mean of `get_npc_stats()['per']` — and adds the session-end-without-death NPC reset via the `release_session_npcs()` choke point with its last-active-session multiplayer guard (Section 4.5). v23 Brief 3 (B3 Effects & Display, #133/#119/#141) stops acuity shift effects at the drinker's own band edge with change-only announcements and one-time terminal lines, promotes the engine's 0.1/1.9 acuity rails to named constants (Sections 4.5, 4.9), withdraws combat state from pane borders (Section 4.15), and splits the level-up message honestly in two (Section 4.9). v23 Brief 4 (B4 Voice Machinery & Transactions, #138/#40-code-half/#146) opens the worthless-item disposal path (0-copper sales with the zero floor in `get_sale_price`), refuses artifacts generically under the no-leak rule, replaces every single-string flavor site with the `npc_voice.py` pools (Section 4.17), and removes the dead `is_artifact` boolean (migration `0037`). v23 Brief 5 (B4 Voice Content & Coverage, #40-data-half/#144/#147) gives the six silent checkpoint services their voices, lifts the tripled dialogue corpus to the module-level `NPC_DIALOGUE` map on a three-response pool floor (Section 4.8), and ships the speech-vs-narration render rule — keyword responses speak, greetings and departures narrate unprefixed with no connective, composed by `npc_voice.dialogue_line` (Sections 4.9, 4.17). See [Section 7](#7-what-is-not-yet-built) for unbuilt systems.

## 2. Infrastructure

*(unchanged from v15)*

### 2.1 Docker Compose stack

```
docker-compose.yml defines six containers:

  nginx         nginx:alpine          SSL termination; WebSocket proxy at /ws/
  django        python:3.12-slim      Daphne ASGI server; Django 5 + Channels
  postgres      postgres:16-alpine    Primary database; persistent volume pgdata
  redis         redis:7-alpine        Channels layer; Shyland presence keys; MC event stream (hot tier)
  ticker        shyland-django image  Tick engine; run_tick_engine management command
  mc-persister  shyland-django image  MC durable-record persister; run_mc_persister management command
```

**Network flow:**

```
Browser
  ↓ HTTPS / WSS  (port HOST_PORT, default 40443)
nginx
  ↓ HTTP proxy_pass http://django:8000
  ↓ Sets X-Forwarded-Proto: https on all requests
  ↓ WebSocket Upgrade headers forwarded for /ws/ paths
Daphne (Django ASGI)
  ├── HTTP requests → Django WSGI stack
  └── WebSocket /ws/* → Django Channels consumers
        ├── reads from / writes to Redis (channel layer)
        └── reads from / writes to PostgreSQL (ORM)
```

Only `nginx` exposes a host port. `django`, `postgres`, `redis`, `ticker`, and `mc-persister` are internal to the Docker network. `postgres` has a healthcheck; `django`, `ticker`, and `mc-persister` all depend on it being healthy before starting. `ticker` and `mc-persister` use the same `shyland-django` image as `django` — no separate build step (v25.1: `mc-persister` mirrors the `ticker` compose block verbatim with its own `command`).

**Login rate limit (v25.6, #284):** `nginx/conf/default.conf.template`'s `location /accounts/login/` block carries `limit_req zone=login burst=10 nodelay` — burst lifted 3 → 10 at v25.6 for bot login patterns and agent testing; the rate itself stays 5r/m (`rate-limit.conf` untouched) and django-defender is untouched. Shared surface (nginx fronts all three games), operator-acknowledged; it reaches each stack with that stack's ordinary deploy.

### 2.2 Makefile workflow

| Target | What it does |
|--------|-------------|
| `make setup` | First-time: `init` + `check-secrets` + `push-certs` + `build` + `start` |
| `make init` | Setup wizard (`scripts/init.py`) — writes `.env` |
| `make hooks` | Point `core.hooksPath` at `scripts/git-hooks` — one-time per clone; new worktrees then auto-initialize their env files |
| `make gen-certs` | Generate self-signed TLS certs into `ssl/` for local dev (requires `init` first) |
| `make check-secrets` | Validate `.env` and cert files (`scripts/check_secrets.py`); runs automatically before `build` and `start` |
| `make push-certs` | Upload local `ssl/` certs into the Docker `ssldata` volume |
| `make build` | Rebuild Docker images (`docker compose build --no-cache`) and recreate containers — **required after any Python/template/settings change** |
| `make start` / `make stop` / `make restart` | Container lifecycle |
| `make nuke` | Remove containers, volumes and images — `require-local`-gated. Does **not** rebuild, start, migrate or seed |
| `make logs` | Follow all container logs |
| `make tick-logs` | Follow ticker container logs only |
| `make migrate` | Run `python manage.py migrate` inside the container |
| `make makemigrations [APP=name]` | Generate migrations and auto-sync generated files back to local filesystem |
| `make shell` | Django shell inside the container |
| `make createsuperuser` | Create a Django admin superuser |
| `make createuser` | Run the `createuser` management command inside the container |
| `make seed` | Run `seed_world` against the current target |
| `make verify VERIFY=verify_<name>` | Run one brief-shipped `verify_*` command against the current target (#248/#249) — from resting posture this is the dev-side test path |
| `make new-app NAME=x` | Scaffold a new game app in `apps/` |
| `make gdd` | Rebuild the monolithic GDD from the section files in `docs/shyland/gdd/` |
| `make help` | List all targets |

**The deployment targets** — the only four permitted to write `.env`, because invoking one *is* the deliberate act of setting the posture:

| Target | What it does |
|--------|-------------|
| `make deploy-dev` | Deploy current source to the local dev stack: build + migrate + build-exhaust sweep. `require-local`-gated |
| `make deploy-prod` | **Operator-authorized, bare-only.** Pins its own `DOCKER_HOST`, refuses an ambient one, flips to prod posture, pre-flights, builds, migrates, restores dev posture, then sweeps build exhaust |
| `make seed-prod` | **Operator-authorized, bare-only.** Same posture contract; runs the seed (#187) — the sanctioned closeout-tail data-action path |
| `make verify-prod VERIFY=verify_<name>` | **Operator-authorized, bare-only.** Same posture contract; runs one read-only `verify_*` command under the forced-rollback harness (#249) |

If a deployment target fails partway, `.env` deliberately **remains** in prod posture — a half-finished production deploy needs a human, and the guards below block dev work until it is restored by hand.

**The guards** — both are check-only: they stop on mismatch and never copy or repair anything.

| Target | What it does |
|--------|-------------|
| `require-local` | Prerequisite guard: refuses to run when `DOCKER_HOST` is set. Gates `nuke` and `deploy-dev` |
| `crosscheck-env` | Prerequisite guard: `.env` must byte-match the env file the target implies — `.env.prod` when `DOCKER_HOST` is set, `.env.dev` when unset. Gates `build`, `start`, `migrate`, `makemigrations`, `createsuperuser`, `shell`, `seed`, `verify` and `push-certs` |

Both read `DOCKER_HOST` from the environment *and* from the `-include`d `.env`, so a posture set either way is caught.

**The build-exhaust sweep (#205, v24.31).** Every build leaves exhaust on the daemon it ran against: orphaned (`<none>`) image records plus BuildKit cache entries. Unswept, production's root volume grew ~500MB per release (20% → 58% across ten releases) and Emma's dev VM ~2GB per build. Both deploy targets now sweep automatically — image prune first, then builder prune:

- **Both prunes are needed, and the builder prune is the load-bearing one.** The dangling images' layers are *also* held by build-cache references, so `docker image prune` alone releases records, not bytes — it reclaimed ~0B on both daemons in the field. The builder prune releases the last references and the layers actually die. Measured on dev at v24.31: build cache 26.86GB → 5.85GB, 21.55GB reclaimed on the first swept deploy, then ~0.5–1GB per deploy at steady state.
- **Dev's eviction is size-capped; prod's is uncapped** *(corrected by ops, 2026-08-15, #257 — v24.31 shipped both lines capped)*. Dev keeps `--reserved-space 5GB`: Emma's daemon is shared and builder-prune eviction is daemon-wide, so the reserve bounds collateral eviction of other projects' caches; entries still in use sit on top of the reserve, so the reported total legitimately runs above it (5.85GB total against 5.27GB reclaimable, observed). Prod runs `docker builder prune -f` bare: its daemon is dedicated to this stack, and since `make build` passes `--no-cache` **by design**, no build ever reads the cache — a reserve there would only preserve unreadable exhaust (observed at correction time: 3.2GB, 134 entries, 0 active, under the old cap, so the v24.31 sweep's first prod run correctly reclaimed 0B from it).
- **Never `-a` on the image prune.** This is the prohibition that is genuinely load-bearing: `docker image prune -a` removes tagged base images, forcing a cold re-pull of `python:3.12-slim` — the 2026-07-29 slowdown the sweep must not reintroduce. Verified after each swept deploy: the build's `FROM` step resolves with zero image-layer downloads.
- **Prune flags are parsed client-side** *(corrected by ops, 2026-08-15, #257 — v24.31 shipped a false "diverges by daemon" story here)*: `DOCKER_HOST` redirects the API call only; the local CLI (29.6.2-rd) parses both deploy targets' prune lines, and it accepted the old `--keep-storage` spelling merely as a hidden deprecated alias (proven live in the v24.31 closeout tail against prod's Engine 25.0.16). The deprecated spelling is gone: dev uses the canonical `--reserved-space`, prod carries no size flag at all.
- **The sweep is loud-but-non-fatal** *(hardened by ops, 2026-08-15, #257 — v24.31 shipped make's silent `-` error-ignore prefix)*: each prune line is `<cmd> || echo "WARNING: ..."`, so a future flag removal or daemon error prints an unmissable warning while housekeeping still can never fail a deploy. On `deploy-prod` it runs **last, after the posture restore**: both prunes pin their own `DOCKER_HOST` and neither reads `.env`, so posture is irrelevant to them, and running them after the restore means not even a Ctrl-C during a long prune can strand production posture.

> **Critical workflow note:** After editing any file under `django/src/`, run `make build` before testing. `make restart` alone recreates containers from the existing image and picks up no changes.

---

## 3. Django Project Structure

The Django project lives in `django/src/game_mvc/`.

### 3.1 `asgi.py`

Entry point for all traffic. Sets `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` to `game_mvc.settings.production` by default.

```python
application = ProtocolTypeRouter({
    'http': django_asgi_app,
    'websocket': AuthMiddlewareStack(
        URLRouter(websocket_urlpatterns)
    ),
})
```

`AuthMiddlewareStack` populates `scope['user']` with the authenticated Django `User` before the consumer receives the connection.

### 3.2 `routing.py` — WebSocket URL registry

```python
path('ws/shyship/<uuid:game_id>/', ShyshipConsumer.as_asgi()),
path('ws/shyland/',               SkylandConsumer.as_asgi()),
```

### 3.3 `urls.py` — HTTP endpoints

| Path | Handler |
|------|---------|
| `/` | `HomeView` (game lobby) |
| `/admin/` | Django admin |
| `/accounts/` | `django.contrib.auth.urls` (login, logout, password) |
| `/api/auth/` | DRF browsable API auth |
| `/shydle` | Shydle app |
| `/shyship/` | Shyship app |
| `/shyland/` | Shyland app |

App-level routes inside the Shyland include are documented in [Section 4.10](#410-http-routes-urlspy).

### 3.4 Settings

| File | Used when | Notable differences |
|------|-----------|---------------------|
| `base.py` | Always imported | PostgreSQL, Redis channel layer, all INSTALLED_APPS |
| `production.py` | Default (container env) | `DEBUG=False`, `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER` set |
| `local.py` | Set `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=game_mvc.settings.local` | `DEBUG=True`, `ALLOWED_HOSTS=['*']`, `InMemoryChannelLayer` |

### 3.5 Platform profile system (`apps.profiles`)

`apps.profiles` extends `auth.User` with a `UserProfile` model (`gamer_tag` CharField, max 20, unique, nullable). A `post_save` signal auto-creates a `UserProfile` for each new `User`. As of v16, Shyland no longer derives `Character.name` from the gamer tag at read time — the gamer tag (falling back to `user.username`) is only the *default value* offered in the character creator's Name field. Once a `Character` exists, its `name` field is authoritative and independent of the profile.

## 4. The Shyland App (`apps/shyland/`)

### 4.1 Models (`models.py`)

#### Module-level constants

*(v24.0 brief 1 adds the Draught Law floor; otherwise unchanged from v15)*

```python
COMBAT_ROUND_TICKS    = 3     # ticks between combat rounds
DYING_DURATION_SECS   = 30    # seconds in dying state before death fires
FLEE_COOLDOWN_TICKS   = 3     # rounds before another flee attempt is allowed
STALE_SESSION_SECS    = 30    # close a CombatSession with no tick activity
XP_PENALTY_MIN_LEVEL  = 10    # level threshold for XP death penalty
DEATH_DURABILITY_LOSS = 10.0  # durability % lost per equipped item on death
CORPSE_DECAY_MINUTES  = 10    # minutes until a corpse is deleted by the tick engine
ACUITY_DRIFT_RATE     = 0.01  # Acuity movement per tick toward baseline
STAT_POINTS_PER_LEVEL = 5     # unspent stat points awarded per level-up
VITALITY_REGEN_SECS   = 120   # seconds to regen full Vitality from zero out of combat
LONGEVITY_REGEN_SECS  = 3600  # seconds to regen full Longevity from zero out of combat
VITALITY_PERCENT_HEAL_FLOOR = 25  # the Draught Law's minimum heal (v24.0, #139)
```

#### `UnarmedMessagePool`, `UnarmedMessage`

*(unchanged from v15)*

#### `Origin`

One new field added in v16:

```
name             CharField(100)
slug             SlugField(unique)
description      TextField(blank)
acuity_baseline  FloatField
acuity_band_low  FloatField
acuity_band_high FloatField
attire_material  CharField(200, blank=True, default='')   ← new in v16
```

Ordering: `['name']`. `attire_material` holds the Origin's material/palette phrase for the starting-attire flavor text (see Section 4.11). `description` is now seeded with real content (see Section 4.8).

#### `Archetype`

One new field added in v16:

```
name                 CharField(100)
slug                 SlugField(unique)
description          TextField(blank)
primary_stat_1       CharField(3, choices=STAT_CHOICES)
primary_stat_2       CharField(3, choices=STAT_CHOICES)
attire_silhouette    CharField(200, blank=True, default='')   ← new in v16
unarmed_message_pool ForeignKey(UnarmedMessagePool, SET_NULL, null, blank, related_name='archetypes')
```

Ordering: `['name']`. `STAT_CHOICES` covers all six stats: str, dex, end, int, wis, per. `attire_silhouette` holds the Archetype's garment-silhouette phrase for the starting-attire flavor text (see Section 4.11). `description` is now seeded with real content (see Section 4.8).

#### `Zone`, `Area`, `Room`, `RoomVisit`

Changed in v20 (brief 1): **`Room` coordinates are now the map's positional source of truth, and `Room` gains four boundary-flag booleans** (migration `0026`).

- **Coordinate semantics.** `coord_x`/`coord_y`/`coord_z` are per-zone map-space — one room per `(zone, x, y, z)` cell, no exceptions. **z is a map plane index, NOT elevation** (the Ridge's climb is flavor text; the whole Z01 surface sits at z=0 while the caves occupy other planes). Exits remain the connectivity source of truth. The core invariant: every unflagged cardinal exit between same-zone rooms lands grid-adjacent at the same z — north `(0,+1,0)`, south `(0,−1,0)`, east `(+1,0,0)`, west `(−1,0,0)`.
- **Boundary flags.** `exit_north_boundary` / `exit_south_boundary` / `exit_east_boundary` / `exit_west_boundary` (`BooleanField(default=False)`, cardinals only — up/down exits always break fragments and need no flag). An undirected edge is a boundary if either side is flagged; the seeder always sets both sides and verification enforces the symmetry. A flagged exit is exempt from the geometry invariant. Cross-zone cardinal exits (the Tree Arch) are boundaries automatically and carry no flag.
- **Extended seed verification (`_verify_map_geometry`).** Runs on every reseed and fails the command loudly on violation: (1) geometry agreement for every unflagged intra-zone cardinal exit; (2) cell uniqueness per `(zone, x, y, z)`; (3) flag symmetry — a flagged exit exists and its reverse is flagged back; (4) flag inventory — flags exist on exactly the five authored cave-mouth pairs (module constant `BOUNDARY_PAIRS`) and nowhere else; plus room-name uniqueness per zone (see the reconcile-identity change in Section 4.8).
- **MapFrag (derived, never stored).** A connected component over unflagged, intra-zone cardinal exits. Rooms with no cardinal exits are single-room fragments. Fog-of-war remains per-character `RoomVisit`; no new tracking model. See Section 4.12.

Changed in v20 (brief 4): **`Zone` and `Area` each gain `theme_color`** (`CharField(max_length=7, default='#CCCCCC')`, hex like `#7DC95E` — migration `0028`), per the data-into-models principle. These are the location bar's server-delivered colors (Section 4.15); nothing is computed client-side. The authored palette (seeded enforce-exact):

| Zone | theme_color |
|---|---|
| The Convergence (Z05) | `#B387E8` |
| The Verdant Reach (Z01) | `#7DC95E` |

| Area | theme_color |
|---|---|
| Wisteria Walk | `#C9A0DC` |
| Bamboo Run | `#A8C77A` |
| Basalt Way | `#9A9A9A` |
| Fern Boards | `#7FA86B` |
| The Everround *(v24.25, #95)* | `#C9AE7A` |
| Morra's Smithy *(v24.25, #95)* | `#C0855C` |
| Fernwater Vale | `#8FCF9F` |
| The Sagewind Flats | `#B4C79A` |
| Spinner's Hollow | `#CCCCCC` *(unauthored — model default until authored)* |
| The Silken Cleft | `#D8D4C8` |
| The Whistling Sink | `#9FC4D8` |
| The Drone Pit | `#D8B45A` |
| The Viridian Ridge | `#40B58C` |
| The Undercrag | `#8FA3B8` |
| Chitterdeep | `#C09A6B` |
| Hollowcrown | `#C4D96B` |

Any zone or area created by other briefs inherits the default until authored. The seed verification asserts the authored values on every reseed.

Changed in v24.25 (brief 1, #41): **`Zone` gains `entry_requires_zone`** — a nullable self-FK (`on_delete=SET_NULL`, `related_name='unlocked_by'`, migration `0043`): to enter this zone a character must have fully explored the named zone (GDD §2.12). Seed-authored world data; null = open; nothing derives from `danger_level`. The only authored lock is The Verdant Reach requiring The Convergence.

Added in v24.25 (brief 1, #41): **`ZoneCompletion`** (placed beside `RoomVisit`) — the permanent per-character zone key: `character` FK (`related_name='zone_completions'`), `zone` FK (`related_name='completions'`), `completed_at` (`auto_now_add`), unique `(character, zone)`. Minted at the `record_room_visit` choke point when a character's distinct visits cover every room of a zone — recorded for every zone, locked or not — and by the grandfather data migration `0044` (every character existing at migration time: the Convergence unconditionally, plus honestly computed completions for every zone their visits already cover; reverse is a deliberate no-op — keys are never revoked, including by rollback). See Section 4.3.

The Convergence's area roster grows to six in v24.25 (#95): The Everround (the 40 ring rooms) and Morra's Smithy (both smithy rooms) join the four park paths; the Heart stays deliberately area-free.

`RoomVisit` itself is *(unchanged from v15)*.

#### `Character`

Changed in v16: `name` is now a real, required database field, placed immediately after `user`:

```
user  OneToOneField(User, CASCADE, related_name='shyland_character')
name  CharField(20)                                                    ← new in v16
```

Uniqueness is **case-insensitive and enforced at the database level** via a functional constraint in `Character.Meta`:

```python
constraints = [
    models.UniqueConstraint(
        Lower('name'),
        name='shyland_character_name_ci_unique',
        violation_error_message='That name is already taken.',
    ),
]
```

Django validates this constraint in `full_clean()`, so the admin and any `ModelForm` path get a friendly "That name is already taken." error rather than an `IntegrityError`. `Character.clean()` also strips surrounding whitespace from `name`, so padded names cannot be stored through any `full_clean()`-based write path.

The previous read-only `@property def name` — which returned `user.profile.gamer_tag` falling back to `user.username` — has been **removed entirely**. `Character.name` is set once at creation (defaulting to the gamer tag in the creator form, but freely overridable) and is thereafter independent of the profile. The max length of 20 matches `UserProfile.gamer_tag`'s constraint. The `ObjectDoesNotExist` import that supported the property was also removed.

Migration `0017` adds the field in the standard safe three-step form so it applies cleanly on databases that already contain `Character` rows (this codebase runs as multiple installations): `AddField` nullable → `RunPython` backfill (gamer tag falling back to username, truncated to 20, deduplicated case-insensitively with numeric suffixes) → `AlterField` to non-null + `AddConstraint`. On this deployment the table was empty, so the backfill was a no-op; the populated-table path was exercised by planting rows on the 0016 schema and migrating forward.

New in v20 brief 3 (#45): `show_timestamps = BooleanField(default=True)` (migration `0027`) — whether the client displays the `[HH:MM:SS.ss]` prefix on stamped-category messages. Display preference only; the envelope's `ts`/`seq` fields are always present regardless (Section 4.13). Written by the `timestamps on|off` command, synced to the client via the connect-time `verbs` message and every `status` payload (Section 4.14). New in v22 brief 2 (DD §10, migration `0031`): `echo_mode = BooleanField(default=True)` — the pane-only command-echo preference, carried exactly like `show_timestamps`; and `brief_mode`'s field default flipped to `False` (brief off for new characters; existing rows untouched). New in v24.29 (brief 1, #235, migration `0049`): `plunder_mode = BooleanField(default=False)` — when on, the rights-scoped corpse sweep runs automatically at the moment combat ends (Sections 4.3 and 4.9). It joins the settings family beside the other three; unlike them it drives server behavior rather than client rendering, so it never rides the `verbs` or `status` payloads.

New in v25.5 (brief 1, #281, migration `0053`): `outfit_snapshot = JSONField(null=True, blank=True, default=None)` — the agent door's strip/dress memory (`[{"instance_id": pk, "slot": code}, ...]`, written before any unequip, consumed — set null — by every dress attempt whatever its outcome; null = no outfit held; Section 4.22). The same release adds the module-level `RESERVED_BOT_NAMES = frozenset({'sudo', 'sirius'})` (GDD §3 — bot names join the name law; the creation form refuses them with the NPC-collision sentence, no-leak) and the module-level `record_room_visit_sync(character, room)` — the arrival choke point (#50 visit law, #41 key minting) extracted from the consumer so the door's move action records arrivals identically; the consumer's `record_room_visit` wrapper delegates to it.

Changed in v24.26 brief 1 (#38, migration `0045`): **`attuned_node = ForeignKey('TravelNode', null=True, blank=True, SET_NULL, related_name='attuned_characters')`** replaces the removed `recall_room`. The bond is the character's home node (GDD §2.11, Attunement): where `home` delivers and where death respawn wakes — one home concept, always the same node. **Null means the founding node** (the Heart of the Convergence, pinned by seed-verify as `travel_name='The Convergence', node_type='obelisk'`), which is what every existing character's `recall_room` pointed at — so the removal needed no data migration and no grandfather step. Character creation sets no bond (null = the design default); `seed_world`'s recall backfill line is deleted. The string reference `'TravelNode'` is required because `TravelNode` is defined after `Character` in the file.

#### `ItemDefinition`

One new field added in v18 (brief 1), alongside the existing display-related fields (`mystery_name`, `mystery_description`):

```python
suppress_mk_suffix = models.BooleanField(
    default=False,
    help_text='If True, display names never show the "Mk N" suffix. '
              'Used for tier-material items (copper/silver/gold/platinum) '
              'whose material name already conveys the tier.',
)
```

This is **display-only**: `mk_tier`, scaling, and rarity machinery are untouched — a Copper Ring of Strength still has a real `mk_tier` and scales normally; the player just never sees "Mk N" appended to a tier-material name. Flavor materials (iron, wood, leather) do *not* suppress — an Iron Sword still displays "Iron Sword Mk 1". The suffix suppression is honored by `get_display_name_with_tier()` in `item_utils.py` (see Section 4.6). Migration `0018` adds the field (single `AddField` with a default — safe on populated databases).

V24.28 (#211, #245) corrects the field's help text: `suppress_mk_suffix` is a **display flag and not ladder membership**, and the two facts are independent **in both directions**. The v19 freebie kit (`tarnished-band`, `cloudy-glass-pendant`) suppresses the suffix while sitting off the tier-material ladder entirely; sphaerium sits on the ladder and does *not* suppress. Ladder membership lives in `tier_material_mk_min` (below), never in this flag.

V24.28 (#211, #245) adds the tier-material range pair (migration `0048` — two nullable `AddField`s plus the `AlterField` for the help text above):

```python
tier_material_mk_min = models.PositiveSmallIntegerField(null=True, blank=True)
tier_material_mk_max = models.PositiveSmallIntegerField(null=True, blank=True)
```

A non-null `tier_material_mk_min` means the definition **is a rung on the tier-material ladder** — the eight-rung sequence copper=1, silver=2, gold=3, platinum=4, rhodium=5, iridium=6, osmium=7, sphaerium=8 (see Section 4.8 for the seeded ladder). Null means the definition is not on the ladder, which is what every pre-existing row takes at migration time — correct by construction, since only the seed authors membership.

`tier_material_mk_max` carries the rung's ceiling. **Null with a set minimum is the unbounded shape** — the rung covers every tier from its minimum upward, without limit. Exactly one rung is authored this way: **sphaerium**, the terminal rung, which is precisely why sphaerium is the one rung that does not suppress its Mk suffix. A bounded rung's material name *is* its tier display; an unbounded rung's cannot be, because a Mk 8 and a Mk 47 piece would otherwise render identically. `tier_material_mk_max` is meaningless unless `tier_material_mk_min` is set.

The range is enforced at generation time — see the guard in `generate_item_instance` (Section 4.6).

Brief 4 adds a second field and a new item type (migration `0021`):

```python
base_value = models.BigIntegerField(
    default=1,
    help_text='Authored worth in copper at Mk 1, Common. Item value = '
              'base_value × mk_tier × rarity multiplier. Sale price is '
              'one third of value.',
)
```

`base_value` is the single authored input to item valuation — everything else is derived by the `item_utils.py` helpers (see Section 4.6). Every seeded definition gets an authored value via the seed's back-fill (see Section 4.8); the default of 1 exists only so the migration is safe, and the seed's verification pass fails if any definition is left at it.

The `ITEM_TYPE_CHOICES` gained **`material`** ("Material") — vendor-sellable materials with no slots, stats, or durability (hides, carapaces, and their future kin). Materials flow through the same `ItemInstance` machinery (they have a real `mk_tier` and rarity, which scale their value) and sell through the same `sell` path as gear.

V24.9 (#129) adds the authored armor base (migration `0041`):

```python
armor_base = models.FloatField(
    default=0.0,
    help_text='Authored base armor per Mk tier (#129): TAV adds armor_base '
              'x mk_tier for every equipped, non-broken instance. 0 = '
              'authors no protection. Rarity-blind; rolled physical_resist '
              'is bonus on top.',
)
```

`armor_base` is the only armor authority (the v22 `ARMOR_SLOT_WEIGHTS` table is deleted, not kept in parallel): `total_armor_value` adds `armor_base × mk_tier` for every equipped, non-broken instance, with no slot or type gate — only armor definitions author nonzero values today, and the code does not care. Rarity never varies the base (secondary slots remain rarity's only lever); rolled `physical_resist` is bonus strictly on top. The 18 seeded armor definitions author their retired slot weights (chest pieces 3, head/legs/off-hand 2, the rest 1 — one full set still totals 13 per Mk tier).

V24.23 (#215) replaces the flat carry field with the percentage pair (migration `0042` — a `RenameField` plus a defaulted `AddField`, safe on populated databases):

```python
carry_pct_base = models.IntegerField(default=0)    # renamed from carry_bonus
carry_pct_per_mk = models.IntegerField(default=0)
```

An equipped bag contributes `carry_pct_base + carry_pct_per_mk × mk_tier` percentage points — the **instance's** Mk tier — to the carry-capacity multiplier (see `carry_capacity` in Section 4.6). Deterministic: no rarity roll, bags roll no stats. The flat `carry_bonus` is gone from every code path; only bag definitions author nonzero values today (satchel 10/5, patchwork-satchel 5/3), and the code does not care.

**V25.12 (#311/#312) — the durability-posture invariant (migrations `0055`/`0056`).** `durability_posture_violations(takes_durability_loss, durability_table)` — module-level in `models.py`, pure, no ORM (it lives here rather than `item_utils.py` because `item_utils` imports from `models`) — returns a list of human-readable violation strings, empty = coherent: wear off ⇒ the table must be `[]`; wear on ⇒ a non-empty list whose entries are dicts with exactly the keys `min`/`max`/`penalty` (numbers — booleans refused; `0 ≤ min ≤ max ≤ 100`, `0.0 ≤ penalty ≤ 1.0`) and whose bands cover **every integer 0–100**, the coverage-gap violation naming the uncovered range. Coverage, not non-overlap: bands may touch or overlap (the seed's do), the reader's first-match-wins is untouched, and `get_durability_penalty`'s 1.0 no-band fallback stays exactly as it was — unreachable now, a tripwire against future misconfiguration. Three layers route through the one validator: **`ItemDefinition.clean()`** (new — `ValidationError` keyed to `durability_table`, so the admin form renders the errors in place), the door's definition-edit validation (Section 4.22), and its cheap core as the repo's first `CheckConstraint` — **`durability_posture_coherent`** in the new `ItemDefinition.Meta` (`condition=~Q(takes_durability_loss=True, durability_table=[])`, the Django ≥ 5.1 kwarg). **#312:** `blank=True` added to exactly six JSON list fields — `ItemDefinition.valid_slots`, `.durability_table`, `.primary_stats`, `.secondary_stat_pool`; `ItemInstance.rolled_primary_stats`, `.rolled_secondary_stats` — so the admin can re-save rows whose lists are legitimately empty (`DialogueEntry.keywords` was the in-repo idiom this extends; `LootTableEntry.rarity_weights` deliberately stays required — never legitimately empty, must sum to 100). Migration order is load-bearing: the authored, reverse-noop `0055_normalize_durability_posture` (broken-pair definitions flipped non-wearing; non-integral `durability_current` instances normalized to `float(int(round(v)))` with `is_broken` re-derived — #314's completing move; one UTC-Z-stamped count line each, dev counts 0/0) runs before `0056`'s six alters + `AddConstraint`.

#### `TravelNode`, `TravelMessage` (new in v18 brief 2) — and `ZoneGate` removed

**`ZoneGate` no longer exists.** The model (introduced v15, authoring-only, never wired to a command; no gate data was ever seeded) was superseded by the Obelisk Network and deleted in brief 2 — model, admin registration, and all references. Migration `0019_travelmessage_travelnode_delete_zonegate` drops the table and creates the two new models below. Do not hunt for `ZoneGate` in current code; it survives only in migration history (`0016` created it, `0019` deletes it).

**`TravelNode`** — the node registry for the Obelisk Network (GDD 2.11). Fields:

| Field | Type | Notes |
|-------|------|-------|
| `room` | `OneToOneField(Room, related_name='travel_node')` | One node per room, enforced by the one-to-one |
| `travel_name` | `CharField(max_length=60, unique=True)` | Unique, typeable destination name shown in travel lists (e.g. "The Convergence") |
| `node_type` | `CharField(max_length=12, choices=...)` | `'obelisk'` (sends to every revealed node) or `'checkpoint'` (relays to revealed obelisks only — never to another checkpoint; v24.26 brief 1, #30) |

There is no per-character revelation table: a character's available destinations are derived entirely from `RoomVisit` — any node whose room appears in their visit records is revealed, permanently. The network is global (no zone scoping) and travel is free (no cost, no cooldown). Node rooms are safe by seeding convention (GDD 2.11); the travel logic performs no combat checks. Since v24.26 (#30) both node types send; the destination pool is sender-typed (see the `travel` command, Section 4.3).

**Effective-home resolution (v24.26 brief 1, #38).** Module-level sync helpers in `models.py`, placed after `TravelNode`: `resolve_home_node(character)` returns `character.attuned_node` when a bond exists, else the founding-node query (`travel_name='The Convergence', node_type='obelisk'`, `select_related('room__zone', 'room__area')`); `resolve_home_room(character)` returns that node's room. One resolver, two consumers — the WebSocket consumer's `get_home_room` (a `database_sync_to_async` wrapper that loads the character with the attuned-node path select_related) and the tick engine's `execute_death` — so `home` and death respawn can never disagree about where home is (ruling B5). Callers in async context must go through a sync wrapper.

**`TravelMessage`** — global flavor-text pools for travel events. Fields: `category` (`'traveler'` — shown to the traveling player; `'departure'` — shown to players in the origin room; `'arrival'` — shown to players in the destination room) and `text`. Witness messages (departure/arrival) may include the literal placeholder `{name}`, replaced with the traveling character's name via `str.replace` (not `.format`, so stray braces in prose are harmless); traveler messages take no placeholders. One message is selected uniformly at random from the appropriate pool per event — travel text is never hardcoded. Pools are global, not per-zone.

#### Battle-zone mechanic fields (new in v18 brief 3)

Three fields, one per model, added by migration `0020` (all nullable/defaulted `AddField`s — safe on populated databases; unset means "exactly the pre-brief behavior"):

- **`RoomSpawn.requires_living_npc`** — nullable FK to `NpcDefinition` (`SET_NULL`, related name `dependent_spawns`). If set, the tick engine only refills this spawn while a live `NpcInstance` of that definition exists in the same room. This is a spawn dependency, not an encounter system: it gates boss minions on their boss — minions respawn on their own `respawn_minutes` timer while the boss lives, reinforcements stop the moment it dies (survivors remain until killed), and gated spawns refill again once the boss respawns, so the encounter resets as a unit. `null` = ungated, spawn behaves as before.
- **`LootTableEntry.guaranteed_group`** — `CharField(max_length=40, blank, default='')`, an optional group label (e.g. `"weapon"`). At roll time each distinct group in a table yields **exactly one** of its entries, every roll, unconditionally; the entry is chosen by weighted selection using `drop_chance` as a *relative weight* (weights need not sum to anything). Grouped entries make no independent drop rolls. Blank = today's independent per-entry roll.
- **`NpcDefinition.death_message`** — `TextField(blank, default='')`. If non-blank, broadcast verbatim to every character in the NPC's room (including the killer) exactly once, at the moment of death, after the kill line and before any corpse/loot output. No substitution placeholders — the text is authored per NPC and stands alone. Blank = no extra message.

#### Commerce fields (new in v18 brief 4)

Two fields added by migration `0021` (alongside `ItemDefinition.base_value` and the `material` type above; all defaulted `AddField`s — safe on populated databases):

- **`NpcDefinition.is_repairer`** — `BooleanField(default=False)`. This NPC offers repair services; the `repair` command routes to a living repairer in the room. Vendors need no analogous flag — an NpcDefinition with one or more `VendorEntry` rows is a vendor, as before.
- **`VendorEntry.sold_count`** — `IntegerField(default=0)`. Units sold so far. An entry with a finite `stock_limit` is **exhausted** when `sold_count >= stock_limit`: it is omitted from `list`, and buying it reports `Sold out.` Entries with `stock_limit=NULL` are unlimited and never exhaust. There is no restock mechanism yet — exhaustion is permanent until an admin resets `sold_count` or raises the limit.

#### Attack focus field (new in v19 brief 6)

One field added by migration `0022` (a nullable/defaulted `AddField` — safe on populated databases; unset means "exactly the pre-brief fallback behavior"):

- **`CombatSession.focus_npc`** — nullable FK to `NpcInstance` (`SET_NULL`, related name `+`). Player-controlled attack focus: the NPC the character's auto-attack targets each round and the NPC the Acuity single-target damage bonus applies to — one field drives both. `null` = no explicit focus; the tick engine falls back to the first live NPC in the session (see Section 4.9), so pre-existing active sessions keep working across the deploy with no data migration. Set by `cmd_attack` on engagement (new session or a newly-added in-session target — engagement always steals focus) and on an explicit refocus against an already-in-session target (see Section 4.3); auto-shifted by the tick engine, with an announcement, when the focused NPC dies and other live NPCs remain in the session; cleared when the session ends.

#### Room-output and combat-protection flags (new in v19 brief 8)

Two fields added to `NpcDefinition` by migration `0024` (defaulted `AddField`s — safe on populated databases; both defaults reproduce the pre-brief-8 stance, so no existing NPC's behavior changes until the seed authors otherwise):

- **`is_fixture`** — `BooleanField(default=False)`. Purely a display switch: fixtures are listed under the room's "What's here?" section instead of "Who's here?" (see Section 4.3). Does not affect combat in any way.
- **`attackable`** — `BooleanField(default=True)`. If `False`, this NPC can never be targeted, engaged, or hold aggro — checked independently of room safe-flags at every point an NPC could become a combat participant (see Sections 4.3 and 4.9). Vendors, repairers, and the sphere/shard/obelisk "who"s that cannot be harmed are seeded with `attackable=False` (Section 4.8).

The same migration changes `Character.brief_mode`'s model default from `False` to `True` (`AlterField`, no data migration — existing characters keep their stored value; only newly created characters get the new default). See Section 4.3 for the corresponding room-rendering rewrite.

#### NPC dialogue engine models (new in v19 brief 9)

Five new models, migration `0025`, FK'd off `NpcDefinition`/`NpcInstance`/`Character`/`Room`:

- **`DialogueEntry`** — one keyword→response map, greeting, or departure reaction for one NPC definition (`npc_definition` FK, `entry_type` one of `keyword`/`greeting`/`departed`). `keywords` (`JSONField`, default `[]`) holds lowercase single-word tokens for `keyword`-type entries; empty for `greeting`/`departed`. `note` is an authoring aid — it also doubles as the seed's per-entry lookup key when an NPC has more than one entry of the same type (Section 4.8), but carries no runtime meaning.
- **`DialogueResponse`** — one line in an entry's response pool (`entry` FK, `text`).
- **`DialogueConnective`** — position-aware connective color for the second (`position_class='second'`) or third-and-later (`'later'`) speaker in a multi-NPC reply to one utterance. `template` uses `{name}`, substituted via `str.replace` — the established `UnarmedMessage`/`TravelMessage` convention (Section 6), not `.format`.
- **`PendingDialogueResponse`** — a scheduled, not-yet-delivered response: `utterance_id` (UUID, groups every responder to one `say` or greeting event), `npc_instance`, `entry`, `character` (the asker), `room` (the delivery room, captured at scheduling time so a departed asker can't silence it), `position` (0 = first speaker), `is_final`, `fire_at`. **Runtime state — never touched by the seed sweep**, joining Brief 8's never-touched list (Section 4.8).
- **`DialogueGreetingRecord`** — marks that an NPC definition has already greeted a character (`unique_together=('character', 'npc_definition')`). **Runtime state — never touched by the seed sweep.**

Three module-level constants in `models.py`: `DIALOGUE_FIRST_DELAY_TICKS = 2` (delay before the first responder speaks), `DIALOGUE_STAGGER_TICKS = 2` (additional delay per responder position — slower than combat's 1-tick cadence, "more sociable, less interrupty"), `DIALOGUE_RECENT_EXCLUDE = 1` (consecutive-repeat exclusion depth — an NPC won't reuse the response it gave last time, when its pool offers an alternative).

#### `ItemInstance` location integrity (v23 brief 2, #137; v24.27 brief 1, #234)

- **`ItemInstance.owner`** — `on_delete` is now **`CASCADE`** (was `SET_NULL`; migration `0046`, an `AlterField` with every other attribute — `null=True, blank=True, related_name='inventory'` — unchanged; v24.27 brief 1, #234). Held items die with their character by ruling: hard delete only, the Django admin console the only deletion surface, the whole inventory — held and equipped, bound and unbound — removed at the collector level. Room items and corpse contents are unaffected by construction: their `owner` is already NULL. The old `SET_NULL` orphaned a deleted character's inventory as all-NULL-location rows (the deletion collector bulk-updates around `save()`, so the exactly-one-location check below never fired); pre-existing orphans were swept one-time by data migration `0047` — the exact ruled filter (owner, current_room, and corpse all NULL), count printed, deliberate no-op reverse; 1 row deleted on dev, expected 0 on prod. `soulbound_to` stays `SET_NULL` by ruling: bound items can never leave inventory (drop excludes them, corpse contents are by definition unbound, vendor sale removes the item), so every row it references dies in the same owner cascade.
- **`ItemInstance.corpse`** — `on_delete` is now **`CASCADE`** (was `SET_NULL`; migration `0035`, an `AlterField` with every other attribute — `null=True, blank=True, related_name='contents'` — unchanged). Corpse contents are by definition unowned, unequipped, unbound loot, so unconditional destruction on corpse delete is always correct: the leak (decayed corpses stranding their contents as location-less rows) is structurally impossible from **any** corpse-deletion path, not just the decay sweep. Decayed loot is gone — no spill-to-ground (deliberately unforeclosed as a future feature; a spill would relocate items before corpse deletion).
- **`ItemInstance.save()` enforces exactly-one location.** The invariant check is now `non_null != 1` over owner/current_room/corpse — zero locations and multiple locations both raise `ValidationError` naming the count found (previously only more-than-one was rejected; zero passed silently, which is what kept the #137 leak invisible). A pre-tightening audit confirmed no game flow (creation, transfer, loot, drop, buy, sell, gift, equip/unequip — consumer, tick engine, item_utils, seed, admin) legitimately saves a zero-location instance. The CASCADE acts at the Django-collector level and never calls `save()`, so the two changes do not interact.

#### `ItemInstance.is_artifact` REMOVED (v23 brief 4, #146; migration `0037`)

The v15-era boolean was read by no code and written by nothing — its only presence was an authoritative-looking **Flags** checkbox in admin. Because artifacts are by settled design hand-authored by super-users through admin, that dead checkbox was a trap: ticking it while leaving `rarity` alone would have produced an artifact with none of #138's vendor protection, silently. **`rarity` is the single artifact marker** — the #138 refusal, valuation multipliers, secondary-slot counts, and the flag block all key on it.

#### All other models

`EffectDefinition`, `EffectComponent`, `EffectInstance`, `EffectComponentInstance`, `LootTable`, `NpcEffect`, `NpcInstance`, `Corpse`, `CombatAction` — *(unchanged from v15, except: v24.0 brief 1 adds `restore_vitality_percent` to `COMPONENT_TYPE_CHOICES` — the Draught Law's component type, whose computed magnitude is a fraction of `vitality_max`; choices-only `AlterField` migration `0040`, DB no-op)*. `ItemInstance` is unchanged apart from the v23 brief 2 location-integrity changes and the v23 brief 4 field removal above. `LootTableEntry` and `RoomSpawn` are unchanged apart from the v18 brief 3 fields above; `NpcDefinition` and `VendorEntry` are unchanged apart from the v18 brief 3 and 4 fields above. `CombatSession` is unchanged apart from the v19 brief 6 field above. The MC models live with their subsystem sections: `MCEvent` (migration `0050`) in Section 4.19, and the `MCKillSwitch` singleton (v25.4 brief 1, #266; migration `0052`) in Section 4.21.

### 4.2 Currency system (`currency.py`)

*(unchanged from v15)*

### 4.3 WebSocket consumer (`consumers.py`)

Changed in v18: brief 1 rewrote the `equip` command and centralized name-plus-tier display formatting; brief 2 added the `travel` command; brief 4 added the `list`/`buy`/`sell`/`repair` commerce commands and targetless `attack`/`kill` under aggro. Changed in v19: brief 1 added the `wallet` command and an `inventory` wallet section, and reordered the vendor-presence checks in `cmd_buy`/`cmd_sell` ahead of the argument checks; brief 2 made `send_room_description` fresh-fetch its character and extended the personal-group message handler with an `event` field (see below); brief 3 reworked `cmd_attack`'s engagement messaging, added the `npc_display_name` ordinal helper, and extended `room_message` to honor engine-side broadcast exclusion (see below); brief 4 added revival to `cmd_use` and a `'clear'` event on `player_message` (see below); brief 6 gave `cmd_attack` a third outcome — refocusing an in-session target — and moved `npc_display_name`/`ORDINALS` out to `combat_utils.py` (see below); brief 9 added the NPC dialogue engine's `say` hook and room-entry greeting hook (see below); brief 10 added the gazebo kibitz hook, pity-repair lines, the worthless-sell refusal, and a currency-display pass over `list`/`buy`/`sell`/`repair` (see below); brief 11 added the `quit` command (see below). Changed in v20 (brief 1): every room-changing path — `connect`, `cmd_move` (both the normal and the aggro-ambush branch), `cmd_travel`, `cmd_flee` (both post-move branches), and the `'respawn'` player-message event — now finishes with `send_map()` (see Section 4.12). Changed in v20 (brief 2): the consumer overrides `send_json` as the single delivery choke point — every outbound message passes through it and receives the `seq` envelope field there; every message-creation site stamps `ts`; the `room_message`/`player_message` handlers carry the event's creation-time `ts` through instead of restamping (see Section 4.13). Changed in v23 (brief 2): `cmd_inventory`'s stacking gate widens from consumable-only to the wear-free type set, and the grouping key gains soulbound state (see the inventory-stacking subsection below, #18). Changed in v23.3 (brief 1): `cmd_use` sends one merged use+effect sentence per item and gains the instant-restore aggregate path (see the use-output subsection below, #149/#151). Changed in v24.4 (brief 1): the `heal` command — a bare-verb front door over that aggregate path (see the heal subsection below, #166). Changed in v24.7 (brief 1): equipment display — the shared paper-doll helper with bare `equip` and consumed hand rows, the examine `Hands:` row, the `_slot_cell` two-handed word, and the hands-conflict refusal clause (see the equipment-display subsection below, #195/#176/#194). Changed in v24.11 (brief 1): knowledge by holding (#80) — `transfer_to_character` and `do_loot_item` flip `is_identified` True on take (unidentifiable-guarded; `do_loot_item` composes its looted name after the flip so the line names the real item), and `cmd_examine` became close inspection: an unidentified (non-unidentifiable) item renders the full identified detail block via an output-only in-memory identify — never persisted — while the `is_unidentifiable` mystery block drops the redundant cannot-determine parenthetical (see the identification rule in Section 6). Changed in v24.16 (brief 1): the inv trim (#208) — `cmd_inventory` renders the Inventory table alone; the paper-doll belongs to bare `equip`, the money line to `wallet` (see the equipment-display subsection below), with the equipped-items query retained for the capacity numbers. All other command handling *(unchanged from v16)*.

#### The sell partition (v23 brief 4, #138)

`cmd_sell`'s bulk path partitions the resolution three ways and reports in a fixed order: **paying** items aggregate per definition through `_aggregate_by_name` (pooled `SELL_SINGLE`/`SELL_BULK` lines, success voice); **worthless** items (0-copper sales) get exactly one trailing `SELL_WORTHLESS_TRAILING` remark covering the whole remainder; **refused** items (artifact rarity) get exactly one trailing `SELL_REFUSAL_PARTIAL` remark (warn voice) — or, when nothing moved at all, one `SELL_REFUSAL_NONE` line. The v22 skipped-counter branch (`Nothing sold — N worthless items skipped.`) is deleted; worthless items are no longer skipped, they sell. The single path mirrors it: artifact → generic refusal; 0-copper → the snark replaces the payment sentence; otherwise a pooled payment line. The bulk price map is keyed by `id(item)` — `do_sell` deletes the row and Django nulls the in-memory pk, so pk-keyed prices collapsed to one entry (a latent v22 shape whose group totals silently read the last-sold price; fixed here).

#### The bulk-sell consumable guard (v23.1 brief 1, #150)

Exactly the noun-less **`sell all <rarity>`** form excludes items of type `consumable` from the sale — the guard lives in the resolver at candidate-pool construction (see Section 4.14), keyed on the **absence of a noun token**, never on what a noun matched, and runs before selection ordering and all value math. Every noun-carrying form (`sell all draught`, `sell 5 draught`, `sell draught`, `sell all common draught`) reaches consumables normally; bare `sell all` keeps the v22 teaching refusal (fn 17); `drop` is untouched. Materials, readables, and keys stay bulk-sellable, the equipped-items exclusion and lowest-first selection are unchanged, and #138's zero-value disposal keeps its non-consumable exit — the guard narrows *default* reach, not sellability. The guard is announced, never silent, with two authored lines (warn voice, verbatim): when a guarded sell skips consumables, `cmd_sell` appends exactly one note after the aggregated sale lines and trailing remarks — `Your consumables stay in your pack — name them ('sell all draught') to sell them.` — driven by `Resolution.bulk_excluded`; when the matches are *all* consumables the resolver refuses the transaction entirely as world-declined — `That's all consumables — name them ('sell all draught') if you mean to sell them.` — and nothing sells. Tab completion is unaffected (noun pools don't change; the guard alters only noun-less selection). *Test suite:* `tests/test_bulk_sell_consumable_guard.py` (resolver + end-to-end consumer coverage; `test_command_grammar.py` case 17 converted to the guarded expectation with original intent preserved).

#### Inventory stacking (v23 brief 2, #18)

`cmd_inventory` groups the unequipped listing by the module constant **`STACKABLE_ITEM_TYPES = {'consumable', 'material', 'readable', 'key'}`** (display constant, alongside `SLOT_ORDER`/`SLOT_CAPACITY`). The rule behind the set: **wear-free interchangeable types stack; per-instance-identity types never stack** — `weapon`, `armor`, `accessory`, and `bag` carry durability and rolled stats, so each renders its own row always. The grouping key is **(definition, mk_tier, rarity, soulbound state)** — a bound copy (e.g. super-user gifted) never merges with unbound copies of the same definition/Mk/rarity; this deliberately tightened the pre-existing consumable stacking too (a bound and an unbound stack of the same consumable render as two rows, correctly). The grouping walks adjacent rows after the sort, so the sort key is extended to `(display name lowercased, definition_id, mk_tier, rarity, is_soulbound)` — alphabetical-by-name remains the only visible order; the trailing components exist solely to make same-group rows adjacent instead of interleaving with same-name ties. A grouped row renders exactly as the consumable grouped row always has: representative = the group's first instance, Quantity = group count, Slot/Details cells unchanged in form. Display-only — command grammar, sell, and use were already stacking-agnostic per the ruled #22 resolution; no model change, no migration.

#### The merged use sentence and the instant-restore aggregate path (v23.3 brief 1 — #149, #151)

**One sentence, one envelope (#149).** For every item the per-item loop applies, `cmd_use` sends **one** success-category message composed by `effect_utils.compose_use_sentence` (Section 4.4) from the transactional subject (`item_ref(item, indefinite=True)`) and the effect layer's clause pairs: `You use a Healing Draught Mk 1 and feel your body recover. (+25 Vitality)`. The v22 two-send shape — the plain transactional sentence followed by per-message `system` effect lines — is dead. Timed-effect consumables (empty pair list, e.g. Focus Tonic) keep the plain `You use a Focus Tonic Mk 1.`; `Nothing happens.` (warn), the #61 full-health gate, the revival sequence's own lines, and the per-loop stop-at-full logic for non-aggregatable items are unchanged.

**The aggregate path (#151).** Taken before the per-item loop when the character is **not dying** and **every** resolved item qualifies: all of its effect's components instantaneous, at least one vitality restore — `restore_vitality` or `restore_vitality_percent` (v24.0 brief 1) — (`use_items_aggregatable`, derived from the components — never a flag). Steps, in order:

1. **Entry gate (#61, unchanged):** fresh character read; at full health → `You are already at full health.` (warn), return.
