2. **Deficit** from that same fresh read; then `_apply_aggregate_heal` (one `@database_sync_to_async` call) **plans consumption without applying** — walking `res.items` in resolution order (use's policy selection is `'oldest'`, oldest-first; never re-sorted), accumulating each item's heal — `restore_vitality`: `computed_magnitude(mk_tier)` verbatim; `restore_vitality_percent` (v24.0 brief 1): `percent_heal_amount(computed_magnitude(mk_tier), character.vitality_max)`, the Draught Law from THAT item's Mk and the DRINKER's max — and stopping at deficit coverage, the requested count (the resolver caps `res.items`), or exhaustion.
3. **Applies once:** exactly ONE atomic clamped vitality UPDATE for the total (`Least(F('vitality_current') + total, F('vitality_max'))` — the #52 law, regardless of count). Non-vitality instant components of consumed items (none in current seed data — never silently dropped) apply via `_apply_instant_component`, their clause pairs joining the sentence; `EffectInstance` bookkeeping runs per consumed item as the per-item path does; consumed instances are deleted.
4. **Composes one line:** subject = the `item_ref` article form when exactly one item was consumed, otherwise comma-joined `(definition, Mk)` groups in consumption order, each `{display name} ×{count}` (the Amendment-5 count form, no article — ×1 groups included in the mixed case). Clause `feel your body recover`, annotation `(+{summed magnitude} Vitality)` — the displayed number is the sum of consumed magnitudes; the DB clamp renders truth on the bars. Deficit covered → the line ends ` You are restored to full health.` and sends as `reward` (the fold — no second message); otherwise `success`.
5. **Shortfall warn:** `You only had {consumed}.` (warn) fires only when the ask exceeded inventory **and** the deficit went uncovered; a deficit-driven stop never warns.
6. **Exactly one status payload** closes the command.

Dying `use` never aggregates — the revival path below owns it, swallowing exactly one restorative (its line merged per #149, the sequence itself untouched). *Test suite:* `tests/test_use_output_merge.py` (merged line, aggregate math and composition, fold, shortfall, single-write capture, gate, dying, NPC-path byte pin); `test_command_revamp.py`'s sequence test converted to the aggregate shape with original intent preserved.

#### The `heal` command (v24.4 brief 1, #166)

`heal` is a **bare verb** — "use as many healing draughts as needed" — one computed transaction on the aggregate machinery above. `cmd_heal` is a thin front door: it composes no sentences of its own, and an aggregate `use N healing draughts` was already legal in every state `heal` works in — the command removes the arithmetic from the player, adding no new power.

**Registration.** `COMMAND_TABLE`: `'heal': ('cmd_heal', False)` — the `False` args-flag means dispatch invokes the handler with no arguments, so `heal 5 draughts` is byte-identical to bare `heal` (DD §9.1 fn 2). In `DYING_ALLOWED` (self-rescue, beside `use`); **absent** from `COMBAT_BLOCKED` (proceeds in combat, identical to `use`), `PROMPT_VERBS` (no required target), and `GRAMMAR_VERBS` (no noun pool — tab completion completes the verb only, flowing from the dispatch table into the connect-time verbs payload with no further wiring). Help row in Action commands between `flee` and `home`: `Drink healing draughts until your vitality is full.`

**The qualifying pool** (the mechanical meaning of "healing draught family"): carried consumables passing the per-item aggregate test — every effect component instantaneous, at least one `restore_vitality`/`restore_vitality_percent` — **never a name match**. The test is the v23.3 qualification extracted per-item as `_item_aggregatable` (a staticmethod; `use_items_aggregatable` is now `all()` over it), and `heal_qualifying_items` filters the carried consumables through it, ordered oldest-first by the machinery's own age key — `command_grammar.oldest_first`, the `'oldest'` selection policy (#168) exposed as a public helper for callers building a resolution-shaped request without a noun. Never re-sorted; each consumed item's heal computes from **its own Mk** under the Draught Law.

**Gate order and delegation.** Dying with a non-empty pool: exactly one restorative (the oldest) runs through the per-item use/revival path — the `cmd_use` loop extracted verbatim as `_use_per_item` — so the v19 revival sequence fires unchanged and the aggregate path stays dying-forbidden. Not dying with a non-empty pool: delegation to `_use_aggregate` with a `Resolution` over the **entire** pool, uncapped, `requested=len(pool)` — the aggregate path supplies the deficit math (fresh read), minimum-count consumption, the merged count-form line, the full-heal fold, the standing shortfall warn (#132), and the single status payload, all unchanged; its entry gate fires the #61 at-full refusal first. Empty pool: **the at-full refusal beats the empty-pool warn** — at full vitality, `You are already at full health.` fires even with nothing carried (heal's purpose is already fulfilled; inventory is irrelevant); otherwise `You have no healing draughts.` (warn; the string is emitted from exactly one site). The #61 gate itself is extracted as the shared `_at_full_gate` (fresh read, unchanged wording), used by both `_use_aggregate` and `cmd_heal` — no refusal string is duplicated.

**The six output cases** (the brief's authoritative table): (1) at full, with or without draughts → the #61 refusal, warn; (2) not full, zero qualifying draughts → `You have no healing draughts.`, warn; (3) supply covers the deficit → one merged count-form reward line ending ` You are restored to full health.` (the fold), surplus untouched; (4) supply exhausted short → the merged success line, then `You only had N.` (warn) on its own line; (5) dying with ≥1 draught → exactly one consumed, the revival sequence verbatim; (6) dying with none → the empty-pool warn. Cases 3/4 end with exactly one status payload. *Test suite:* `tests/test_v244_heal.py` — registration and absences, all six cases, gate order, oldest-first mixed-Mk consumption, combat passage, args-ignored dispatch, the help row (18 tests).

#### The repair kit and the per-item durability gate (v24.12 brief 1, #134)

The Repair Kit is the field-repair consumable — `use repair kit` patches the character's most-damaged owned item through the live `durability_restore` application (Section 4.4). Kits always take the per-item path: `_item_aggregatable` is false for them by construction (no vitality-restore component — asserted in tests, never re-derived), so the aggregate path needs no change. Resolution, tab completion, stacking, and the `use` grammar are untouched.

**The gate.** In `_use_per_item`, every item whose effect passes `effect_restores_durability(effect_def)` — true when any component's `component_type == 'durability_restore'`; derived from components, **never a name match** (the #61 helper's law, beside `effect_restores_vitality`) — is gated **before** `do_apply_effect` and **before** `consume_item`. A refusal consumes nothing and ends the command. The order is normative:

1. **Dying** (`was_dying` — dying wins over combat when both hold): `Patchwork won't save you now — you need healing.` (warn). Nothing but healing while dying.
2. **In combat** (`get_active_combat_session`): `There's no patching anything up in the middle of a fight!` (warn).
3. **Eligibility** — a fresh `durability_restore_need(char)` read returning `(eligible, broken)` over everything owned that takes durability loss. No eligible target and `used == 0`: the broken-only warn `What's broken is beyond a field patch — you need a real repairer.` when broken (0%) items exist, else the zero-need warn `Nothing you own needs patching.` — kit not consumed either way. No eligible target and `used >= 1`: the **fulfilled-purpose stop** — `Everything you own is in good repair.` (reward, its own line, mirroring heal's full-health fold precedent) and the sequence stops; `stopped_fulfilled` suppresses the #132 only-had-N warn.
4. Otherwise: apply, consume, print the merged per-line mend sentence (success) — existing machinery, one envelope: `You use a Repair Kit Mk 1 and patch up the Iron Mace Mk 1. (+25 durability)`.

**Sequences are per-item** (repair-family output stays per-line, never count-form): each kit in a `use N repair kit` sequence re-targets most-damaged-first through its own fresh query inside the application, so two kits over two damaged items print two mend sentences, each naming its item. After the loop the standing shortfall rule fires unchanged (`You only had N.` when the request exceeded inventory and the sequence wasn't fulfilled-stopped), and the standard status payload sends when `used >= 1`. *Test suite:* `tests/test_v2412_repair_kit.py` — seed shape, targeting/clamp/Mk/tie-break/broken cases, all four refusals with non-consumption, the fulfilled stop, shortfall, per-line output, non-aggregatability, equipped eligibility (20 tests).

#### Revival in `cmd_use`, and the `'clear'` event (v19 brief 4)

`cmd_use` captures `was_dying = self._character_is_dying` before doing anything else — the in-memory dying flag kept current by the `'dying'`/`'respawn'` event handling below, not a DB read, since the character may have fallen between commands. The effect applies exactly as before (instantaneous `restore_vitality` mutates the same `Character` object `cmd_use` already holds, in place, inside the `@database_sync_to_async` call), the item is consumed, and its messages print unchanged *(v23.3 brief 1: the use line and effect messages now merge into the one composed sentence — see the use-output subsection above; the revival sequence itself is unchanged)*. Only then does revival get checked, against a fresh read: `char_fresh = await self.get_character_fresh()`; if `was_dying and char_fresh.vitality_current > 0`, the character revives —

1. `revive_character(char_fresh)` clears `is_dying`/`dying_since` (two-field save, mirrors the tick engine's own dying-state writes).
2. `self._character_is_dying = False` — the `receive_json` command gate opens back up on the very next message, no round-trip through a fresh fetch required.
3. Personal system line: `Breath floods back into your lungs. You are alive — barely.`
4. `send_room_description(room, entering=False)` — the standard full room render (which also pushes the current, now-revived status payload), not a special-cased status dict.
5. `broadcast_to_room_exclude(f"{char_fresh.name} staggers back to their feet!", 'combat')` — the room hears it; the reviving player doesn't get a redundant copy.
6. Early `return`, skipping the generic post-`use` status send below it (the room description already sent one).

No bonus and no forced full heal — the character rises with **exactly** the amount the item restored, capped at `vitality_max` by the same `min()` `_apply_instant_component` always applied. A non-healing `use` (or one that doesn't push vitality above zero) takes the untouched path: item consumed, messages printed, generic status sent, dying window keeps closing — `cmd_use` never special-cases or blocks a non-revival use while dying, matching `receive_json`'s existing allowlist (`use` is the only verb permitted through the gate regardless of what it does).

**The character was never removed from their `CombatSession`** — only actual death does that (`execute_death`, Section 4.9) — so revival needs no session repair. The next combat round picks the character back up exactly as `load_participants` always would: the NPC-attacks-character guard (`if character.is_dying: continue`) no longer trips, and the auto-attack guard (`not character.is_dying`, also Section 4.9) no longer blocks a fresh player action either. Rising at a sliver of Vitality into an unfinished fight is intentional, not an oversight.

**The `'clear'` event** — `player_message` now checks `event.get('event') == 'clear'` first, ahead of the existing `text`/`status` delivery, and sends a bare `{'type': 'clear'}` to the client whenever it fires (independent of whether the same engine message also carries text — the tick engine's fall sequence sends it as its own zero-text message, see Section 4.9). Client-side (`game.html`), a `type: 'clear'` message runs `output.innerHTML = ''` — the exact same primitive the room-entry path already used for `msg.enter`, just exposed as its own message type so the engine can trigger it outside a room render. This clears the **contents** of the `#output` div only; the div itself (`role="log" aria-live="polite"`) is never removed or rebuilt, so a screen reader's live-region subscription survives the clear and the very next appended line — the fatal-blow line (see Amendment 1 below) — is announced normally. `aria-live="assertive"` is never used for any of this; politeness is unchanged.



#### Fresh-read status and the `player_message` event field (v19 brief 2)

`send_room_description(room, entering, force_long)` now opens with `char = await self.get_character_fresh()` instead of reading the cached `self.character`. Real bar changes (combat damage, passive regen, level-ups) happen in the tick-engine process and are written straight to the DB; the consumer's cached `self.character` only reflects what it looked like at connect or at the last command that happened to fresh-fetch. Since every caller of `send_room_description` (`look`, `cmd_move`, `travel`, login) built its status payload from that stale cache, all of them could show a bar value that the next tick "corrected" a moment later. `get_character_fresh()` re-assigns both `self.character` and `self._character_is_dying` as a side effect, so this one change also keeps the dying gate current on any command that reaches this method. The other two status-payload builders (`cmd_use`, `cmd_stats`) already called `get_character_fresh()` before building their payload and are unchanged.

The `player_message` handler (the `player_{character_pk}` group event fired by the tick engine, see Section 4.9) gained an `event` field on top of the existing `text`/`category`/`status` fields, read after those are delivered: `None` (default, no special handling — the vast majority of ticks), `'dying'`, `'respawn'`, or — since v21.1, checked *before* any delivery — `'superseded'` (see the single-session enforcement subsection below).

- **`'dying'`** — sets `self._character_is_dying = True` directly, no DB fetch. The tick engine sends this the instant it sets `character.is_dying = True` server-side (both the combat brink-of-death path and the DoT-kill path), so the command dispatch gate (`receive_json`, blocks all commands but `use` while dying) is correct on the very next message the player sends — previously the flag only updated when a command happened to call a fresh-fetching handler, so a command sent between the fall and that next fresh fetch could slip through.
- **`'respawn'`** — runs the same synchronization sequence a normal move performs: `get_character_fresh()` (clears `_character_is_dying`, since the DB row is already updated by the time the engine sends this event), swap `self.room_group` from the old room to `room_{character.current_room_id}` (`group_discard` the old group, `group_add` the new one), reset `self.last_direction = None` (respawn has no direction for `flee` to reverse — the same rule `travel` follows), then `send_room_description(room, entering=True)` for the new room — identical to normal movement, including `RoomVisit` bookkeeping. Without this, a respawned character's client stayed subscribed to the death room's broadcast group and showed the death room's last-rendered output until their next explicit `look`.

#### Wallet display (v19 brief 1)

`cmd_wallet()`, dispatched on the verb `wallet` (no arguments; any given are ignored). Fetches a fresh character via `get_character_fresh()` — already `select_related('current_room__zone', ...)`, satisfying `format_wallet()`'s need for `character.current_room.zone` — and sends a single line: `Wallet: {format_wallet(character)}`. Subject to the same dying-state command gate as every other command (no special-casing).

`cmd_inventory()` gains a wallet section appended after the existing item listing, separated by a blank line:

```
Wallet:
  {format_wallet(character)}
```

The wallet line also uses a fresh `get_character_fresh()` fetch, independent of the `self.character`-derived equipment/item listing above it, so the copper and zone-display alias shown are always current. `format_wallet()` (previously defined but called by nothing) is no longer orphaned.

#### Buy/sell vendor-check order (v19 brief 1)

`cmd_buy`/`cmd_sell` now check for a living vendor in the room **before** checking whether arguments were given. A bare `buy`/`sell` in a vendor-less room now reads `There is no one here to trade with.` instead of `Buy what?`/`Sell what?`, which previously implied a shop was present. At a vendor, bare `buy`/`sell` is unchanged (`Buy what?`/`Sell what?`). Only the check order moved; no message text changed.

#### The `travel` command (v18 brief 2; shard relay in v24.26 brief 1, #30)

`cmd_travel(args)`, dispatched on the verb `travel`, with two forms. Both forms start with the same source check against the current room's `TravelNode`:

- **No node in this room** → warn: `There is no obelisk or shard here. Travel is a gift of the network — you must stand before one of its stones.`
- **Both node types send (v24.26, #30).** The former checkpoint refusal is deleted; the destination pool is sender-typed instead.

The character's revealed destinations are computed as all `TravelNode` rows whose room has a `RoomVisit` for this character (helper `get_revealed_destinations(current_node, spheres_only=False)`), excluding the node the character is standing at, ordered alphabetically by `travel_name`. No zone filtering — the network is global. **The relay filter (v24.26, #30):** `spheres_only=True` adds `.filter(node_type='obelisk')` to the standing membership check — no new revelation machinery, no new per-character state. `cmd_travel` and the tab completer `_complete_travel` both pass `spheres_only=(node.node_type != 'obelisk')`, so a shard's pool is its holder's revealed **spheres** only: an unrevealed sphere is absent from the listing, absent from completion, and refused on a direct `travel <name>` attempt; a revealed shard never appears from a shard sender. From an obelisk the full revealed pool is unchanged.

**`travel` (no argument) — list.** Empty set → `The Obelisk is silent. It has nothing to show you yet — the network reveals itself only to those who walk it.` (shard-sender variant in the shard's voice: `The Shard is silent. It knows no sphere to pass you to — the network reveals itself only to those who walk it.` — defensive only; the Heart reveals at first login, so a shard's sphere pool is never empty in practice). Otherwise the per-zone block listing (Section 4.14, "Travel listing") — the opener is `The Obelisk offers passage to...` from a sphere and `The Shard reaches through the network to the spheres it answers to...` from a shard; format otherwise identical for both sender types, muted locked-zone rows included.

**`travel <destination>` — go.** The argument is matched against the revealed set by case-insensitive prefix match on `travel_name`, additionally ignoring a leading `The ` on the node name (`_travel_name_matches` static helper — `travel verdant` and `travel the verdant crown` both match "The Verdant Crown"). No match → `The Obelisk knows no such place — or you have not yet stood there. Type "travel" to see where it can send you.` Multiple matches → the matching names are listed and the player is asked to be more specific; nothing changes. On a unique match, the travel sequence runs:

1. A random `departure` message (with `{name}` substituted) is broadcast to the origin room, excluding the traveler.
2. The character moves using the same machinery as normal movement: `group_discard` the old room group, the existing `move_character()` helper, `group_add` the new room group (`self.last_direction` is reset to `None` — travel has no direction for flee to reverse).
3. A random `traveler` message is sent to the traveler, followed by the full standard room output (`send_room_description(entering=True)` — identical to normal movement, including `RoomVisit` bookkeeping on arrival; no special-casing).
4. A random `arrival` message (with `{name}` substituted) is broadcast to the destination room, excluding the traveler.

Travel is free: no currency cost, no cooldown, and no combat check (node rooms are safe by design — safety is a seeding concern, not travel logic). The obelisk speaks no words: no NPC dialogue is involved; all travel text comes from the `TravelMessage` pools, selected randomly per event. Supporting DB helpers: `get_travel_node(room)`, `get_revealed_destinations(current_node, spheres_only=False)`, `get_random_travel_message(category)`.

#### Zone entry locks and keys (v24.25 brief 1, #41, GDD §2.12)

Locks are world data (`Zone.entry_requires_zone`, Section 4.1); keys are player data (`ZoneCompletion`, permanent). Three pieces of machinery, all in `consumers.py`:

- **Key minting — `record_room_visit`.** The fog-of-war choke point every arrival path already calls (connect, move, travel, flee, home, respawn) also mints keys: when the visit row is newly created, the character's distinct `RoomVisit` count for the room's zone is compared to that zone's room count; equality creates the `ZoneCompletion` via `get_or_create`. The return signature is `(first_visit, zone_completed)` — `zone_completed` is `True` only when the completion row was newly created, so a grandfathered (pre-existing) key suppresses the announcement, and the check runs only on first visits (revisits cost nothing). Completions are recorded for every zone, whether or not any lock currently requires them.
- **The unlock announcement — `announce_zone_completion`.** Each arrival path calls it after the room render when minting occurred: one random line from `ZONE_COMPLETE_LINES`, **reward** category (green), celebrating the completed zone by name and never naming the zone(s) it unlocked.
- **The gate — `check_zone_lock(character, destination, current_zone_id)`.** One shared `@database_sync_to_async` helper called from both transition sites — `cmd_move` (after the exit resolves, before the departure broadcast or any state change; no cooldowns touched) and `cmd_travel` (after the destination match resolves, before departure messaging). Logic: same-zone destination → pass (only *entering* a locked zone is gated; no character is ever ejected from a zone they stand in); no lock on the destination zone → pass; key held → pass; otherwise it returns a refusal line the call site sends in the **warn** voice. The refusal draws from the generic, door-agnostic `ZONE_LOCK_REFUSAL_LINES` — every gate shares one pool, lines speak about the requirement (never the door), naming the required zone and exactly one Area of it: the Area holding the most rooms this character has not visited (`Count` aggregate over unvisited rooms excluding `area IS NULL`, ordered `-n, area__name` for a stable tie-break). When no unvisited room carries an Area, `ZONE_LOCK_REFUSAL_LINES_NO_AREA` speaks instead. Lines never state counts. All three pools are module-level constants (three lines each, the pooled-speech floor).
- **The travel listing (GDD §2.11).** Locked destinations stay listed and stay matchable — revelation, the match pool, and tab completion (`_complete_travel`) are untouched; the attempt draws the refusal. Display: `get_completed_zone_ids` fetches the character's key ring once per listing; a zone block whose lock is set and unkeyed renders its destination **table rows** through the existing `muted` seg voice (`_table_lines`' tuple-cell form — no new color literal; the chart set-equality test stays green) while the zone **heading** keeps its zone-theme color — the lock mutes the destinations, not the zone's identity.

#### Attunement: the `attune` command and the effective home (v24.26 brief 1 — #38, #30 GDD §2.11)

**`cmd_attune`** — bare verb (chart fn 2: all arguments ignored), registered in `COMMAND_TABLE` as `('cmd_attune', False)`. Presence is the argument; three exhaustive cases:

1. The current room has no `TravelNode` → warn, one line from the module-level pool `ATTUNE_NO_NODE_LINES`.
2. The room's node equals the **effective home** (`get_effective_home_node` → `resolve_home_node`; a null bond compares as the founding node, so a fresh character typing `attune` at the Heart is told they are already attuned) → warn, one line from `ATTUNE_ALREADY_LINES`.
3. Otherwise → atomic `Character.objects.filter(pk=...).update(attuned_node=node)` (`set_attuned_node`; never read-modify-write on a cached object), then one line from `ATTUNE_SUCCESS_LINES` ending in the stable, exact parenthetical `(Home: {travel_name})` — the house cooldown-refusal shape: varied prose, machine-honest parens, composed outside the pool so it never varies. Success voice.

The three pools live at module level alongside the zone-lock speech, ≥3 lines each. **Gating:** `attune` is deliberately absent from `COMBAT_BLOCKED` — every attunable room is a safe room, so the success path cannot occur in combat and an in-combat `attune` can only ever draw the no-node warn; while dying the central deny-by-default gate (`DYING_ALLOWED` whitelist) refuses it. Any travel node is attunable — shard and sphere alike, the Heart included (re-attuning at the Heart is the way back to default). One home at a time: attuning replaces the previous bond, free, instant, no cooldown of its own, and never touches `home`'s cooldown machinery. Help (`HELP_SECTIONS`), the connect-time verbs list, and verb tab completion pick the command up from the dispatch table; there is no noun pool.

**The stats `Home:` row (operator-ruled on #38).** `_send_stats` renders `  Home: {travel_name}` directly under the `Player:` line, before the blank line and the stat rows — the effective home node's `travel_name` (a null bond renders `The Convergence`), resolved via `get_effective_home_node` over the already-loaded character (`get_character_fresh` select_relates `attuned_node`). Value-color key/value form: a rendering, exact, never varying.

#### The equip exchange rule (`cmd_equip`)

The old behavior — refuse whenever a target slot is occupied or a two-handed conflict exists — is replaced by a general exchange rule. For each slot in the item's `valid_slots` (in order), `cmd_equip` computes a **displacement set**: every currently equipped item that must come off for the new item to legally occupy that slot.

- The current occupant(s) of the slot, if it is at capacity. Slot capacity comes from the module-level `SLOT_CAPACITY = {'RING': 2}` — RING is the only slot a character has two of; every other slot holds one item. A slot below capacity contributes an empty base set; a slot at capacity contributes one candidate per occupant (displacing that occupant).
- If the new item is two-handed: every equipped item in MAIN_HAND or OFF_HAND, plus every equipped item whose definition is two-handed **regardless of its slot** — a two-handed item claims the character's hands from wherever it sits, including a two-handed bow equipped in RANGED.
- If the new item is one-handed and the candidate slot is MAIN_HAND or OFF_HAND: additionally, any equipped item whose definition is two-handed (its hands-claim conflicts even from RANGED).

Outcome selection, in `valid_slots` order:

1. **Any candidate with an empty displacement set** → equip there. Output: `You equip <item> in your <slot>.`
2. Otherwise, take the minimal displacement sets across candidates:
   - **Minimum size ≥ 2** → refuse, naming every item in the set: `You'd have to unequip your Iron Sword and your Wooden Shield first.` Nothing is unequipped.
   - **Minimum size 1, all minimal sets contain the same item** → **auto-swap**: unequip the displaced item, equip the new one, and send a single exchange message: `You unequip your Broadsword and equip your Wooden Shield in your Off Hand.` (Display names via `get_display_name()`.)
   - **Minimum size 1, but different candidate slots displace different items** (ambiguous — a third ring while both RING slots are full; a MAIN_HAND/OFF_HAND item while both hands hold different items) → refuse, naming the candidates: `Both ring slots are full — unequip your Copper Ring of Strength or your Copper Ring of Wisdom first.` (Non-ring ambiguity uses `You'd have to unequip your X or your Y first.`) Nothing is unequipped.

**Auto-swap defers to the existing unequip constraints.** Before swapping, the displaced item is checked with the same rules `cmd_unequip` applies — cursed items cannot be removed; a bag cannot come off if doing so would violate the carry limit. These checks live in the shared helper `_unequip_blocked_reason()`, used by both `cmd_unequip` and the auto-swap path, so the refusal messages are identical and a partial swap can never occur. The swap itself reuses the existing `unequip_item()`/`equip_item()` helpers, so soulbinding and slot bookkeeping behave exactly as a manual unequip-then-equip would.

A module-level `_join_owned_names(items, conj)` helper formats the `your X and/or your Y` lists in refusal and exchange messages.

#### Equipment display: the shared paper-doll, bare `equip`, handed-ness disclosure (v24.7 brief 1 — #195, #176, #194)

Three display surfaces, one theme — the display says "two-handed" and shows its consequences. Zero mechanics changes: the resolver, hand-claiming rules, auto-swap/refusal logic, and every state gate except the combat-gate scoping below are byte-identical.

**The shared paper-doll (`_equipment_doll_lines(equipped)`, #195).** The Equipment block — the `Equipment...` header plus the 14-row `Slot / Name / Details` table over `SLOT_ORDER`/`SLOT_CAPACITY` — is extracted from `cmd_inventory` into this one helper; bare `equip` is its sole caller since v24.16 (#208 — `cmd_inventory` renders the Inventory table alone), so the composition has one owner and one rendering (GDD §6.11, §9.1 fn 21). **Bare `equip`** (empty args after the verb, whitespace-insensitive; checked in `cmd_equip` before the resolver): renders the paper-doll block **only** — no inventory table, no carry count, no wallet line — via `send_report_lines` (report category, unstamped, never varies). `equip`/`eq` left `PROMPT_VERBS` (the fn-10 prompt is dead for this verb — the bare form is a valid information rendering now); the help usage row reads `equip [<item>]`; `GRAMMAR_VERBS` and tab completion are untouched (the noun pool still completes).

**Consumed hand rows (#176).** Inside the helper: a MAIN_HAND or OFF_HAND row with no direct occupant, while a two-handed item is equipped in any other slot, is a **consumed row**, not an empty one — Name is the consuming item's `get_display_name_with_tier`, muted; Details is `(two-handed)`, muted. The item's home row keeps its normal rendering and real Details. A two-hander in a hand slot consumes the other hand row; one in RANGED consumes both. Genuinely free slots keep the muted `-`. The hand-claiming invariant (at most one two-hander ever equipped, GDD §3.6) makes the consumer unique; only the two hand rows can render as consumed (RANGED itself is never claimed, #178).

**Handed-ness disclosure (#194).** `_format_identified_item_lines`: every `item_type == 'weapon'` gains a `Hands:` row — `Two-handed` when `definition.is_two_handed`, else `One-handed`, both values always rendered — immediately after the `Damage:` row (after `Genre:` when no damage row). `_slot_cell`: the Slot cell names **all** valid slots — sentence-case labels joined with `/` in authored `valid_slots` order (`Main hand/Off hand` — the seed Combat Knife, the only current dual-slot case; v24.8 brief 1, #197) — and a two-handed weapon appends `(two-handed)` once, after the full joined label (`Main hand/Off hand (two-handed)` — defensive composition, no such item in seed) — inherited automatically by every caller (inventory table, vendor list). **The hands-conflict refusal clause:** the two `You'd have to unequip … first.` refusal forms (the `min_size >= 2` 'and' form and the ambiguous 'or' form) replace their terminal period with `— the <two-hander> needs both hands.` when the displacement involves hands claimed by (or needed for) a two-hander — composed by the module-level `_hands_conflict_clause(incoming, displaced)`, naming the incoming item if it is two-handed, else the equipped two-hander in the displacement set (`item_ref` composition). Refusals with no two-hander involved, including the both-rings line, keep their standing wording byte-identically.

**Combat-gate scoping (#195 addendum).** The gate follows the act, not the verb: `_dispatch`'s central combat gate skips the session query for a bare `equip`/`eq` invocation (the same rows are already visible in combat via `inv`); targeted `equip` keeps the standing authored refusal unchanged. See Section 4.14.

*Test suite:* `tests/test_v24_7_brief1.py` (bare-form rendering and byte-identity with inv (until v24.16 — #208: re-oracled against the helper directly), consumed rows in all three configurations, the examine `Hands:` row placements, `_slot_cell` variants, both refusal-clause cases with the rings line pinned byte-identical, the combat-gate split, and the dispatch-table deltas; suite 455 → 473).

#### Commerce commands (v18 brief 4)

`cmd_list`, `cmd_buy(args)`, `cmd_sell(args)`, `cmd_repair(args)`, dispatched on the verbs `list`, `buy`, `sell`, `repair`. No aliases.

**Routing.** A **vendor** is a living `NpcInstance` in the character's current room whose definition has at least one active `VendorEntry`; a **repairer** is a living instance whose definition has `is_repairer=True` (DB helpers `get_vendor_in_room` / `get_repairer_in_room`, first match by instance pk). `list`/`buy`/`sell` route to the vendor; `repair` routes to the repairer. Absent or dead service NPCs are out of business until they respawn: `There is no one here to trade with.` / `There is no one here who can repair.` If multiple qualify, the first found is used; checkpoint seeding never places two.

**`list`** — one line per active, non-exhausted `VendorEntry`: the entry's display name, the price in copper, and `(N left)` when `stock_limit` is finite. Exhausted entries are omitted. Vendor entries reference definitions, not instances, so the name is formatted directly from the definition (`_entry_display_name` static helper: `item_definition.name` plus ` Mk {mk_tier}` unless `suppress_mk_suffix`), **not** via `get_display_name_with_tier()`, which takes an instance.

**`buy <item>`** — case-insensitive prefix match against the vendor's active entries' definition names. Checks, in order: entry exists (`They don't sell that.`); stock (`Sold out.`); price ≤ character copper (a can't-afford message naming the price); carry capacity (the same check and message as `pickup`). On success `do_buy` runs atomically (`select_for_update` on both the entry and the character, re-checking stock and funds inside the transaction): copper deducted via `currency.subtract`, `sold_count` incremented, and an `ItemInstance` generated at the entry's `mk_tier` with **rarity always `common`**, owned by the character, unbound (soulbind happens on first equip, as with any item). Vendor buy prices are authored data (`VendorEntry.price`) — never formula-derived.

**`sell <item>`** — case-insensitive prefix match against the character's carried items; if every match is equipped: `You'll have to unequip it first.` Only unequipped items can be sold, but **soulbound status is irrelevant — soulbound items sell normally**. Selling is compensated disposal: `do_sell` credits `get_sale_price(item)` copper (one third of value, minimum 1) and **deletes the ItemInstance** — vendors never resell player items, and there is no buyback. Materials, gear, and consumables all sell through the same path; unidentified items sell at their stored (apparent) rarity.

**`repair <item>` / `repair` / `repair all`** — repair targets are the character's items (equipped or carried) with `takes_durability_loss=True` and durability below 100%. Repair is **paid per attempt; failure is harmless** — copper is spent, the item is unchanged, and the player may retry immediately. Success always restores `durability_current` to 100% (and clears `is_broken`). Items are never destroyed by repair. Cost and odds come from the Section 4.6 helpers: cost = `value × missing_durability_fraction × 50%` (floored, min 1); success chance = `20% + current_durability_fraction × 75%`. An attempt the character cannot afford is refused *without charging*, naming the cost.

- `repair <item>` — prefix match among the character's items; an undamaged or no-durability match gets `That doesn't need repair.`
- `repair` (bare) — one attempt on the most-damaged eligible item (lowest durability, pk tie-break — stable). Nothing damaged → `You have nothing to repair.` Repeated invocations walk the damage list: a failure re-targets the same item, a success moves on.
- `repair all` — one paid attempt per eligible item, most-damaged first, one report line each; stops (and says so) if copper runs out mid-batch; ends with a one-line summary (items repaired, attempts failed, copper spent).

All money movement goes through `apps.shyland.currency` (`add`/`subtract`) inside `transaction.atomic()` blocks with `select_for_update` on the Character row (`do_buy`, `do_sell`, `do_repair_attempt`). Prices and costs are displayed as plain copper integers (`60 copper`), not through the zone-aliased `display_for_zone` — vendors deal in copper.

#### Targetless `attack`/`kill` (v18 brief 4)

`cmd_attack` invoked **without** a target now auto-targets only while the player has aggro — an active `CombatSession` with at least one living NPC opponent. The target is the **first attacker**: the earliest-engaged living NPC in the session, resolved by `get_first_attacker_npc` as the lowest-pk row of the session's `npcs` M2M through table whose NPC is alive (through-row insertion order = engagement order). From there the command proceeds exactly as if the player had named that NPC. With no aggro, bare `attack` still refuses with `Attack what?`. Explicitly named targets behave exactly as before. Net effect: a player jumped in an aggro room can spam bare `attack`/`kill`, stays on the enemy that hit them first until it dies, then rolls to the next-earliest attacker.

#### Engagement messaging, ordinals, and broadcast exclusion (v19 brief 3)

v18 play-testing found `cmd_attack` printed a fresh `You attack the <npc>!` on every invocation even though combat is session-based with automatic per-round attacks — repeat commands against an already-engaged target are pure no-ops, so the ack lied. After resolving the target NPC (both the targetless-aggro path and the parsed-noun path, `npc = await self.parse_npc_noun(args, npcs_in_room)`), `cmd_attack` now fetches the character's active session (`get_active_combat_session`) and checks membership (`npc_in_session(session, npc)`, `session.npcs.filter(pk=npc.pk).exists()`):

- **Target already in the active session** → personal-only output `You're already fighting {display}.` (category `combat`). No broadcast, no `start_combat` call.
- **Otherwise (no session, or a genuinely new target)** → personal output `You move to attack {display}!`; room broadcast (excluding the actor via `broadcast_to_room_exclude`, the existing WS-side channel exclusion) `{character.name} moves to attack {npc_display(npc)}!` — witness lines always use the plain composed reference, never the ordinal; then `start_combat` runs as before.

**`npc_display_name(npc, npcs_in_room, capitalize=False)`** — module-level helper in `combat_utils.py` (moved there from `consumers.py` in v19 brief 6; imported into `consumers.py`, see below), alongside the module-level `ORDINALS = ['first', 'second', … 'tenth']` list. Since v20 brief 5 it composes through `npc_display` (Section 4.16): `"{article} {name}"` when the NPC's `definition.name` is unique among `npcs_in_room`; `"{article} {ordinal} {name}"` when multiple NPCs share that name (proper nouns and `plural_phrase` names never take an ordinal), where the ordinal is the NPC's **position** among same-name NPCs in `npcs_in_room`'s order (the same order `parse_npc_noun` indexes against for `N.noun` syntax) — **not** a stable per-instance number. Killing the first of two same-name NPCs makes the survivor "the {name}" again (or "the first …" if a third of that name is still present) on the next lookup, since the position is recomputed from the room's current live-NPC list every call. Beyond ten same-name NPCs in one room, the helper falls back to the bare name — treated as a seeding smell, not a display problem to solve.

**`room_message` broadcast exclusion.** The tick engine cannot exclude a channel-group member by channel name (it doesn't have one), so room broadcasts that need to skip a specific character now carry `exclude_pk` in the `group_send` payload (see Section 4.9). The consumer's `room_message` handler checks it after the existing WS-side `exclude` (channel name) check: `if exclude_pk is not None and exclude_pk == self.character_pk: return`, dropping the message silently for that one connection before anyone else's copy of the same `group_send` is affected. This is a second, independent exclusion mechanism alongside the pre-existing `broadcast_to_room_exclude`/`exclude` (channel name) used by consumer-originated broadcasts (`cmd_attack`, `cmd_flee`) — the engine has no channel name to key off, so it excludes by character pk instead.

#### Attack focus (v19 brief 6)

`cmd_attack` now drives `CombatSession.focus_npc` (Section 4.1) as well as the engagement messaging brief 3 built. After resolving the target NPC (unchanged — targetless-aggro path or `parse_npc_noun`), the same session/membership lookup from brief 3 now branches three ways instead of two, keyed off `session.focus_npc_id` rather than mere membership:

- **No session, or target not in the active session** — a genuine new engagement, unchanged from brief 3's ack and broadcast (`You move to attack {display}!` / `{character.name} moves to attack the {npc.definition.name}!`), then `start_combat([npc], first_attacker='character', focus_npc=npc)`. `start_combat` sets `session.focus_npc = npc` unconditionally when a `focus_npc` argument is passed, whether it created a brand-new session or added the NPC to one already in progress — **engagement always steals focus**, by ruling, covering both the fresh-engage case and the mid-fight "add a new target" case with one code path.
- **Target in session and `session.focus_npc_id == npc.pk`** — brief 3's already-fighting no-op, unchanged: personal-only `You're already fighting {display}.` (category `combat`), no broadcast, no `start_combat` call.
- **Target in session but not the current focus** — new case. `session.focus_npc` is set to the target and saved; personal-only message, category `combat`: `You change your attacks to focus on {display}.` No room broadcast — witnesses see the ensuing attacks land on the new target; the refocus decision itself is internal to the player.

The `flee` command's aggro-engagement call to `start_combat` (fleeing into a room with aggressive NPCs) passes no `focus_npc` argument, so it is unaffected by this brief — the resulting session's `focus_npc` stays `null` and the tick engine's first-live-NPC fallback (Section 4.9) applies, same as it always implicitly did for a freshly-created session.

#### Room output, rendering semantics, and attack refusals (v19 brief 8)

**Two-section occupant listing, with empty-section suppression.** `send_room_description`'s NPC loop is split by `npc.definition.is_fixture`: living NPCs (`is_fixture=False`) print under a `"Who's here?"` header, fixtures (`is_fixture=True`) print under a separate `"What's here?"` header — each NPC's per-line format was `"{npc.name} is here."` (since v21 brief 1, #55, the lines are bare noun phrases — see Section 4.16). A section with no members is omitted entirely; a room with neither living NPCs nor fixtures shows neither header. Connected-players' line and the ground-items/corpse sections are unaffected and remain separate — ground items are never folded into "What's here?".

**Rendering semantics — a single `_resolve_room_rendering(character, room, force_long, first_visit=None)` replaces the old `_check_and_record_visit`/`_record_visit` pair.** The old code recorded the `RoomVisit` and decided brief-vs-long in two different branches depending on `force_long` (`look`), and unconditionally prepended the area description regardless of which form rendered. As of Brief 1 Amendment 2 (issue #50) the function is **read-only with respect to visits**: recording moved to arrival time — every arrival path (`cmd_move` both branches, `cmd_travel`, `cmd_flee`, the respawn event handler, and `connect`) calls a `record_room_visit(character, room)` helper (`get_or_create`, returning whether the visit is new) immediately after the move and before any aggro check, and passes the result in as `first_visit`; non-arrival renders (`look`, revival) pass `None` and the function looks the visit up without creating it. This fixes the #50 fog-of-war holes: the aggro branch skips the room description by design (ambush), and while recording lived inside rendering those entries never landed a visit. The bookkeeping itself is unchanged — travel revelation still depends on `RoomVisit` — and the function still returns whether the **long** form (long description + area description, if any) should render:

| Situation | Rendering |
|---|---|
| First entry to a room (no prior `RoomVisit`), either `brief_mode` | Area description (if any) + long description |
| Revisit, `brief_mode=True` | Brief description only — no area description |
| Revisit, `brief_mode=False` | Area description (if any) + long description |
| `look` (`force_long=True`), always | Area description (if any) + long description |

The area description is no longer unconditional — it renders exactly when the long description does; a `brief_mode=True` revisit shows the one-line brief and nothing else. `send_room_description` computes `show_long = await self._resolve_room_rendering(...)` once and branches `description_text`/`area_context` off that single boolean.

**Bare `brief` reports the current setting.** `cmd_brief` special-cases an empty argument before the `on`/`off` validation: `Brief mode is {on|off}. Usage: brief on | brief off` (category `system`), no state change — a read, not a write. `brief on`/`brief off` are unchanged. This refines rather than breaks the "boolean commands always require an explicit value" rule (Section 6): the rule governs **setting**, not **querying** — bare `brief` is a query.

**Attack refusal for unattackable NPCs.** In `cmd_attack`, immediately after target resolution (both the targetless-aggro path and the parsed-noun path) and **before** the ordinal-name lookup or any session/engagement logic: if `npc.definition.attackable` is `False`, output `The {npc.definition.name} cannot be attacked.` (category `error`, personal only — the raw definition name, not the ordinal display) and return. No `CombatSession` is created or modified, no room broadcast. This check is independent of, and precedes, any safe-room check — unattackable is a property of the NPC, not the room (Section 6).

**Aggro-on-entry excludes unattackable NPCs.** `get_aggro_npcs_in_room` (used by both `cmd_move`'s entry check and `cmd_flee`'s destination check) gained `definition__attackable=True` alongside its existing `definition__is_aggressive=True` filter — an unattackable NPC can never ambush a player on room entry, regardless of its `is_aggressive` flag.

#### NPC dialogue: the say hook, greetings, and help text (v19 brief 9)

**The `say` hook.** `cmd_say` broadcasts the player's speech exactly as before (`[say] {character.name}: {text}`, category `chat`, no exclusion — asker and witnesses see the identical line), then calls `schedule_npc_dialogue_responses(text)`. That method:

1. **Tokenizes** the said text — lowercase, `[a-zA-Z']+` word extraction via `_tokenize_said_words` (a module-level helper) — into a word set. Empty set (punctuation-only input) short-circuits with no scheduling.
2. **Finds eligible responders** — live `NpcInstance`s in the character's current room whose definition has at least one `keyword`-type `DialogueEntry` with any keyword intersecting the word set. Fixtures and NPCs mid-combat are not excluded (an unmapped fixture is silent anyway because it has no matching entry; a future mapped one, or a fighting NPC, can speak).
3. **Entry-first draw, per eligible NPC** — `random.choice` over that NPC's *matched* entries (not yet the response; the response is drawn at fire time by the tick engine, Section 4.9).
4. **Shuffles and schedules** — `random.shuffle` on the eligible list, then one `PendingDialogueResponse` per NPC under a shared `utterance_id` (`uuid.uuid4()`), `position` = shuffle index, `is_final` on the last, `fire_at = now + (DIALOGUE_FIRST_DELAY_TICKS + position × DIALOGUE_STAGGER_TICKS)` seconds.

No eligible responders → nothing is created, byte-identical to pre-brief `say` behavior.

**The greeting hook.** `send_room_description` already computes `npcs = await self.get_npcs_in_room(room)` for the "Who's here?"/"What's here?" listing (Section 4.1); when `entering=True`, it now also calls `schedule_npc_greetings(room, npcs)` at the very end of the method (after the status payload send), reusing that same NPC list rather than re-querying. This fires on every room-entry path that already passes `entering=True` — `cmd_move`'s no-aggro branch, `cmd_travel`, the flee destination (no-aggro branch), and tick-engine-driven respawn — matching the brief's "same path that runs aggro checks" instruction; `connect()`'s initial login render and the mid-combat revival re-look both call `send_room_description` with `entering` at its `False` default and are unaffected. For each live NPC with a `greeting`-type entry and no existing `DialogueGreetingRecord` for this character: draw one entry (there is normally only one), collect it into the same shuffle-and-stagger scheduling `schedule_npc_dialogue_responses` uses (so simultaneous greeters stagger instead of firing at once), and create the `DialogueGreetingRecord` **immediately at schedule time, not at delivery**. Scheduling the record up front — rather than when the tick engine actually delivers the line — is what makes the guarantee "once per character per NPC, forever" hold even if the same character re-enters the room again before the first greeting has fired.

**Help text.** One line added to `cmd_help`'s output, directly under the `say` entry: `Some of the world's inhabitants listen when you speak aloud.`

#### Commerce: multi-vendor resolution, kibitz, pity repair, worthless-sell refusal, currency display (v19 brief 10)

**Deterministic multi-vendor/repairer resolution was already in place.** `get_vendor_in_room` and `get_repairer_in_room` (Section 4.6 helpers, called from `cmd_list`/`cmd_buy`/`cmd_sell`/`cmd_repair`) already ordered candidate `NpcInstance`s by `.order_by('pk').first()` — the lowest-pk living NPC in the room serves every commerce command. This brief's ruling (lowest pk wins, deterministically) required no code change; it added a symmetric helper, `get_other_vendor_in_room(room, exclude_instance_pk)`, for the kibitz hook below.

**The kibitz hook.** After a successful `buy` or `sell` (not on refusal, not on `sold_out`), `cmd_buy`/`cmd_sell` call `maybe_kibitz(room, vendor)`: it looks up another living vendor `NpcInstance` in the same room besides the one that served the transaction (`get_other_vendor_in_room`), and if one exists, broadcasts one line drawn from the module-level `KIBITZ_LINES` pool (`{other} watches the exchange and nods approvingly.` / `{other} pretends not to supervise, and supervises.` / `{other} rearranges the shelf, satisfied.`), `{other}` substituted with the non-serving vendor's name via `str.replace`, category `room`, no exclusion (the buyer sees it too, same as any other room broadcast). In practice this only fires at the gazebo (Pella and Ferwick are the only NPCs sharing a room with an active vendor entry each), but the check is room-general, not gazebo-specific.

**Pity repair.** `do_repair_attempt`'s cost is normally `get_repair_cost(item)`, which floors at 1 copper even for a worthless item — a real gap the brief's ruling closes: when `get_item_value(item) == 0` (the newbie kit; any future base_value-0 item), the attempt charges **0**, not the floored 1, before the existing success/fail roll runs unchanged. `cmd_repair` (both the single-item and `repair all` paths) then checks the same `get_item_value(item) == 0` condition on a **successful** repair only, and substitutes the standard "restored to full condition" line with a per-repairer pity line — `PITY_REPAIR_LINES`, keyed by `NpcDefinition.slug` (`morra`, `pella`, `ferwick`, `repairbot-prime`), falling back to a generic templated line (`_pity_repair_line`, `{name}` substituted with the repairer's display name) for any other repairer. A **failed** roll on a zero-value item still shows the standard fail message, now correctly reading `(0 copper)` rather than `(1 copper)` — the roll itself is unchanged, only the money is.

**Worthless-sell refusal.** `cmd_sell` checks `get_item_value(item) == 0` before calling `do_sell`; if true, it refuses with `That's not worth anything to me.` (category `error`) and the item is never touched — `do_sell` (which deletes the sold instance) is not reached. This is a new micro-ruling, not previously specified: `get_sale_price` has the same floor-to-1 behavior as `get_repair_cost`, so without this check a worthless item would have quietly sold for 1 copper.

**Currency display pass.** Every player-facing amount in `list`/`buy`/`sell`/`repair` — vendor entry prices, insufficient-funds refusals, sale proceeds, repair costs (including the `repair all` summary line's total spent) — now renders through a new `format_amount(character, amount)` consumer method (a `format_wallet` sibling: same zone-alias lookup via `display_for_zone`, but for an arbitrary amount rather than the character's full wallet). No command in this group prints a raw `N copper` string directly anymore; zone-local currency aliasing (Section 4.2) applies here for free.

#### The quit command (v19 brief 11)

`cmd_quit`, dispatched on the verb `quit` (no arguments). The dispatch entry sits with the other non-combat commands, below the dying gate — so a dying character's `quit` gets the gate's standard refusal exactly like `north`, with no special-casing and no exemption alongside `use`. Two outcomes:

- **In combat** (an active `CombatSession` contains the character, checked against a fresh fetch): refuse with `You cannot leave in the middle of a fight! Break away first — try 'flee'.` (category `error`) and do nothing else — the fight continues.
- **Otherwise:** send the farewell `The world folds itself away behind you. Come back soon.` (category `system`), then `{'event': 'quit'}` — the first client-facing use of the `event` field (Brief 2's field previously rode only the internal `player_message` group payload) — then close the WebSocket server-side with the normal close code. Everything past the close is the existing `disconnect` path, none of it duplicated in `cmd_quit`: guarded presence delete, player/room group discards, heartbeat cancellation, `last_seen` touch. Logging back in after a quit is the standard login flow, landing the character where they quit.

**Client-side (`game.html`):** a message carrying `event == 'quit'` sets a `quitting` flag and, after a ~1s delay, navigates to the games server lobby (`{% url "home" %}` — the same target the site's existing navigation uses). The delay lets the farewell line render in, and be announced by, the polite ARIA live region before navigation. The `quitting` flag suppresses only the close handler's "Connection closed. Refresh to reconnect." placeholder for this deliberate close; a socket close *without* the quit event (browser close, network drop, server restart) behaves exactly as before.

**Help text:** `cmd_help` gained a `quit` line (`leave the game and return to the games lobby`), directly above the `help` entry.

#### Single-session enforcement (v21.1, #116)

The newest WebSocket connection for a character takes over; any older connection prints a farewell and closes. Implemented entirely on the existing personal-group and presence-token machinery — no models, no migration, no tick-engine or presence-Lua changes, and `disconnect()` is untouched.

**The supersede broadcast (`connect()`).** Immediately after the `player_{pk}` group join and the `session_token` mint, and *before* the unconditional presence `SET`, the new connection sends `{'type': 'player_message', 'event': 'superseded', 'token': <its session_token>, 'ts': envelope_ts()}` to the character's personal group — no `text`, `category`, or `status` fields. Every consumer for the character receives it, including the sender. Ordering with the old session's guarded presence delete is safe in either interleaving: if the old session's delete runs before the new presence write, it deletes the old value and the new unconditional `SET` follows; if after, the guarded compare no-ops against the new session's value.

**The `superseded` branch (`player_message`).** Checked first, before the `clear`/`text`/`status`/`fight` delivery and the `dying`/`respawn` event handling. Token match → this is the connection that fired the broadcast; return immediately, delivering nothing. Token mismatch → this consumer has been superseded: (1) the authored farewell `The world's attention shifts — your story is being told through another window now. This one falls quiet.` via the normal output path, category `system`; (2) the client-facing event `{'event': 'superseded', 'ts': …}`, matching `cmd_quit`'s `{'event': 'quit'}` message shape with only the event name changed; (3) a normal `await self.close()`. Everything after is the existing `disconnect()` path, none of it duplicated in the branch: guarded presence delete (a no-op or harmless delete against the new session's key, per the interleaving argument above), group discards, heartbeat cancellation, `last_seen` touch.

**Not a command.** The superseded close passes through no command gates: it fires even while the character is dying and even in combat. The in-combat refusal belongs to `quit` only — a takeover mid-fight is the entire point: combat is DB state and output follows the `player_{pk}` group, so the new connection inherits the fight with zero handover logic.

**No room broadcast.** Witnesses see nothing — the character never left the room; only the link moved.

**Client-side (`game.html`).** `event == 'superseded'` sets a `superseded` flag parallel to `quitting`. On the subsequent close the flag suppresses the reconnect placeholder *and* the auto-reconnect loop (which would otherwise retake the session right back), and does nothing else — no navigation, no reload; the farewell stays visible as the last line on screen (announced by the polite ARIA live region like any other output), and refresh-to-retake remains a legitimate action. A close without the superseded or quit event behaves exactly as before.

**Tests.** `tests/test_session_takeover.py`: the two-connection takeover (ordered farewell → superseded event → close on the old connection; silence plus a functional `look` on the new; the presence key left holding the new session's token after the old session's disconnect settles) and the single-connection regression (a fresh connection ignores its own broadcast by token match).

#### Display formatting

`consumers.py` no longer formats Mk suffixes inline. Every name-plus-tier display (inventory equipment block, inventory item lines, examine header) goes through `get_display_name_with_tier()` from `item_utils.py` (see Section 4.6). Since v20 brief 3 (#48), rarity is no longer prepended by the call sites — every item line composes through `compose_item_line()`, which puts rarity in the trailing `[<Rarity>, Bound|Unbound]` flag block (Sections 4.6 and 4.14; bind word renamed in v21 brief 1, #60); the old `[bound]`/`[drop]` inventory tags are absorbed into that block.

Carried over from v16 unchanged: character-less connections get a structured redirect; `disconnect()` guards against `self.character is None`; profile joins removed.

### 4.4 `effect_utils.py`

**The clause contract (v23.3 brief 1, #149).** `_apply_instant_component` returns structured clause data — a `(clause, annotation)` pair per component (`('feel your body recover', '(+25 Vitality)')`; `restore_longevity` → `feel your stamina return`, `restore_acuity` → `feel your mind steady`, `durability_restore` → the v23.3 fizz placeholder `watch the repair kit fizz to no useful effect`, superseded by the live application in v24.12 brief 1 — see the field-repair paragraph below; component types with nothing to say return `None`) — and `apply_effect_definition` returns the list of these pairs (instant components only; timed components still produce no entries, and the reapplication early-return still returns an empty list). Sentence composition lives beside it in two forms: **`compose_standalone_sentence(pair)`** — `You {clause}.` with the annotation appended after the period — reconstructs the full sentence for callers outside `use` (the NPC path, Section 4.5: `apply_npc_effects` recomposes via this form so its returned strings keep the pre-contract shape, pinned byte-identical for the representative `restore_vitality` component); **`compose_use_sentence(subject, pairs)`** — `You use {subject} and {clause1} and {clause2}. {ann1} {ann2}`, clauses joined with ` and ` in component order, single period, non-empty annotations space-joined after it, an empty pair list yielding the plain transactional sentence — builds `cmd_use`'s merged line (Section 4.3). The longevity/acuity/repair-kit wordings are re-authored by the ruled clause table (`Your stamina is restored.` → `You feel your stamina return.`, `Your mind steadies.` → `You feel your mind steady.`, `The repair kit fizzes but does nothing useful yet.` → `You watch the repair kit fizz to no useful effect.`); no current NPC seed effect carries those components, so no live NPC output changes.

**The Draught Law (v24.0 brief 1, #139).** `percent_heal_amount(fraction, vitality_max)` is the law's single arithmetic home: `max(VITALITY_PERCENT_HEAL_FLOOR, math.ceil(fraction × vitality_max))` — the fraction is of MAX, never of deficit; `math.ceil`, never bare `round()` (banker's rounding, the #105 lesson); the floor constant (25, in `models.py` beside the regen constants) keeps every character at least as well off as the old flat `20 + 5×Mk` and disengages once `vitality_max` exceeds 125 at Mk 1. For `restore_vitality_percent` components, `computed_magnitude(mk_tier)` yields the fraction itself (`0.15 + 0.05 × Mk` for the seeded draught — Mk 1 = 20%, five drinks zero-to-full at every level; the Mk axis buys rounds, not raw HP). `_apply_instant_component`'s percent branch computes the heal via the helper and issues the same atomic clamped UPDATE as the flat branch — `vitality_max` is safely read from the caller's character object because only `vitality_current` is tick-contested — returning the standard clause pair with the computed heal in the annotation. The #151 aggregate path (Section 4.3) imports the same helper for its per-item deficit math; the flat `restore_vitality` type remains in the vocabulary (additive-only law) — only the seeded draught's component converted.

**Field repair (v24.12 brief 1, #134).** The `durability_restore` branch is live — the fizz placeholder is retired. Target selection is automatic, most-damaged-first over everything the character owns, carried and equipped alike: `ItemInstance.objects.filter(owner=target, definition__takes_durability_loss=True, durability_current__gt=0.0, durability_current__lt=100.0).order_by('durability_current', 'pk').first()` — the v22 `get_damaged_items` query plus the broken-exclusion (`__gt=0.0`): broken gear is ineligible here, the vendor's very-difficult roll owns it, and the kit can never target itself (kits are `takes_durability_loss=False`). A kit **always succeeds** — no roll; the roll stays vendor repair's mechanic. The application is one atomic clamped UPDATE in the #52 house style (`Least(F('durability_current') + magnitude, Value(100.0))`) after a pre-read of `durability_current`; the returned pair is `patch up {item_ref(item)}` (the definite article form, exactly as repair's own mend lines render names) with annotation `(+N durability)`, `N = int(min(magnitude, 100.0 − before))` — the **actual** points applied after the clamp, never the nominal magnitude. `magnitude` arrives as `computed_magnitude(mk_tier)` = 15 + 10×Mk (Mk 1 +25, Mk 2 +35) from the unchanged pipeline. A no-eligible-target call returns `None` — defense, not flow: the use-pipeline gate (Section 4.3) refuses before application and consumption in every such case.

**Bar restores are atomic database operations (v21 brief 3, #52) — a standing invariant.** `_apply_instant_component`'s `restore_vitality`/`restore_longevity`/`restore_acuity` branches issue a single atomic UPDATE on the character row (`F('<bar>_current') + magnitude` clamped with `Least` to the bar's max; acuity uses a conditional toward-baseline expression capped at `F('acuity_baseline')`, clamped to the 0.1–1.9 scale, and `Round`ed via a `Cast` to numeric because Postgres has no `round(double, int)`). They never compute on the caller's cached object — the consumer's cached character can be arbitrarily stale against the tick engine's per-round damage writes, and stale-add-then-save silently resurrected persisted damage (the #52 lost-update race, exploitable as a free full heal). **The invariant for all future code: the consumer NEVER read-modify-writes character bar fields; bar mutations from the consumer/effect layer are atomic queryset updates, the display path re-fetches before rendering (`get_character_fresh()` reassigns `self.character`), and the tick engine remains the only other bar writer.** `apply_stat_effect` writes `stat_*` fields, not bars. **v22 brief 5 (#109/#110) extends the invariant to the max-changing mutations:** `cmd_spend`'s v21-era `recalculate_bars` refill — audited then as "deliberately unconverted" — is gone; equip, unequip, and spend now all write bars through the atomic `bar_rescale_updates` path (Section 4.5), which preserves fill fraction instead of refilling. Character creation and level-up remain the only `recalculate_bars` object-mutation callers (level-up's full refill is explicitly preserved behavior). Everything else *(unchanged from v15)*.

### 4.5 Combat utilities (`combat_utils.py`)

**`resolve_hit` is a contested d20 to-hit with an independent critical roll (rewritten v19 brief 5).** The v18 formula (`d100 + attacker_dex` against absolute bands anchored at the defender's DEX) was replaced because the d100's range dwarfed every stat in the game — in v18 play-testing a DEX-18 player vs. a DEX-5 bear produced criticals on ~84% of player swings and ~58% of bear swings, with miss and graze mathematically unreachable for the player. The new formula: `total = d20 + attacker_dex`; static `defense = TO_HIT_DEFENSE_BASE + target_dodge`. `total >= defense` is a success (hit or critical); on the gap `defense - total`, `1..GRAZE_WINDOW` short of defense is a graze, further short is a miss. Critical is then a **separate, independent roll** on any success: `crit_chance = clamp(CRIT_BASE + CRIT_PER_DEX_ADVANTAGE × (attacker_dex − target_dodge), CRIT_BASE, CRIT_CAP)`. Five module-level named constants (never inline literals, so post-play tuning is a one-line change) — `TO_HIT_DEFENSE_BASE = 10`, `GRAZE_WINDOW = 3`, `CRIT_BASE = 0.05`, `CRIT_PER_DEX_ADVANTAGE = 0.01`, `CRIT_CAP = 0.25` — are **tunable starting values by design ruling**, not final balance. Signature and return contract are unchanged (`resolve_hit(attacker_dex, target_dodge) → 'miss'|'graze'|'hit'|'critical'`); neither call site (`run_tick_engine.py`'s player-attack and NPC-attack rounds) changed. `calculate_damage` and its multipliers (graze 0.5×, hit 1.0×, critical 1.5×) are untouched. At equal DEX: ~55% hit-or-crit, 15% graze, 30% miss, crit ≈5% of successes. Large attacker advantage: always hits (deliberate — outleveled content is trivially hittable under infinite level scaling), crit bounded at 25% forever. Attacker at a disadvantage: crit stays at the 5% floor, never below.

**`get_unarmed_message` gained `attacker_name` and `fallback_slug` parameters (v19 brief 3).** New signature: `get_unarmed_message(attacker_pool, target_name, attacker_name=None, fallback_slug='default')`. Substitution is now literal `str.replace` — `'{target}'` → `target_name`, `'{attacker}'` → `attacker_name` when provided — **not** `.format(target=…)` (see the superseded design-principles line in Section 6); stray braces left in a template are harmless, matching the `TravelMessage` precedent. `fallback_slug` names the pool used when `attacker_pool` is `None` or has no messages: the player attack call site (unarmed player swings) leaves it at the default `'default'`; the NPC attack call site in the tick engine passes `attacker_name=npc.definition.name, fallback_slug='npc-default'` so an NPC's unarmed attack can never fall back to the player-perspective `default` pool (whose templates are second-person, `"You swing at {target}"` — narrating an NPC's hit in the player's own voice was the bug this brief fixes). If even the fallback pool is missing, the ultimate hardcoded fallback also branches on `attacker_name`: `f"The {attacker_name} strikes {target_name}."` for NPC callers, `f"You strike {target_name}."` for the player.

One change in v18 (brief 3): **`xp_for_kill(npc_instance, character)` now reduces XP for outleveled kills.** Base XP is unchanged (`int(mk_tier × 10 × scaling_factor)`) and is paid in full while the character is within the NPC's Mk level band — band top = `mk_tier × 10`. Beyond the band top the multiplier drops 20% per level over, floored at 10% of base, and the awarded amount is additionally floored at 1 XP (`max(1, …)` — matters for low-base NPCs where 10% truncates to 0). Outleveled content always pays something. Worked values for a Mk 1 NPC at `scaling_factor=1.0` (base 10): levels 1–10 → 10 XP; 11 → 8; 12 → 6; 13 → 4; 14 → 2; 15+ → 1. The product is rounded at the 9th decimal before truncation to correct binary-float error (0.20 × 3 → 0.6000…01) so results match the decimal formula. The signature already took both arguments, so no call sites changed. **The combat-tier XP multiplier (v24.15 brief 1, #26).** The base becomes `int(mk_tier × 10 × scaling_factor) × NPC_TIER_XP_MULT[combat_tier]` — the authored doubling ladder (`{'normal': 1, 'elite': 2, 'champion': 4, 'boss': 8, 'world_boss': 16}`, integers, defined beside `NPC_TIER_OFFSET`; missing/unknown tier pays ×1 via `.get(…, 1)`). The multiplier composes **before** the outleveled decay: the −20%/level multiplier and its 10% floor operate on the tier-multiplied base, and the absolute `max(1, …)` floor closes as ever. Escorts and adds pay their own tier — no shared-encounter logic; the call site already has `npc.definition` loaded, so no new queries. Worked Matron sentinel (boss, Mk 1, `scaling_factor` 3.0, in-band): 30 → 240; outleveled at character level 13: 240 × 0.4 = 96. Everything else *(unchanged from v15)*.

`recalculate_bars(character)` gained a second call site in v16: the character creator calls it on the unsaved `Character` to compute starting bars from the level-1 stats before the first save (see Section 4.11).

**`(spawned_at, pk)` is the single canonical NPC order (v21 brief 3, #64).** Every room/session NPC queryset carries `order_by('spawned_at', 'pk')` — `get_live_npcs_in_room`, `get_aggro_npcs_in_room`, the consumer's `_fight_enemies`, and the tick engine's session loads — so the Who's-here listing, the resolver's default pick (whose `_age_key` already sorted this way), the `N.noun` index, and message ordinals all derive from one order by construction: bare `k bear` engages the FIRST bear and says so. Ordinals render only while same-name duplicates are present in the encounter (the `npc_display_name` fallback keeps singles byte-identical to their pre-#64 strings); the Who's-here listing stays bare names — order is its contract, not labels. Engagement snarl lines, round messages, and kill broadcasts are all ordinal-aware.

**`npc_display_name` and `ORDINALS` relocated here from `consumers.py` (v19 brief 6).** See Section 4.3 for the full description (v20 brief 5 rebuilt it over the `npc_display` composer, Section 4.16); the move makes the ordinal display helper importable from the tick engine (`management/commands/run_tick_engine.py`) without pulling in the whole `consumers.py` module (Channels/redis imports, WebSocket-only helpers), since the engine's focus-death shift announcement (Section 4.9) now needs the identical ordinal logic the consumer already used for engagement/refocus messages. `consumers.py` imports it back: `from .combat_utils import npc_display_name`.

**`get_npc_stats` rewritten: NPC contest stats scale additively, not multiplicatively (v19 brief 7).** The v18 formula (`int(base_stat × d.scaling_factor × npc_instance.mk_tier)`) grew every contest stat exponentially against players' flat `+5/level` growth; since a d20 contest can bridge at most ~20 points, the gap became unreachable past the early game (26 of Z01's 42 combat NPCs sat at 0% hit chance for an at-level player — every giant, every elder, and all six bosses). Two new module-level helpers and four new named constants:

```python
NPC_CONTEST_BASE = 18        # matches a level-1 player's primary stat
NPC_CONTEST_STEP = 2.5       # per level, matches player primary-stat growth
NPC_TIER_OFFSET = {'normal': 0, 'elite': 2, 'boss': 2}   # blessed: 55% / 45% / 45% at-level hit
MK_LEVEL_SPAN = 10           # each Mk tier spans 10 levels (matches the item system's bands)

def npc_level(npc_instance):
    return npc_instance.definition.scaling_factor + MK_LEVEL_SPAN * (npc_instance.mk_tier - 1)

def get_npc_stats(npc_instance):
    d = npc_instance.definition
    L = npc_level(npc_instance)
    curve = NPC_CONTEST_BASE + math.floor(NPC_CONTEST_STEP * (L - 1))
    offset = NPC_TIER_OFFSET.get(d.combat_tier, 0)
    growth = round(NPC_CONTEST_STEP * (L - 1))
    return {
        'dex': curve + offset, 'str': d.base_str + growth,
        'per': d.base_per + growth, 'int': d.base_int + growth,
        'vitality': npc_instance.vitality_current,
    }
```

`scaling_factor` is now read as the NPC's **within-band level** (1–10, matching its authored difficulty placement); Mk tier lifts that level by whole `MK_LEVEL_SPAN`-sized bands, so a Mk 2 instance of a `scaling_factor=2.0` definition is level 12. DEX is derived purely from `curve + offset` — it *is* the difficulty dial, uniform across every NPC at a given level and tier regardless of authored base, which is what makes the blessed at-level hit targets exact and constant across every level and Mk tier. **The curve's growth term floors (v24.17 brief 1, #105):** `curve = NPC_CONTEST_BASE + math.floor(NPC_CONTEST_STEP × (L − 1))` — the floor mirrors the reference player's own floor-share DEX accrual (`18 + floor(2.5 × (L − 1))`, 5 points per level split across two primaries), so NPC DEX equals the attainable at-level primary at every level of every band and the exactness claim above holds without caveat. The original `round()` was banker's rounding, which sent the `.5`-boundary growth values UP at two levels per band (L4/L8 — Mk-band lifts add an integer 25, so the pattern repeated per band), overstating NPC DEX by 1 pip there: −5% hit against the reference build (survey finding G5, #89; elite L4 DEX 28 → 27, L8 38 → 37; aligned levels byte-identical). **The offsets flattened in v21 brief 2 (#101): 0/+3/+6 became 0/+2/+2, moving the blessed targets from 55%/40%/25% to 55%/45%/45%.** Boss and elite now share the +2 dodge tier — boss identity is carried by HP, damage, and escorts rather than the miss rate, because the #89 kill-feasibility survey showed the old +6 offset made every delve boss mathematically unwinnable for builds that lag the DEX curve, while HP walls did the rest. The retune's full derivation lives in `docs/shyland/Shyland_V21_B3_Retune_Proposal.md` and the #89 survey; the per-boss numbers are in Section 4.8. STR/PER/INT instead add `growth` to the NPC's authored `base_*` value, so species identity survives (a rabbit never punches like a buffalo) while damage stays proportionate to level. **`STR/PER/INT growth is `base + round(NPC_CONTEST_STEP × (level − 1))` — growth is uniform per level by ruling (Brief 7 Amendment 1).`** (The brief's original worked table computed a few values by rounding the summed stat rather than the growth term, producing ±1 artifacts at the five levels where `NPC_CONTEST_STEP × (level − 1)` lands on an exact `.5` boundary — levels 2, 4, 6, 8, 10; the design chat ratified the formula as written and corrected the table.) The v24.17 DEX floor deliberately does not touch this line — the ratified banker's ±1 artifacts are damage-side noise with no hit% effect. **`base_dex` is no longer read by combat resolution** — the field remains on `NpcDefinition` for now but no longer drives to-hit, dodge, or crit math; `get_npc_stats()['dex']` is the only source of truth for an NPC's effective DEX.

**`npc_max_vitality` — NPC HP scales with Mk tier at spawn (v24.13 brief 1, #104).** Contest stats band-lifted (above) but HP never did — `base_vitality` went into new instances flat, so any Mk 2 spawn would have paired level-12+ contest stats with Mk 1 HP. `npc_max_vitality(npc_definition, mk_tier)` returns the spawn-time HP: `base_vitality × (1 + NPC_HP_BAND_LIFT × (mk_tier − 1))`, `NPC_HP_BAND_LIFT = 0.75` (module-level constant beside the contest constants), **rounded half-up via `int(lifted + 0.5)` — never built-in `round()`**, whose banker's rounding sends `.5` cases to even (`round(262.5) == 262`; the #105 parity trap — Silk Matron 150 at Mk 2 is 263 by ruling, and the case is test-pinned as a sentinel). The lift is linear per band and uniform across all combat tiers (normals, elites, bosses — deliberately not exponential; doctrine: at-level time-to-kill is band-invariant, so `base_vitality` remains the authored *within-band* value carrying species identity and the #101 boss-ladder tuning). Mk 1 multiplies by exactly 1 — the identity invariant is test-pinned across the full seeded range. Called from exactly one place: the tick engine's respawn sweep (Section 4.9), the codebase's only non-test `NpcInstance` creation site; the value persists to `vitality_current`/`vitality_max` at creation and nothing scales at read time.

**`acuity_damage_modifier` replaces the inline Acuity derivation: band-relative, not absolute (v19 brief 7).** The v18 damage path computed `round(max(0.1, min(1.9, character.acuity_current)), 1)` — compared against a hard-coded 1.0 neutral point regardless of Origin, and the 1-decimal rounding silently zeroed out several Origins' baselines (Feral's 0.95 rounded to 0.9, a permanent hidden penalty never intended). The new function is band-relative and deviation-based, with no rounding anywhere in the path:

```python
def acuity_damage_modifier(character):
    a = min(1.9, max(0.1, character.acuity_current))
```
