- **Legacy absorption.** Before any item is processed, the legacy generic `copper-ring` definition is renamed in place (`.filter(slug='copper-ring').update(...)`) to `copper-ring-of-wisdom` / "Copper Ring of Wisdom". The old dictionary was removed from the seed list, so the generic ring can never be recreated and the rename is safe to re-run on both fresh and existing databases.
- **Gear** (weapons, armor, shield) stays on `get_or_create(slug=...)` — created once, never overwritten, so admin-tuned balance survives re-seeding.
- **The 12 copper accessories** use `update_or_create(slug=...)` uniformly, so the absorbed legacy ring is normalized to its authored shape on existing databases and re-seeding stays idempotent.

**Full post-brief ItemDefinition inventory (33 rows):**

| Group | Definitions |
|-------|-------------|
| Leather armor set (6 new + adopted vest) | Leather Cap (HEAD), Leather Shoulders (SHOULDERS), Leather Gloves (HANDS), Leather Belt (WAIST), Leather Leggings (LEGS), Leather Boots (FEET); the pre-existing Leather Vest (CHEST) is adopted into the set unchanged |
| Shield (new) | Wooden Shield (armor-typed, OFF_HAND, one-handed) |
| Weapons (4 new) | Iron Mace (MAIN_HAND, 1H), Broadsword (MAIN_HAND, 2H), Battle Axe (MAIN_HAND, 2H), Hunting Bow (RANGED, **2H** — all bows are two-handed for now) |
| Copper accessories (11 new + 1 absorbed) | Copper Ring of Strength / Dexterity / Endurance / Intelligence / Wisdom / Perception (RING) and Copper Amulet of the same six stats (NECK). Copper Ring of Wisdom is the absorbed legacy `copper-ring`. All twelve: `suppress_mk_suffix=True`, scaling 2.0/0.8, no durability, primary = suffix stat at base 2.0 / factor 0.8, secondary pool = the two adjacent stats at 0.5/0.2 (str→dex+end, dex→str+per, end→str+wis, int→wis+dex, wis→int+end, per→dex+int) |
| Pre-existing, unchanged | Iron Sword, Combat Knife, Pulse Pistol, Apprentice Staff, Leather Vest, Ballistic Jacket, Satchel, Healing Draught, Focus Tonic, Repair Kit |

Net effect on an existing v17 database: 22 new rows plus the in-place rename (11 → 33). On a fresh database the full seed creates all 33 directly. No proper nouns in any new item name; no new technology weapons (the Pulse Pistol is untouched and receives no new associations). This brief seeds **no zone content** — no rooms, NPCs, or drop tables.

Changed in v17: the world-seed portion of the command was rewritten for the Infinity City map. `handle()` first deletes the placeholder content (the five Fracture Point plaza rooms by name, the `goblin-scout`/`training-dummy`/`fracture-wraith` NPC definitions, and the `the-fracture-point-plaza` area — the old `_seed_npcs` method that created them is gone), then builds The Convergence: the zone, 4 areas, 54 rooms keyed on `(zone, coord_x, coord_y, coord_z)` via `update_or_create`, exits wired in a second pass (every room's six exit FKs are assigned explicitly, absent ones to `NULL`, so re-runs fully normalize), 9 unique non-combat NPC definitions placed via `RoomSpawn`, and character relocation (characters with no `current_room`, or one outside The Convergence, move to Heart of the Convergence at (0,0,0); since v24.26 there is no home-field backfill — a null `attuned_node` *is* the design's default home). The command ends with a built-in verification pass (room/area/NPC/spawn counts, ring-loop and path-connectivity walks, flags) that raises `CommandError` on any failure. The command is idempotent. One deliberate deviation from the v17 content brief: the brief assigned ring room R16 the same coordinates as the Basalt Way's last room; R16 sits at (1,−5,0) instead. NPC stats and drop/respawn numbers follow the balance-data convention below (create-only); NPC name, description, genre tag, and behavior flags are refreshed on every run.

Changed in v16: `_seed_origins` and `_seed_archetypes` now use **`update_or_create`** instead of `get_or_create`, keyed on `slug`. Re-running the seed command therefore applies content changes to already-existing rows instead of silently skipping them (their stdout lines now report `"created"`/`"updated"` accordingly). This mattered for v16 because all seven Origins and seven Archetypes already existed from prior seeding.

**Balance data was create-only through v18 — superseded by v19 brief 8.** Historical note, kept for context on the paragraphs above: through v18, the `defaults` dict (applied on every run) carried only content fields — `name`, `description`, the attire phrase — while balance values (`Origin.acuity_baseline`/`acuity_band_low`/`acuity_band_high`, `Archetype.primary_stat_1`/`primary_stat_2`, and every `NpcDefinition` stat/drop field not explicitly back-filled) went in Django 5's create-only `create_defaults` bucket, so re-running `seed_world` refreshed descriptions but never reverted admin-tuned numbers. **This convention is abolished as of v19 brief 8** — see the top of this section for the replacement (enforce-exact reconciliation). There is no more create-only bucket anywhere in `seed_world.py`; every field the seed authors, balance included, is enforced on every run.

Both methods seed real content:

- **Origins** — each of the seven (`highborn`, `feral`, `streetborn`, `irradiated`, `undying`, `machinekind`, `voidtouched`) has a non-empty `description` (flavor text tying the Origin's genre to its Acuity profile) and a non-empty `attire_material` phrase (e.g. Voidtouched: `'shifting, void-dark cloth that seems to drink the light'`). The `acuity_baseline` / `acuity_band_low` / `acuity_band_high` values are unchanged from v15.
- **Archetypes** — each of the seven (`blade`, `bulwark`, `shade`, `conduit`, `warden`, `gunner`, `machinist`) has a non-empty `description` (role summary referencing its two primary stats) and a non-empty `attire_silhouette` phrase (e.g. Machinist: `'a utility vest lined with tool loops'`). The `primary_stat_1` / `primary_stat_2` values are unchanged from v15.

All other seed methods (`_seed_unarmed_pools`, `_seed_effects`, zone/room/area creation) *(unchanged from v17, except: v24.0 brief 1 converts the Healing Draught's order-0 `EffectComponent` in `_seed_effects()` to `restore_vitality_percent / magnitude_base 0.15 / magnitude_scaling 0.05` — a reconcile-in-place update, 0 deletions, 1 updated row; durations stay 0.0, the Focus Tonic and every other effect untouched)*.

### 4.9 Tick Engine (`management/commands/run_tick_engine.py`)

**Combat-session lifecycle: the kill path is M2M-wide, and the loop head self-heals (v24.19 brief 1, #218).** `CombatSession.npcs` is M2M — one `NpcInstance` can belong to multiple players' sessions — and until v24.19 the kill block removed a dead NPC from the killer's session only, leaving every other holder a zombie: dead row attached, last-kill deactivation unreachable, members stuck behind the in-combat gate, and unreapable because the tick refresh preceded any liveness check. Two mechanisms now govern the lifecycle:

- **M2M-wide kill removal.** In the kill block, immediately after the killer's-session removal (`session.npcs.remove(npc)`): the dead NPC's other active sessions (`npc.combat_sessions.filter(is_active=True).exclude(pk=session.pk)`) each drop the row. One left with **no living NPCs** closes on the standard end pattern in the same round — `is_active=False`, `focus_npc=None`, `release_session_npcs`, `"Combat has ended."` (`reward`) and a fresh status to each member via the round's pk-addressed `messages`/`statuses` flush (deliverable to other-session members because the flush is pk-addressed, not session-bound), and its fight-clear payload after the round (closed sessions accumulate in `ended_sessions`, threaded out of `execute_actions` as a fourth return element; `build_fight_payloads` on an inactive session already returns the clearing payload). One that **retains living NPCs** but had the dead NPC as `focus_npc` reassigns focus to its next living NPC (canonical `(spawned_at, pk)` order) with the standard `"You turn your attacks on {name}."` announcement, the name ordinal-composed via `npc_display_name` against that session's own remaining live NPCs — focus changes are never silent. The killer's own session keeps the pre-existing flow (the focus-death shift and session-end clearing paragraphs below) untouched, and no room broadcast changes — the existing kill announcement already informs bystanders.
- **Loop-head self-heal, before the tick update.** The active-sessions loop opens each iteration with a fresh DB read (`read_session_state`: current `is_active` plus living-NPC existence — never the prefetched cache, which is stale for any session the kill path closed earlier in the same tick's loop). A session no longer active in the DB is skipped silently (already closed and messaged this tick). A session still active with **zero living NPCs** — dead rows attached or no rows at all — closes immediately on the same standard pattern (`self_heal_close` + `"Combat has ended."`/status/fight-clear per member, logged as `Combat session {pk} closed (self-heal: no living NPCs)`), and `update_session_tick` never runs for it. Because the check precedes the tick update, a zombie never refreshes its own `last_tick_at` — the stale sweep (`get_stale_sessions`/`close_session`, untouched) is restored as the outer backstop by construction, and the stuck window for any leak path, present or future, caps at one engine tick.

`release_session_npcs` is unchanged by ruling (#218 ruling 4): its `is_alive=True` filter and other-active-session guard mean dead NPCs are never restored by any close path and a living shared NPC another player is still fighting never snaps to full. Test-pinned in `tests/test_zombie_sessions.py`.

**Zero-character session close (v24.27 brief 1, #234 — verified pre-existing, test-pinned).** Deleting an in-combat character (admin hard delete, the only deletion surface) empties the session's character side via the M2M through-row cascade. The close needs no dedicated guard: at the next round boundary the participants load returns an empty character list and the combat-v1 `if not characters or not npcs` branch closes the session through `close_session` — `is_active=False` plus `release_session_npcs` (#25: NPCs released and restored per the disengagement doctrine; there are no members left to message) — within one combat round of the deletion. The v24.19 loop-head self-heal deliberately checks the NPC side only; the character side's trigger is the participants load itself, honoring the #107 per-tick query discipline (no added per-session count query). Pinned in `tests/test_v24_27_brief1.py`.

**Respawn-sweep instance HP comes from `npc_max_vitality`, not flat `base_vitality` (v24.13 brief 1, #104).** The sweep's `NpcInstance.objects.create(...)` — the codebase's only non-test creation site — computes `hp = npc_max_vitality(spawn.npc_definition, spawn.mk_tier)` once per spawn row (imported from `combat_utils` alongside the sweep's other local imports) and writes it to both `vitality_current` and `vitality_max`. Mk 1 spawns are byte-identical to the old flat assignment (the multiplier is exactly 1); Mk 2+ spawns carry the per-band linear lift so the first Mk 2 content authored (a future zones major) arrives with HP matching its already-lifted contest stats. Full formula, rounding law, and doctrine in Section 4.5.

**The proportional regen law (v24.3 brief 1, #165).** Phase 4 ("Passive bar regeneration", the tail of `process_effects`) regens out-of-combat bars at `bar_max / CONSTANT` points per second, so a full refill from zero takes the constant's number of seconds at every level — `VITALITY_REGEN_SECS` (120), `LONGEVITY_REGEN_SECS` (3600), both in Section 4.1's constants block, whose comments this brief makes true. Vitality uses the **per-tick form**: `heal = ceil(vitality_max / VITALITY_REGEN_SECS)`, clamped by the existing `min()` — the old deficit-proportional `ceil(deficit / CONSTANT)` was exponential decay with a 1 HP/s tail (~5.6 minutes at a 718 bar, the last ~120 HP crawling). Longevity uses the **interval form**: exactly one point on ticks where `tick_number % interval == 0`, `interval = ceil(LONGEVITY_REGEN_SECS / longevity_max)` (`tick_number` is `process_effects`' own parameter) — the vitality form cannot express the law here because a sub-constant max makes the per-tick ceil always 1 (**the ceil trap**: the old formula degenerated to a flat 1 point/second, refilling a 274 bar in ~4.6 minutes and silently falsifying the one-hour design). An engine restart resets `tick_number` and at worst delays one longevity point by up to one interval — accepted by ruling; no persistent state, no model changes. Unchanged: `get_regen_candidates` (not dying, below max on either bar, no active combat session), the combat and dying exclusions, the `update_fields` save, regen silence (no output message ever — the status-pane push on changed fields is the only signal), and the changed-fields gate (a longevity-only candidate on a non-interval tick changes nothing → no save, no push). Known boundary, not scope: if `longevity_max` ever exceeded `LONGEVITY_REGEN_SECS` the interval form saturates at 1 point/tick, slower than the law — file a new issue if play approaches it. Tests: `tests/test_v243_regen.py` (engine-harness style) pins the rate, the clamp, the 120-tick law, the interval, the exclusions, and the silence.

**Acuity shift semantics and the effect-tick announcement doctrine (v23 brief 3, #133).** `shift_acuity_high` stops at the character's own `acuity_band_high`: below the edge it climbs with the existing round-to-0.1 display behavior; the arrival tick stores `band_high` EXACTLY (bands are 2-decimal) so `acuity_damage_modifier` (1.0 at equality — in-band) and the stats-pane band gauge agree; at or above the edge it is a silent no-op. Climb-and-sustain: the effect runs its full duration and holds its peak because Phase 2 drift stays paused by the has-shift exclusion (the `acuity_shift_types` filter). **Drift's second pause condition — active combat (v24.18 brief 1, #142):** Phase 2's candidate queryset (`get_characters_needing_drift`) excludes any character with an active `CombatSession` (`.exclude(combat_sessions__is_active=True)`) — the same combat-membership predicate Phase 4 regen uses, shared by ruling and never duplicated: one definition of "in combat" for both phases, so nothing passively recovers in combat on any of the three bars. The two pause conditions are independent — a running shift pauses drift regardless of combat state and vice versa. When the character's last active session ends, drift resumes at `ACUITY_DRIFT_RATE` per tick — no burst correction, no catch-up. Test-pinned in `tests/test_acuity_drift_pause.py` (drift law, snap rule, pause, ordinary resume, shift independence). `shift_acuity_low` keeps the hard `ACUITY_FLOOR` boundary — no band stop, ruled: dragging below band into fizzle territory is a hostile effect's entire point. **The directional invariant:** shift effects are one-way — the high shift never lowers acuity, the low shift never raises it. **The announcement doctrine (promoted standing doctrine, in force on both shift branches):** effect ticks never announce no-ops; boundary arrival gets exactly one terminal line — `Your focus settles at its keenest.` (band arrival) / `Your focus frays to nothing.` (floor arrival) — and holding is silent; change ticks keep their value-suffixed lines. The not-yet-migrated branches (`hot_acuity`'s at-baseline no-op loop, `dot_acuity` and the flat-floor resource DoTs at their floors) are filed as #145. **Level-up messaging (v23 brief 3, #141):** the kill-branch level-up emits TWO `reward` messages, each with its own envelope — the level/bars line, then `You have N unspent stat point(s). Type 'spend' to allocate them.` with the post-increment (accumulated) count — no `***` prefix on either; multi-level rounds emit two per level.

**Corpse decay now destroys the corpse's contents with it (v23 brief 2, #137).** `delete_corpse`'s existing `Corpse.objects.filter(pk=pk).delete()` needed no code change: with `ItemInstance.corpse` now `on_delete=CASCADE` (Section 4.1), Django's collector cascades the contents on the ORM delete — decayed loot vanishes with the corpse, no orphans. Before the migration, the decay sweep's `SET_NULL` stranded unlooted contents as location-less rows; the accumulated orphans are removed one-time by `purge_orphaned_items` (Section 4.6).

**Respawned aggressive NPCs engage on the spawn tick (v21 brief 3, #17).** The respawn sweep (batched to four fixed queries per tick since #107) now ends with an engagement stage that runs only when a respawn actually fired — zero recurring per-tick queries. When an `is_aggressive`+`attackable` NPC (re)spawns into a room containing living, connected (Redis presence key), non-dying characters, it engages that tick exactly as if the player had walked in: each present character's active combat session gains the room's unengaged aggro NPCs (or a new session is created, `first_attacker='npc'`), the engagement line broadcasts to the room (excluding dying players), and fight/status payloads follow last — the #81 ordering. Because no respawn message precedes it, the engagement line introduces the NPC (indefinite article, the #79 first-presentation context): "A giant cave spider snarls and moves to attack!". Respawns into empty rooms and passive respawns engage nothing.

Two changes in v18 (brief 3), one in brief 5, three in v19 brief 2, two in v19 brief 3, the fall/dying/expiry rework in v19 brief 4, the focus-driven targeting rework in v19 brief 6, one call-site change in v19 brief 7, three changes in v19 brief 8, and a new delivery phase in v19 brief 9 below; everything else *(unchanged from v15)*.

**NPC dialogue delivery — `process_dialogue_delivery`, a new phase run last in `process_tick` (v19 brief 9).** Each tick: fetch every `PendingDialogueResponse` with `fire_at <= now` (`select_related('npc_instance__definition', 'entry')`, ordered by `fire_at, position`), and deliver each in turn:

1. **Liveness check.** If `npc_instance.is_alive` is `False`, delete the row and continue — silent, no broadcast. This is an explicit check, not a reliance on cascade-on-death: a dead `NpcInstance` is *not* deleted at the moment of death (Section 4.9, respawn), only much later when `clear_expired_dead` clears it at `respawn_at`, so a row scheduled just before an NPC's death would otherwise still fire during that window without this guard.
2. **Response draw at fire time**, excluding the NPC's own most recent response. `self._last_dialogue_response: dict[npc_instance_pk, DialogueResponse.pk]`, a `Command.__init__`-initialized in-memory dict living only in the running tick-engine process — the same shape as `_dying_ladder_sent` (Section 4.9 below). Exclusion only applies when the entry's pool has more than one response; an engine restart just loses the memory (no consecutive-repeat guarantee across a restart, accepted — the rule is "no consecutive repeat," not "global novelty").
3. **Position-aware connective — speech entries only (v23 brief 5, #147)**: the position 1 (`second` pool) / position ≥2 (`later` pool) connective draws run only when the row's entry is in `npc_voice.SPEECH_ENTRY_TYPES` — a narration greeting must not announce an answer to a question nobody asked. `{name}` is substituted with the NPC's name via `str.replace`, broadcast as a plain `category='room'` line.
4. **The line itself, composed by `npc_voice.dialogue_line(entry_type, npc_name, text)` (v23 brief 5, #147 — THE RENDER RULE):** keyword responses are **speech** — `{npc name}: {response text}`, `category='say'`, the exact format `cmd_say` uses for a player (the v22 DD §13 form); greeting and departed entries are **narration** — authored in the third person, broadcast **verbatim, unprefixed, at `category='room'`**. The old one-size-fits-all speech composition printed a greeting's name twice (`Aldric: Aldric looks you over…`); one composer now decides, and the two speech-form greetings (VND-9, Mother Tansy) were re-authored as narration. Same governing line as Brief 4: speech gets prefixed; narration does not.
5. **Departure reaction**, only if `is_final` and the asker's current room (a fresh read, `Character.objects.filter(pk=...).values_list('current_room_id', flat=True)` — never the row's stale FK snapshot) differs from the row's `room_id`: draw one response (same recent-exclusion mechanism) from the NPC's `departed`-type entry, if authored — routed through the same `dialogue_line` composer (departed = narration; output byte-identical to the pre-#147 form). One per utterance, last speaker only.
6. **Delete the row.** An NPC that dies mid-stagger is caught by step 1 on its next due tick; a row whose entry has no authored responses yet (a keyword entry with no `DialogueResponse` rows) is silently deleted rather than broadcasting nothing forever.

**Unattackable NPCs are excluded from combat, with defense in depth (v19 brief 8).** Combat-session candidate sets are built exclusively in the consumer (`get_aggro_npcs_in_room`, `cmd_attack`'s refusal check — both Section 4.3), so an unattackable NPC should never reach a `CombatSession`'s `npcs` set in the first place. `process_combat` enforces this a second time regardless: immediately after `load_participants` loads a session's characters and NPCs, any NPC whose `definition.attackable` is `False` is dropped from the round's `npcs` list and a warning is logged (`Unattackable NPC {slug} (instance {pk}) found in CombatSession {pk}; skipping.`), before the empty-participant check that would otherwise close the session. This is belt-and-suspenders, not the primary guard — it exists so a future bug in the consumer-side exclusion degrades to "this NPC sits out the round" rather than "this NPC fights."

**Ordinal NPC names on player-facing attack lines (v19 brief 8).** The character-attacks-NPC branch of `execute_actions` now computes `display = npc_display_name(npc, live_npcs)` once, immediately after resolving the target NPC, and uses it everywhere a bare `"the {npc.definition.name}"` previously appeared in a line sent to the attacking player: the miss line, the weapon/unarmed hit-or-critical flavor (including the unarmed-message `{target}` substitution — `get_unarmed_message(pool, display)` replaces the old `f"the {npc.definition.name}"` literal), the wound-state line (`f"{display[0].upper()}{display[1:]} {health_desc}."`), and the kill line (`"You have slain {display}! (+{xp} XP)"`). This reuses the same helper and the same room-parse-order semantics `cmd_attack` and the brief-6 focus-shift line already use (Section 4.5) — a two-bear fight reads "the second black bear" on every line about that bear, and the ordinal drops the moment the other bear dies, since `display` is recomputed from `live_npcs` fresh on every action. **NPC-attacks-character lines and room broadcasts are deliberately left plain** — the lines describing an NPC's attack *on* the player, and every `room_messages` witness line (the room's kill announcement, the room's fall announcement, etc.), still use the bare `npc.definition.name`. Witnesses already parse NPCs in the room's own order; ordinals are for disambiguating which of several same-name NPCs a player is *acting on*, not for describing what's happening *to* them.

**Kill/level-up message order (v19 brief 8).** In the kill block, the player-facing `messages` list now receives the slain line (`"You have slain {display}! (+{xp} XP)"`, plus the room's kill/death-message broadcasts) immediately after XP is added to the character, **before** the level-up `while` loop runs — previously the level-up announcement(s) were appended first and the slain line after, so a levelling kill read "You have reached level N!" before "You have slain the bear!", backwards from the actual event order. The level-up loop, the post-loop status push, and the existing kill-vs.-still-fighting / focus-shift logic that follows are otherwise unchanged — this is purely a reordering of two `messages.append` calls relative to the loop, not a content change.

**Acuity call site now uses `acuity_damage_modifier` (v19 brief 7).** `execute_actions`'s character-attacks-NPC branch previously computed `acuity_mod = round(max(0.1, min(1.9, character.acuity_current)), 1)` inline; it now calls `acuity_damage_modifier(character)` (see Section 4.5). No other change to the surrounding damage calculation — `calculate_damage`'s call signature, focus gating, and hit-result multipliers are untouched.

**The fall sequence, run at both places a character's Vitality can hit zero (v19 brief 4).** `process_combat`'s NPC-attacks-character branch and `process_effects`'s `dot_vitality` component handler each run the identical sequence the instant `vitality_current <= 0`, before either function's usual per-hit messaging:

1. `vitality_current = 0`, `is_dying = True`, `dying_since = now`, saved.
2. Every active `EffectInstance` on the character, and its `component_instances`, deactivated with `removed_by='dying'` — the same bulk-update shape `execute_death` already used with `removed_by='death'`. This includes the very effect instance whose `dot_vitality` tick just caused the fall, if that's the site; a same-tick guard (`newly_dying`, a local set built while iterating `ticking`) skips any other not-yet-processed component belonging to a character who fell earlier in the same phase-1 loop, so a multi-component DoT can't tick a second component (and print a second message) on a character already falling this tick.
3. `CombatAction.objects.filter(character=character, is_processed=False).delete()` — the character's own queued/future attacks are gone. Combined with a new guard on auto-attack creation (`if not player_actions and npcs and not character.is_dying:` in `process_combat` — was unconditional), a dying character never gets a fresh attack queued in a later round either. **This is one-directional.** The pre-existing guard on the NPC-attacks-character branch (`if character.is_dying: continue`) already stopped incoming hits from mattering; this adds the other direction, so a character who falls mid-round cannot land a posthumous kill on the NPC that killed them (or one it's fighting alongside) even if their own attack action was already queued for later in the same round's `ordered_actions`. If the character's attack had already resolved *earlier* in the same round, that kill stands — the discard only reaches forward from the moment of falling.
4. Two `send_to_player` calls, in order: an `event='clear'` message with no text (see Section 4.3 for the client-side effect), then the personal fatal-blow line, `category='error'`, riding `event='dying'` (no separate send for the flag sync — Section 4.3's consumer handling is unchanged from brief 2). No hit line, kill line, or "Combat has ended." for this character is generated after this point while `is_dying` is true — the NPC-attacks-character branch's existing early guard and the new character-attacks-NPC guard together make "no combat output of any kind reaches the dying player" absolute, not just "no damage." *(Amendment 1 removed a room-title line, `category='room'`, that originally sat between the two — the room name is already shown in the client's persistent header chrome; the extra line was found redundant during brief 4 play-testing.)*
5. The third-person room announcement, `"{character.name} has fallen and is dying!"`, `category='combat'`, `exclude_pk=character.pk` — unchanged from brief 2/3, still using the same exclusion machinery brief 3 built.

The combat-branch version of this appends to the same `messages`/`room_messages` lists `execute_actions` already threads out to the per-round flush loop (the clear/title/fatal-blow lines are just three more tuples with a `None` category-and-`'clear'`-event, `'room'`-category, and `'error'`-category-plus-`'dying'`-event entry respectively); the effects-branch version calls `send_to_player`/`broadcast_to_room` directly, since `process_effects` is already an async method rather than a synchronous `@database_sync_to_async` closure.

**The dying lore ladder — `process_dying_ladder`, called once per tick after `process_effects`.** A module-level `DYING_LADDER` list of nine `(elapsed_seconds, line)` pairs (5, 10, 15, 20, 25, 26, 27, 28, 29 — dense in the final five seconds) — no numerals or time units appear in any line; the escalation itself carries the urgency. Bookkeeping is a single in-memory `Command.__init__`-initialized dict, `self._dying_ladder_sent: dict[character_pk, set[int]]`, living only in the running tick-engine process:

- Each tick, query `Character.objects.filter(is_dying=True)`. For each: `elapsed = int((now - dying_since).total_seconds())`. **First sight of a pk this process** (no dict entry yet) seeds its sent-set with every threshold `<= elapsed` already — this covers both an engine restart mid-window (the dict is empty again, but the DB's `dying_since` still says how far in the character actually is) and a character who just fell this same tick (`elapsed` is ~0, so the seed is the empty set and nothing is skipped). Every tick after that, any threshold `<= elapsed` not yet in the sent-set fires once and is added — a tick that runs late (engine hiccup, not a normal case) emits the line it missed rather than silently skipping it, and no line is ever sent twice.
- After the per-character loop, any pk in the bookkeeping dict that the current `is_dying=True` query no longer returned is dropped. This is the entire "stop the ladder" mechanism for both revival and death — neither path touches `self._dying_ladder_sent` directly; clearing `is_dying` (revival, `cmd_use`; death, `execute_death`) is sufficient, because the next tick's query simply won't include that pk anymore and the sweep removes its entry.

**Death declaration before the existing respawn flow (v19 brief 4).** The dying-expiry loop in `process_combat` (`get_expired_dying` → `execute_death` → respawn message) now opens each iteration with `await self.send_to_player(character.pk, "The darkness takes you.", 'error', None)`, sent before the unchanged `"You have died and awakened at …"` message/status/`event='respawn'` flow from brief 2. `execute_death` itself was untouched by that brief — penalties, home respawn, and full-bar restoration all unchanged; it only added the lore line in front of them. (Since v24.26 #38, `execute_death` resolves the destination through `resolve_home_room` — the attuned node's room, the founding Heart node when no bond exists — and `get_expired_dying` select_relates the `attuned_node__room__zone`/`__area` path; the message shape is unchanged, with the fallback string now `'your home'`.)

**`broadcast_to_room` gained an `exclude_pk` parameter (v19 brief 3).** `broadcast_to_room(self, room_id, text, category='room', exclude_pk=None)` — the `group_send` payload to `room_{room_id}` now always carries `'exclude_pk': exclude_pk`. The engine has no per-connection channel name to exclude by (unlike the consumer's own `broadcast_to_room_exclude`, which excludes `self.channel_name`), so this excludes by character pk instead; the consumer's `room_message` handler drops the message for the one connection whose `character_pk` matches (see Section 4.3). `room_messages` tuples threaded through `execute_actions` gained a fourth element, `exclude_pk`, dispatched as `await self.broadcast_to_room(room_id, text, category, exclude_pk=exclude_pk)`. Two broadcasts pass their subject's pk, since that character already received an equivalent personal line and would otherwise see it twice: the kill announcement (`"{character.name} has slain the {npc.definition.name}!"`, `exclude_pk=character.pk` — the killer already got `"You have slain …"`) and the fall announcement (`"{character.name} has fallen and is dying!"`, `exclude_pk=character.pk` — the faller already got the personal brink-of-death line). The NPC `death_message` broadcast is **not** excluded from anyone, including the killer — it is world flavor addressed to the whole room, unchanged from the v18 brief 3 design (below). The one other `broadcast_to_room` caller (corpse decay) passes no `exclude_pk` and is unaffected.

**NPC attacks use their own flavor pool, never the player's (v19 brief 3).** The NPC-attacks-character branch of `execute_actions` calls `get_unarmed_message(npc_pool, character.name, attacker_name=npc.definition.name, fallback_slug='npc-default')` (was: `get_unarmed_message(npc_pool, character.name)`, which silently fell back to the player-perspective `default` pool and printed second-person lines like `"You swing at Shy-Guy"` for a bear's own attack). See Section 4.5 for the full substitution-rule change and Section 4.8 for the new pools.

**Level-up status push (v19 brief 2).** The character-attack branch's level-up `while` loop (in `process_combat`, refills Vitality/Longevity via `recalculate_bars` on each level gained) previously appended only a text message per level. Immediately after the loop completes — not per iteration, so a multi-level kill still pushes one status — `statuses.append((character.pk, self._build_status(character)))` runs, placed inside the `if npc.vitality_current <= 0:` kill block so a levelling kill always pushes the refilled bars to the client even when the same round ends the fight. Before this, a level-up that also ended the fight left the header showing the pre-level bars until some later command or tick happened to push a fresh status.

**`send_to_player` gained an `event` parameter (v19 brief 2).** `send_to_player(character_pk, text, category, status, event=None)` — the group-send payload to `player_{character_pk}` now carries `'event': event` alongside the existing fields. All pre-existing call sites are unaffected by the default. Two values are sent:

- **`event='dying'`** — sent at both places in the file that set `character.is_dying = True`: the combat brink-of-death path in `process_combat`'s NPC-attack branch, and the DoT-kill path in `process_effects`'s `dot_vitality` component handling (the latter call was previously missing its required `status` argument entirely — a pre-existing bug that would raise on any DoT kill; fixed as part of adding the event, since the two changes touch the same call). The `messages` list threaded through `execute_actions` gained a fourth tuple element (`event`, default `None`) so the brink-of-death text carries `'dying'` through to its `send_to_player` call without a special-cased second send.
- **`event='respawn'`** — sent from the dying-expiry path once `execute_death` has moved the character to their effective home room (v24.26 #38: the attuned node's room, the Heart when no bond exists), alongside the existing death text and status payload (the payload dict is named `status_payload` rather than being built inline; the home room's zone and area travel with `get_expired_dying`'s `select_related`, so `area_name` carries the real area when one exists).

Consumer-side handling of both events is in Section 4.3.

**Respawn-timer fix in `process_npc_respawn` (brief 5).** The creation gap is now computed against **live + dead** instance counts (`to_create = min(count − (live+dead), count×2 − (live+dead))`), matching the `RoomSpawn` docstring. A dead instance therefore holds its spawn slot until `clear_expired_dead` deletes it at `respawn_at`, and the replacement is created on the following tick. Before the fix the gap was computed against live instances only, so every kill was refilled one tick later (up to the 2× cap) — `respawn_minutes` never mattered in practice, a killed boss reappeared immediately, and the minion gate below never actually engaged. Found by brief 5's verification step "brood stops respawning while the Matron is dead", which was unpassable against the old arithmetic.

**Spawn gate in `process_npc_respawn` (brief 3).** For each active spawn, after `clear_expired_dead` runs and before any instances are created: if `spawn.requires_living_npc` is set and no live `NpcInstance` of that definition exists in the spawn's room (`gate_npc_is_alive`, one `exists()` query per gated spawn; ungated spawns query nothing), instance creation is skipped for that spawn this tick. Dead-row clearing still runs — it is bookkeeping. Net behavior: gated minions respawn on their own timer while their boss lives, stop refilling the moment it dies, and refill again on the tick after the boss respawns — the encounter resets as a unit. (A brief 3 note here previously described the count/cap arithmetic giving a `count=1` spawn a buffered instant replacement; that behavior was the bug fixed in brief 5 above.)

**Death broadcast in the combat block.** Where an NPC's death is processed (`vitality_current <= 0` → `is_alive = False`), immediately after the existing kill output and before any corpse/loot output: if `npc.definition.death_message` is non-blank it is appended once to the round's `room_messages` and broadcast to the NPC's room — every character present, including the killer (personal kill lines are flushed before room broadcasts, so the killer always sees the kill line first). Blank message = byte-identical pre-brief output.

**Focus-driven attack targeting (v19 brief 6).** A pure-Python helper, `resolve_focus_npc(session, npc_list)` (no DB access — reads the already-loaded `session.focus_npc_id` and an in-memory NPC list), replaces the old hardcoded `npcs[0]`: it returns `session.focus_npc` if that id is set and still present in `npc_list`, otherwise the first entry of `npc_list`. Two call sites now go through it:

- **Auto-attack creation** — where `process_combat` queues the character's action for the round (`create_auto_attack`, guarded by `not player_actions and npcs and not character.is_dying`, unchanged), the target passed is `resolve_focus_npc(session, npcs)` instead of `npcs[0]`.
- **`execute_actions`'s Acuity `is_focus` computation** — `focus_npc_pk` (previously `npcs[0].pk if npcs else None`, computed once at the top of the closure) is now `resolve_focus_npc(session, live_npcs).pk` at that same point. Because the player's auto-attack target and the round's `is_focus` reference now come from the identical resolution, `is_focus` is no longer a separate concept shadowing the real target — Acuity's single-target bonus always applies to whatever the character is actually attacking, by construction. `focus_npc_pk` is then a plain local variable, live-updated in place by the kill-block shift below (not re-resolved from `session` mid-round, since `session.focus_npc` itself is only written when a shift occurs).

**Focus-death shift and session-end clearing (v19 brief 6).** In the kill block (`npc.vitality_current <= 0`), immediately after the killed NPC is dropped from `live_npcs` and removed from `session.npcs`:

- **No live NPCs remain** — unchanged combat-end path, now also clears focus: `session.is_active = False; session.focus_npc = None`, saved together, then the existing `"Combat has ended."` message.
- **Other live NPCs remain, and the killed NPC was the round's focus** (`npc.pk == focus_npc_pk`) — `session.focus_npc` is set to `live_npcs[0]` (first in the existing live-NPC ordering) and saved; `focus_npc_pk` is updated in place so any later action in the same round sees the new focus immediately; and a personal message is appended, category `combat`: `You turn your attacks on {name}.`, where `{name}` is `npc_display_name(new_focus, live_npcs)` — the same ordinal helper the consumer uses (Section 4.3/4.5), so a shift onto the last same-name survivor correctly drops the ordinal (`"the black bear"`, not `"the first black bear"`). If the killed NPC was *not* the focus (an off-focus kill isn't possible today — there's no multi-target damage, so the character only ever damages its own focus — but the check exists for whenever that changes), no shift or message fires.

**Gear in the round (v22 brief 5, #100/#68).** `execute_actions` loads the character's equipped set once at the top of the closure (`equipped_all`, one query — gear cannot change mid-round) and derives everything from it: `eff = effective_stats(character, equipped_all)`, `char_tav = total_armor_value(...)`, `gear_crit_bonus = summed_gear_stat(..., 'crit_chance') × 0.01`, and `gear_lifesteal`; the weapon pick filters `equipped_all` instead of running its own query, and the round-order initiative outside the closure reads effective DEX/PER through a small `@_dsa` helper. Player attacks: `resolve_hit(eff['dex'], npc_dex, crit_bonus=gear_crit_bonus)`; the weapon term is the v24.6 composite strike (next paragraph). On a landed hit (hit/critical — a graze rolls nothing), `roll_gear_bonus_damage(equipped_all)` fills the gear pool; the NPC loses `damage_int + gear_bonus`, and a nonzero pool renders as the parenthetical — `You hit the giant cave spider for 14 (+7) damage.`, crits composing `for a critical 14 (+7) damage!` — base first, gear's part in parens, zero pool leaving the line byte-identical to pre-B5. Lifesteal then heals via the atomic clamped F()-update, refreshes the in-memory `vitality_current` so the rest of the round computes on truth, and pushes a status (the bar moving is the whole announcement). NPC attacks: `resolve_hit(npc_dex, eff['dex'])` (no crit bonus — gear is the player's), and the raw final damage passes through `apply_armor_mitigation(raw, char_tav)` before it touches `vitality_current` — the only mitigation site in the game.

**The composite strike retires `equipped_weapons[0]` (v24.6 brief 1, #177/#178).** The player-attack branch keeps its existing weapon filter (`item_type == 'weapon'` and not `is_broken`, over the round's `equipped_all` — still no per-round query) but no longer selects a single `weapon_item` from it by queryset ordering: when the filtered list is non-empty, `base_damage = composite_weapon_term(equipped_weapons, eff['str'], eff['dex'])` (Section 4.5), fed through the unchanged `calculate_damage` call with `stat_bonus=0` and `durability_mod=1.0` — every equipped, non-broken weapon contributes at its slot factor (primary 1.0 by `MAIN_HAND → RANGED → OFF_HAND` priority, every other weapon 0.5), each with its own damage roll, governing stat (DEX for ranged, STR otherwise), and durability, while the acuity modifier and the graze/crit multiplier apply once, to the composite. A dual-wielder, a bow-plus-blade loadout, and a two-hander-plus-pistol loadout all compound; a bow-only or off-hand-only loadout fights at full strength; a broken weapon simply isn't in the list, so a broken primary means the next occupied slot in priority is primary, and zero non-broken weapons means unarmed. The armed/unarmed branch condition at both the damage site and the flavor-text site is now "any equipped, non-broken weapon" — armed flavor with one or more weapons, the unarmed pool only with zero — and the unarmed `else` branch (base `uniform(1, 3)`, STR bonus, durability 1.0, unarmed message pools) is byte-identical to pre-v24.6. Output shape unchanged: one hit roll per round, one line per player attack, no per-weapon breakdown, the gear parenthetical as before — only the number grows. NPC-side combat is untouched. Tests: `tests/test_v24_6_brief1.py` (helper math unit tests plus engine-harness wiring tests).

**The plunder hook (v24.29 brief 1, #235).** `Character.plunder_mode` runs the rights-scoped corpse sweep automatically when combat ends. **The anchor rule is the whole trigger: plunder fires wherever `Combat has ended.` is delivered to a character, and nowhere else.** That line is emitted from exactly three sites in this file, all on the same condition — no living NPCs remain in the session — and all three carry the hook: **(A)** the loop-head self-heal close (#218's path, a session found holding no living NPCs), **(B)** an M2M sibling session emptied by someone else's kill, and **(C)** the killer's own session emptied by its last kill. B and C sit inside `execute_actions`, which already runs synchronously in a worker thread, so they call `_queue_plunder` and append onto the round's `messages` / `room_messages` accumulators; A is in async context and goes through `deliver_plunder`, which wraps the hook and sends directly. In every case the plunder lines follow the combat-end line and any level-up lines already queued for that character: ordering is not cosmetic — the transition is announced, then its consequence.

`loot_utils.plunder_on_combat_end(character_pk)` is the hook itself. It takes a **pk rather than a character object**, so the setting is genuinely read *at combat end* and a player who flips `plunder` mid-fight governs that same fight whatever the calling site loaded earlier in the round. It filters to the room's corpses this character killed — **the same predicate bare `loot` uses**, not a variant — and only then calls `sweep_corpses`.

**The silence contract (ruled).** Plunder is silent unless it plunders. With the setting off, with no corpses in the room, or with no corpses this character has rights to, it emits **nothing whatsoever**. `cmd_loot`'s refusals — `There is nothing to loot here.` and `That is not your kill; you may not loot it.` — belong to the typed command and are never spoken on plunder's behalf, which is why the filtering happens in the hook, before the sweep, and never routes through the command. When plunder *does* sweep, its output is the sweep's output verbatim: a plunder is indistinguishable from a `loot` the player typed, which falls out of the Section 4.3 extraction rather than being maintained separately.

**Flee and death are excluded by the anchor, not by a guard.** Flee's end path lives in `consumers.py` and emits no `Combat has ended.` line; a dying character is removed from its session before the session can close. Neither has — or should gain — an explicit plunder guard: the anchor already handles them and a second guard would be a second source of truth. Both exclusions are pinned by test instead. **No connected player is required** (ruled): quit is allowed in combat and combat continues after it, so a fight can end for a logged-out character — the mutations land and `send_to_player`'s `group_send` reaches an empty group, a no-op rather than an error. There is deliberately no connected-player check; the loot is theirs by the same rights predicate either way.

**Tick-loop async-safety rule (v22 brief 6, #135 the founding case).** Synchronous helpers cross into the async tick loop only via `database_sync_to_async` or on verifiably prefetched data — never as bare calls that execute ORM queries. The founding defect: `process_effects`' full-expiry branch called the documented-sync `_expiry_message_for_effect(parent)` bare; its fresh query (`definition.components.order_by('order').first()`) raised `SynchronousOnlyOperation` and killed the entire engine process on every full expiry of a timed effect (latent since v19 — instant effects never reach the expiry path; Docker's restart policy masked the deaths as silent one-tick skips). The fix wraps that one call in `database_sync_to_async`; the per-component sibling (`_expiry_message_for_component`) remains bare **by prefetch**, reading only attributes loaded by `get_expiring_component_instances`' `select_related('component', 'effect_instance__target', 'effect_instance__definition')` — any change to that loader or helper must re-verify the query-free property. Regression tests drive `process_effects` directly in async context (`tests/test_tick_expiry.py`); a negative-control run without the wrap reproduces the exact crash. **The one-vocabulary rule is outgoing-only (amendment 2, superseding amendment 1's incoming half):** on outgoing hit lines the leading number is the base damage and the parenthetical is gear's added share — `You hit the giant cave spider for 14 (+7) damage.`, zero pool byte-identical. Incoming lines carry **only** the post-mitigation number (the bar delta): `The giant cave spider hits you for 14 damage.` — amendment 1's `(-N)` armor parenthetical was scaffolding to verify armor in play and was removed by operator ruling (the grammar read ambiguously; every wordier alternative was too wordy). The quiet-line law now covers incoming lines unconditionally — armored and unarmored share the same form — and armor visibility lives permanently in the stats Armor row and the examine contribution line. Mitigation math is untouched; the `raw_damage`/`blocked` bookkeeping was deleted with the display.

The dying path is untouched by this brief: a dying character's own attacks are discarded before action generation (Section 4.9's fall sequence, above), so a dying character never reaches either of these focus-resolution call sites.

### 4.10 HTTP routes (`urls.py`)

The app-level URL configuration (`apps/shyland/urls.py`, namespace `shyland`) as of v16:

| Path | View | URL name |
|------|------|----------|
| `/shyland/play/` | `GameView` | `shyland:game` |
| `/shyland/create/` | `CharacterCreateView` | `shyland:create_character` |
| `/shyland/create/check-name/` | `CheckNameView` | `shyland:check_name` |
| `/shyland/` | `RedirectView` → `/shyland/play/` (non-permanent) | — |

All views are gated by `ShylandAccessMixin` (login required + `players.shyland` group or superuser).

### 4.11 Character Creation Flow (`views.py`, `forms.py`, `templates/shyland/character_create.html`)

New in v16. One character per account was already enforced at the schema level (`Character.user` is a `OneToOneField`); the creation flow now also enforces it at the view layer.

**Gating (`GameView.get`)** — if the authenticated user has no `Character`, `/shyland/play/` redirects to `shyland:create_character` instead of rendering the game terminal. A player without a character can do nothing in Shyland except use the creator or follow its "Back to front page" link to the site root (`{% url 'home' %}`).

**`CharacterCreateView`** (`GET`/`POST` at `/shyland/create/`) — both handlers first check for an existing `Character` and redirect to `shyland:game` if one exists (visiting the creator with a character never shows the form). The form has exactly three fields: Origin, Archetype, Name. No portrait selection exists, by design.

**Default name** — `_default_character_name(user)` returns `user.profile.gamer_tag or user.username`, matching the fallback order of the removed `Character.name` property, **truncated to 20 characters** so a long username can never pre-fill a value that fails validation.

**`CharacterCreationForm`** (`forms.py`) — `origin`/`archetype` are `ModelChoiceField`s with no empty label; `name` is a max-20 CharField. `clean_name` is the single source of truth for name validation, used by both the AJAX check and the final POST:

1. strip; reject blank
2. reject > 20 characters
3. reject if `Character.objects.filter(name__iexact=name).exists()` (advisory pre-check for the friendly error; the DB constraint is the authoritative gate)
4. **only if the submitted name differs from the user's set gamer tag** (`vetted_name`, passed in by the views): reject if `better_profanity.profanity.contains_profanity(name)`. The exemption covers only the gamer tag — the `username` fallback default has no upstream vetting and is always profanity-checked.

**`CheckNameView`** (`GET /shyland/create/check-name/?name=...`) — real-time availability endpoint. Instantiates `CharacterCreationForm` with only the name populated, runs validation, and returns `{"available": bool, "error": str|null}` from the name field's errors alone. The client-side check is advisory; the POST re-validates authoritatively.

**Creation (`CharacterCreateView.post`)** — on a valid form:

- **Spawn point** — `Room.objects.filter(zone__slug='the-convergence', coord_x=0, coord_y=0, coord_z=0).first()`. The lookup is by zone slug + coordinates, **never by room name** — the coordinate convention is stable across content rebuilds (as of v17 the room seeded there is "Heart of the Convergence"; before the Infinity City seed it was the placeholder "The Fracture Point"). If no room matches, the view fails loudly with a non-field form error ("Spawn point is not configured. Contact an admin.") rather than picking a different room. `current_room` is set to the spawn room; since v24.26 (#38) no home field is set — a new character's `attuned_node` stays null, which *is* the founding-node default.
- **Starting stats** — flat baseline of 8 on all six stats, with the Archetype's `primary_stat_1` and `primary_stat_2` raised to 18. No Origin-based stat modifiers.
- **Acuity** — `acuity_current`, `acuity_baseline`, `acuity_band_low`, `acuity_band_high` are copied from the chosen Origin (overriding the model defaults of 1.0/1.0/0.8/1.2).
- **Bars** — `recalculate_bars(character)` is called on the unsaved instance so `vitality_max/current` and `longevity_max/current` are computed from the level-1 stats above, not left at the model's raw 100/100 defaults.
- **Race safety** — `character.save()` runs inside `transaction.atomic()` with `IntegrityError` handling: concurrent same-name submits (both passing the form's advisory `exists()` check) re-render the form with "That name is already taken.", and a double-submit that trips the `user` OneToOne constraint redirects to the game. No creation path can 500 on a constraint violation.
- On save, the player is redirected to `shyland:game` and drops into the terminal at the spawn room.

**Starting attire** — generated flavor text only: the Archetype's `attire_silhouette` phrase combined with the Origin's `attire_material` phrase (e.g. Warden + Undying → "simple, unadorned vestments" of "black lace and grave-worn cloth"). No `ItemDefinition`/`ItemInstance` is created and no equipment slot is occupied. The phrases live on the two model fields; no code path renders the combined text yet (see Section 7).

**Template** (`character_create.html`) — extends the shared `base.html`, continuing the dark monospace terminal aesthetic of `game.html`. Native `<select>`/`<label>` elements (accessible without custom ARIA work), the chosen Origin's and Archetype's `description` shown beneath each select via a small vanilla-JS show-on-select, and a debounced (~400 ms) `fetch` to `shyland:check_name` that writes availability feedback into an inline `aria-live` status area under the Name field. Responses are sequence-guarded: stale in-flight checks are invalidated both by newer input and by clearing the field. `{% csrf_token %}` is the first element inside the form. All fields remain editable until submit.

**No-character WebSocket handling** — the consumer's character-less connect path sends `{"type": "redirect", "url": "/shyland/create/"}` before closing, and `game.html` navigates on that message type, so the HTTP gate and the WS gate route to the same place (see Section 4.3).

**Dependency** — `better-profanity>=0.7` added to `django/requirements.txt` (image rebuild required).

---

### 4.12 Map system (v20 brief 1; Maps V2 in v22 brief 1 — #82, #115, #53 absorbed)

**MapFrag definition.** A MapFrag is derived, never stored: the connected component over unflagged, intra-zone cardinal exits containing the character's current room. Up/down exits and boundary-flagged or cross-zone cardinal exits break fragments; rooms with no cardinal exits are single-room fragments. Fog-of-war is the existing per-character `RoomVisit` — no new tracking model.

**The ruled visual language (2026-07-18 design session).** Four named colors, declared as CSS variables:

| Name | Value | Meaning on the map |
|---|---|---|
| key-color | `#7FB3D5` | The one key fact: the here-dot |
| value-color | `#E8E4D8` | Known: discovered rooms, known edges/stubs, discovered-destination gates and badges |
| muted-color | `#6b6b80` | Unknown: frontier dots and their lines, unknown-destination gates and badges |
| agro-color | `#E24B4A` | Stroke of a **visited** room with any aggressive spawn configured. Re-unified with error-color by the v22 B2 amendment 2 ruling (it had diverged to `#cc4444` in amendment 1); the two names stay deliberately separable in the future — map code uses `--agro-color`, never `--error`. |

Rule of grammar: **stroke color carries room state** (value / agro); **solid fills mean points** (here-dot, frontier dot); value-vs-muted carries known-vs-unknown everywhere.

**Geometry.** The SVG stays 300×300 inside `#map-wrap` (CSS untouched — #115 is solved inside the renderer). A **pinned 16px margin** on all sides leaves a centered 268×268 drawable area. The window is **7×7 cells (radius 3)**, current room centered, CELL = 268/7 ≈ 38.29px; rooms outside the window are not drawn. Unified 2px stroke for room circles, all edges, all stubs, gate lines and triangles. Glyphs:

- **Room:** hollow circle, r=10, fill `--surface`, stroke value-color (agro-color when the room has an aggressive spawn configured).
- **Here-marker:** solid key-color dot, r=6, centered, drawn atop the room glyph; no ring, no fill change.
- **Travel node:** octagon, circumradius 12, stroke 4px value-color, vertices at 22.5°+45k° (flat sides face the cardinals), fill `--surface`. One glyph for shard and sphere rooms — the distinction lives in the travel listing. **Never agro** (seed invariant below).
- **Gate:** 8px connector line from the room glyph's outer stroke edge, perpendicular into the base midpoint of a solid equilateral triangle, side 10px, apex pointing outward. Value-color if the destination room is visited, muted-color if not — line and triangle together (this coloring is what closed #53).
- **Undiscovered (frontier) room:** solid muted-color circle, r=5, no stroke. Its connecting line from the discovered room is muted-color.
- **U/D badges:** 14px bold `Courier New` text, U at the upper-right corner and D at the lower-right, centered on the 45° diagonal away from the glyph center under the **tuck rule** (amendment 1): each badge sits as close to its glyph as possible without touching — the letter's ink clears the glyph's outer stroke edge by ~0.5px (the attachment law's spirit applied to text). Per-glyph offsets, derived from the measured rendered ink of 14px bold Courier New (U/D ink ≈6.8px wide, spanning −4.2..+5.0px vertically around the placement point; re-derive under closest-without-touching if the font's metrics ever change): **circle 12.25**, **octagon 13.75** — text center at (+OFF, −OFF) for U and (+OFF, +OFF) for D. The tight tuck dissolves every opposing-badge adjacency collision (circle–circle ≈4.6px air, mixed ≈3.1px, octagon–octagon ≈1.6px), so no authored-content constraint on vertically adjacent U/D rooms exists. Per-badge color: value-color if the destination room is visited, muted-color if not — independent per direction. (The v20 "UD" composite badge is retired; the base brief's uniform 17px offset is superseded.)

**Attachment law.** Anything that attaches to a glyph attaches at the outer edge of its stroke, never the geometric radius — circles at 10 + 1 = 11px; the octagon's flat side at its apothem 12·cos 22.5° ≈ 11.09 + 2 = 13.09px. This prevents cross-color stroke overlap.

**Rendering rules.** Edges draw first, glyphs on top, here-dot and badges last. An edge between two drawn rooms is a full line center-to-center (value-color to a known destination, muted-color to a frontier dot). An exit whose destination lies outside the window renders as a solid half-cell stub — value if the destination is visited, muted if not. The old dashed-stub and boundary-tick vocabulary is retired entirely: no dashes, no perpendicular ticks, anywhere. Frontier rooms draw no exits of their own — only lines *from* discovered rooms reach them (terminus rule). Every `map` message discards and fully re-renders (no incremental diffing). Vanilla JS generating SVG — no framework, no libraries; north is up.

**Payload schema** (`send_map()` → one JSON message):

```json
{"type": "map", "zone": "<zone slug>", "current": {"x": 0, "y": 0},
 "rooms": [
   {"x": 0, "y": 0, "discovered": true, "here": true,
    "travel_node": true, "agro": false,
    "exits": {"north": "known", "east": "unknown", "south": "gate-unknown"},
    "up": "known", "down": "unknown"},
   {"x": 0, "y": 1, "discovered": false}
 ]}
```

Field semantics:

| Field | On discovered rooms | On frontier rooms |
|---|---|---|
| `x`, `y` | always | always |
| `discovered` | `true` | `false` |
| `here` | `true` on the current room only; omitted elsewhere | never |
| `travel_node` | `true` iff a `TravelNode` exists for the room; omitted when false | **never present** |
| `agro` | `true` iff any `RoomSpawn` in the room references an `NpcDefinition` with `is_aggressive=True` — **configuration, not instance state** (a dead or unspawned instance still flags) | **never present** |
| `exits` | per cardinal exit present: `"known"` / `"unknown"` (destination visited or not), `"gate-known"` / `"gate-unknown"` (boundary-flagged **or** cross-zone exit, by destination visit). Directions with no exit omitted. | **never present** |
| `up`, `down` | `"known"` / `"unknown"` by destination `RoomVisit`; key omitted when the exit doesn't exist | **never present** |

**Masking by construction.** A frontier entry contains exactly `x`, `y`, `discovered: false` — nothing else, ever. Fog-of-war is enforced in the wire format: an undiscovered room discloses existence only; the server never relies on the client to hide anything.

**Room set.** The discovered set is the MapFrag intersected with the character's `RoomVisit` set; the current room is always included (defense-in-depth — since #50 every arrival path records its visit, so this covers moves that bypass the consumer, e.g. an admin editing `current_room`). The frontier set is the unvisited fragment rooms cardinally adjacent (via an unflagged, intra-zone exit) to a discovered room. Nothing deeper than the frontier ever enters the payload. Gate destinations never enter the `rooms` array; they are looked up only for their visit bit.

**Query discipline (binding).** Payload builds fire per player, per room change, plus connect — post-#107 per-operation discipline applies. The build is a bounded, constant **five queries**: the current room, the zone's rooms (fragment-BFS source data), one `RoomVisit` query over the union of zone room ids and all out-of-zone destination ids (so gate coloring and U/D badges cost nothing extra), aggro room ids (`RoomSpawn` on `room__zone` with `npc_definition__is_aggressive=True`), and travel-node room ids. No queries inside the BFS loop; no per-room queries of any kind. Locked by an `assertNumQueries(5)` regression guard in `tests/test_map_payload.py`.

**Seed invariant (octagons never agro).** `seed_world._verify` permanently checks that no travel-node room has an aggressive spawn configured — no `RoomSpawn` whose room has a `TravelNode` references an `NpcDefinition` with `is_aggressive=True`. Law: an octagon is a special room that will never have agro.

**Delivery events.** Full state on connect (the v19 client-state sync pattern) and on every room change: move (including the aggro-ambush branch that skips the room description), flee, travel, and the `'respawn'` player-message event. `look` does not send a map (the room did not change).

**Client (`game.html`).** The right pane is fixed 300 px content width running top to bottom — ruled in the v20 layout pre-decision as the map's permanent home. Player stats sit at the top of the pane, the fixed 300×300 px map square sits at the bottom, and the space between holds the fight-info feed (Section 4.15).

**Accessibility stance.** The map container carries `aria-hidden="true"`. Room descriptions and exits in the text output remain the accessible source of truth; the map adds no information not already present in text.

**Client message-type inventory** (for reference — the full set the client handles as of v20 brief 4): `output`, `status`, `clear`, `redirect`, `map`, `verbs` (connect-time verb list + `show_timestamps`, brief 3; + `echo_mode`, v22 brief 2), `complete` (tab-completion reply, brief 3), `fight` (fight-info feed, brief 4 — Section 4.15), `pong` (connection-indicator echo, brief 4 — Section 4.15), plus the `quit` event. Since v20 brief 2, every one of these — all types, without exception — additionally carries the `ts` and `seq` envelope fields (Section 4.13). The client sends two non-command message types upward: `{"type": "complete", "text": "<current line>"}` (Section 4.14) and `{"type": "ping", "nonce": <int>}` (Section 4.15).
### 4.13 Output message envelope (v20 brief 2, #32)

**Envelope fields.** Every outbound WebSocket message to the client — all types in the inventory above — carries two server-generated fields:

- `ts` — epoch **milliseconds**, UTC, stamped **where the message is created**, via `apps/shyland/envelope.py`'s `envelope_ts()` (the module's only job; it exists so the consumer and the tick engine share one stamp). Never trusted from, or supplied by, the client. Creation sites: every direct `send_json` payload in the consumer (including the `output`/`send_output` helpers, `send_room_description`'s room and status payloads, `build_map_payload`, the no-character `redirect`, and `cmd_quit`'s `{'event': 'quit'}`), every `room_message` `group_send` dict the consumer builds, and the tick engine's two send funnels `send_to_player` / `broadcast_to_room` (`send_to_player` stamps the rider `status` dict separately, since it is delivered to the client as its own message).
- `seq` — **per-connection monotonic integer**, stamped at the delivery choke point (below), starting at 1 on each new connection (the counter is initialized in the consumer's `__init__`; one consumer instance per connection) and strictly increasing for the life of that connection. Resets on reconnect.

**Ordering semantics (binding).** `seq` order is authoritative for rendering. `ts` may occasionally be non-monotonic relative to `seq` — a broadcast created earlier can be delivered after a direct message created later — and that is correct behavior, not a defect. The client renders in arrival (`seq`) order and never reorders by timestamp.

**The delivery choke point.** `SkylandConsumer` overrides `send_json(content, close=False)`. It increments and assigns `seq`, guarantees `ts` is present — stamping it at delivery only as a fallback, with a server-side warning (`shyland.envelope` logger) naming the offending message type so the unstamped creation site gets found and fixed — and then delegates to the parent send. There is no other legal send path: nothing in the consumer calls `self.send()` raw, and the channel-layer handlers (`room_message`, `player_message`) deliver only through this method, carrying the event's creation `ts` through rather than restamping it. A message that reaches the client without passing the choke point is a bug by definition. **This single point is the envelope stamp — not the capture point for Monitoring and Command (MC, #37/#33).** MC taps at creation level — one record per event, in the consumer and ticker processes alike — because this choke point is per-connection: a delivery-level sink would record one row per *recipient* of every broadcast, not one per event (ruled 2026-08-17, #37). **The sink shipped in v25.1 (Section 4.19):** every creation site now pairs its send with an `mc.mc_emit` record — the personal-out helpers, the direct creation sends, the tapped `send_status`, and the `mc_group_send` broadcast wrapper in the consumer; the two outbound helpers in the ticker. This choke point itself remains exactly what it was — the envelope stamp, untapped.

**Client rendering (`game.html`).** Messages of type `output` in a **stamped category** display a dim timestamp prefix in the player's local time, `[HH:MM:SS.ss]` (hours/minutes/seconds plus two decimal places on the seconds, derived from `ts` via `new Date`), built by the `tsSpan()` helper in `append()`. The span is styled `.msg-ts` (muted palette color, slight opacity — visually subordinate to the message text) and carries **`aria-hidden="true"`**, so the `role="log"` live region announces message text clean, with no timestamp noise on any line; the assertive combat live region likewise receives bare `msg.text`. Non-`output` message types carry the envelope but display nothing new. The client keeps no seq counter of its own — arrival order is render order, and a reconnect is a fresh page/JS state, which is the v19 client-state-sync full-state pattern applied to sequencing. A message without `ts` (impossible in normal operation) simply renders unprefixed.

**Display categorization — timestamps mark events, not renderings (Amendment 1, #56).** An event happens *at a moment* and the moment is information; a rendering is a snapshot of now, and stamping its lines adds noise. The display rule is category-driven: the server assigns every `output` message a category, and the client stamps by category membership only — it never infers stamping from message text, and an unknown category defaults to **stamped** (events are the common case for novel messages). **`ts`/`seq` remain universal beneath the display rule**: every outbound message of every category still carries both through the delivery choke point; MC capture is untouched. The authoritative partition:

| Display | Category | Content |
|---|---|---|
| Stamped | `combat` (+ the brief-5 family `combat-hit-out`, `combat-crit-out`, `combat-hit-in`, `combat-crit-in`, `combat-miss-out`, `combat-miss-in` — Section 4.16) | Hits, misses, crits, damage lines, engagement/flee lines |
| Stamped | `reward` (brief 5) | Kill/XP lines, level-ups |
| Stamped | `success` (brief 5) | Sales, buys, repairs, pickups, looted items/coin |
| Stamped | `echo` (brief 5, #15) | The player's own command echo `> …` — displayed-prefix like output, never re-broadcast |
| Stamped | `chat` | Player says, NPC dialogue replies |
| Stamped | `system` | Presence (arrivals/departures), action results (drop/equip/use), setting-change confirmations, quit farewell, corpse decay/disappearance, empty-corpse responses |
| Stamped | `error` | Every error-category line (refusals, no-exit, dying gate, fatal-blow) |
| Stamped | `room` | Ambient room events: other players' pickups/drops, travel flavor broadcasts, kibitz, dialogue connectives |
| **Unstamped** | `room-render` | The **entire room-rendering block**, on both entry and `look` — since brief 5 one structured message: prose, exits, players, `who`/`what` line arrays, `zone_color` for the closing separator (Section 4.16; the bracket header was removed by amendment 1, #77) |
| **Unstamped** | `report` | **State reports**: `inventory`, `wallet`, `stats`, `help`, `who` listings, vendor `list`, `examine` output, the `travel` destination listing, the bare `brief` query |

Two categories are new in Amendment 1, both assigned server-side in `consumers.py`: `room-render` (which is also the dispatch key for the client's room-block renderer and the styling hook the output & messaging brief's look-section work, #14, builds on rather than re-plumbing) and `report`. The tick engine's sends are all events and kept their existing stamped categories unchanged. The client's `UNSTAMPED_CATEGORIES` set (`room-render`, `report`) is the single display switch; the `timestamps on|off` preference (#45, shipped in v20 brief 3 — Section 4.14) governs **stamped categories only** — unstamped categories are never stamped regardless of the preference.

---

### 4.14 Command layer: chart, parser, response layers, gates, pools (v20 brief 3; command revamp in v22 brief 2 — #111, #29, #54, #58, #59, #61, #65, #67, #75, #96, #98, #121, #122)

**Design source.** The authoritative specification for this layer is the B2 command spec DD — `docs/shyland/Shyland_V22_B2_Command_Spec_DD.md` (chart with stable-numbered footnotes, palette, doctrines, matrix, pools, standards). This section describes what is built to it.

**The chart governs.** Every command belongs to one of four types — action, information, movement, settings — and its argument cell is law. Dispatch is `COMMAND_TABLE` (verb/alias → handler, takes-args) wrapped in the v20 catch-all guard (a crashing handler logs its traceback, sends one `error` line, and leaves the connection alive — unchanged). New in v22: `PROMPT_VERBS` maps every command with a required target to its canonical prompt verb — bare invocation answers `What do you want to <verb>?` (fn 10, CLI error) centrally in `_dispatch`, before the handler (v24.5, #189: `loot` left the set — its target is optional and bare `loot` is the `all` sweep, GDD §9.1 fn 20; v24.7, #195: `equip`/`eq` left the set — the target is optional per chart cell `4 · 21` and bare `equip` renders the Equipment paper-doll, an information rendering, GDD §9.1 fn 21; fn 10 is untouched for every other command); a bare numeric answers `<verb> <N> what?` (fn 13) or `spend <N> points on which stat?` (fn 15). Information commands, movement, `flee`, and `quit` ignore all arguments (fn 2). The unknown-command line is an `error` (CLI layer).

**The resolver (`command_grammar.py`).** Still the single authoritative reference matcher; matching mechanics are unchanged from v20 (ordered token-prefix subsequence over the player-visible name-with-tier, plural fallbacks, rarity as a closed-vocabulary instance filter, `N.noun` age-order index, cross-definition ambiguity refusal, per-verb selection ordering: sell/drop lowest-most-damaged-oldest, equip highest-best-oldest, else oldest; equipped items never resolve for sell/drop). v22 policy changes (DD §1): **`use` takes a numeric-only optional quantity** (#65 — `use 3 healing draught`; `all` refused with teaching wording); **`buy` and `drop` are numeric-only** (fn 11 — `all` refused); **drop's candidate pool excludes bound items** (fn 16 — a bound-only match answers the mechanical `…is bound to you and cannot be dropped.` warn); **bare `sell all` is blocked** (fn 17 — `Sell all of what? …`, warn) while `sell all <noun>` / `sell all <rarity>` stay legal; **the noun-less `sell all <rarity>` form excludes consumables** (v23.1, #150, GDD §9.1 fn 19 — `Policy.bulk_exclude_type='consumable'` filters the match pool when the quantifier is `all` and no noun token is present, before selection and value math; `Resolution.bulk_excluded` flags a skip for the handler's teaching note; every noun-carrying form bypasses the guard — see Section 4.3); **`loot` is `[all] | <NPC>`** (v24.5, #189) — the target is optional: bare `loot` IS the `all` room sweep, verbatim in every case, or one corpse by its NPC name (`parse_corpse_noun`); the v20 item-noun/union loot forms are retired; loot is the first and only verb whose bare form performs the `all` sweep — deliberately no precedent for `sell`, whose fn-17 block stands (#150); **`attack` requires a target** — the targetless auto-attack fossil is removed (aggro NPCs self-engage since v21 #17); **`spend` is `spend [<quantity> | all] <stat>`** (fns 7/14/15 — the argument order flipped; `all` spends every unspent point; the old `<stat> <amount>` order errors). `Resolution` gained `requested` — on partial-policy verbs an over-count returns the possible part with the asked-for count for the handler's shortfall report.

**Three-layer response doctrine (DD §3, #121).** Every refusal belongs to a layer: **CLI error** (`error` category, error-color) — the parser refused: unknown command, bad syntax, the fn 10/13/15 prompts, settings usage lines, quantifier misuse; **world declined** (`warn` category — new, plumbed through the choke point like any other) — resolution failures (pool miss, bad index, ambiguity, the sell-all block) and mechanical failures (state-gate refusals, no vendor/repairer/obelisk, can't afford, sold out, capacity, repair didn't hold, heal at full, bound-drop, cursed/bag unequip blocks, unattackable, failed flee, unknown/ambiguous travel destination, no-exit movement); **world answered** — success prose per DD §6, combat family, loot-color gains. The mapping for resolver refusals is `SkylandConsumer._refusal_category` (syntax codes → error; everything else → warn). The re-tag touched 49 fixed warn-layer call sites and 10 fixed error-layer sites in the consumer; the tick engine's dying fatal-blow line keeps its v19-ruled red (`error` category). **B5 amendment 3 (#132): consequence must be seen — the partial-fulfillment shortfall reports (`You only had N.` on use and drop, `There were only N here.` on pickup, `They only had N.` on buy) and use's no-effect `Nothing happens.` (functionally a refusal) moved `system` → `warn`, wording untouched. Deliberate exceptions, ruled correct as-is: the sell shortfall keeps its `success` voice (`You only had 3 — the vendor was happy to take them.` — the vendor is pleased, guarded by test), and the ambient system voice keeps the logout farewell and the v21-ruled muted combat misses.**

**State-gating matrix (DD §5, #29).** Applied centrally in `_dispatch` — one lookup, at most one session query. **In combat**, refused (warn, in voice): buy/sell/repair, drop/pickup/loot, equip (**targeted forms only** since v24.7, #195 — the gate follows the act, not the verb: `_dispatch` skips the combat gate for a bare `equip`/`eq` invocation, which renders the paper-doll, GDD §9.1 fn 21)/unequip, travel, all movement — plus **spend** since B5 amendment 2 (#131), gated inside its handler with the generic `You can't do that while in combat.` rather than a `COMBAT_BLOCKED` authored line; allowed: attack, flee, use, examine, say, quit, bare `equip` (v24.7), all information, all settings. **While dying**, allowed: use (self-rescue), say, quit, information, settings; everything else refused (warn). **Quit is allowed in combat and while dying, and combat continues after quit** — `CombatSession` is DB state, no code path ends it on disconnect; the player can die logged out. Tab-closing and quitting are identical in cost. **The durability-restore exception (v24.12 brief 1, #134):** `use` itself stays allowed in combat and while dying, but a consumable whose effect carries a `durability_restore` component refuses in both states inside `_use_per_item` — component-keyed, never a name match, dying's line winning over combat's, no item consumed (Section 4.3's per-item durability gate); healing consumables pass both states exactly as before.

**Resolution pools (DD §8).** buy → room vendor stock · sell → inventory excluding equipped (bound sellable — vendors are the designed sink) · drop → inventory excluding equipped and bound · pickup → room floor · equip → carried equippables · unequip → equipped · use → carried consumables, never vendor stock · repair → everything owned including equipped · **examine → the union**: inventory + equipped + floor + NPCs here + corpses + players here + vendor stock (the vendor-examine gap is closed; players answer with their composite line), resolved nearest-first — self before room before vendor · attack → living room NPCs · loot → lootable corpses here · travel → revealed destinations · spend → the six stats. **Name invariant (#122):** a player and an NPC may never share a name — character creation rejects any name matching an `NpcDefinition.name` case-insensitively (`That name belongs to the world already.`), and `seed_world._verify` permanently checks that no NPC definition name collides with any existing character name.

**Partial fulfillment (DD §7).** Do the possible part and report warmly; fail (warn) only when nothing is possible.
