Sell shortfall sells what exists and prints the ruled line (`You only had 3 — the vendor was happy to take them.`); drop/use/pickup shortfalls do likewise with plain notes; any pickup at capacity fails outright (warn); partial `pickup all` takes oldest-first (age = `created_at`/pk — the floor-arrival proxy). **Heal sequences (#61, #65):** `use N` applies one item at a time; any heal attempted at full vitality fails (warn, `You are already at full health.`); a heal that lands at full prints the loot-color `You have been restored to full health.` and stops the sequence (heal detection derives from the effect's own `restore_vitality`/`hot_vitality` components). **`repair all` (#75):** passes over what is still damaged until everything is repaired, funds run out, or 5 passes; each mend line prints as it lands. The v20 bulk-operation messaging rule stands unchanged: streams of per-event messages plus a summary, never joined batches.

**Success sentences (DD §6; multi-item split re-ruled by amendment 5).** The transactional commands answer with one plain past-tense sentence, item references composed through `item_utils.item_ref` (definite `the …`; indefinite a/an for `use`), rarity words rarity-colored wherever item names render: `You buy the Iron Mace Mk 1 for 9 coppers.` · `You sell the Leather Gloves Mk 1 for 3 coppers.` · `You drop the Battle Axe Mk 1.` · `You pick up the Healing Draught Mk 1.` (loot-color) · `You equip the Iron Mace Mk 1.` / swap-aware `You equip the Battle Axe Mk 1, replacing the Iron Mace Mk 1.` · `You unequip the Iron Mace Mk 1.` · `You use a Healing Draught Mk 1 and feel your body recover. (+25 Vitality)` (v23.3 brief 1's merged sentence — formerly the plain sentence + a separate `system` effect line) · repair's existing line stands · `You spend 3 points on Dexterity.` + the new value. `success` renders value-color (transactional default); gains render loot-color via `reward`. **Spend's bar behavior reversed in v22 brief 5 (#109):** the v21-era full-bar refill on END/STR/WIS spends — briefly blessed as a bankable free heal — is dead; spend flows through the bar law (Section 4.5: bigger bar, same fill fraction, one atomic update), and its output remains exactly the two DD §6 sentences (no refill line ever existed to remove — the refill was silent bar state). **Amendment 2 (#131) blocks spend in combat entirely, fully superseding #109's state-matrix half ("spend remains legal in combat"):** the gate sits at the very top of `cmd_spend` — before any validation output or mutation — via `get_active_combat_session`, refusing with `You can't do that while in combat.` (warn). This is the **first generic in-combat refusal**; every `COMBAT_BLOCKED` entry remains a per-command authored line, and the gate lives in the handler rather than the dispatch table. The central dying gate in `_dispatch` keeps its precedence (spend is not dying-allowed; a dying player never reaches the handler). **Multi-item output splits by nature (amendment 5):** buy/sell/drop/pickup **aggregate** — a transaction is one act, so N>1 emits one count-form line per item definition (`You buy Healing Draught Mk 1 ×100 for 9 silver.` — no article, name verbatim, total money through the tier formatter; mixed operations emit one line per definition in first-reached floor order, singles among them staying singular; warm shortfall notes precede the aggregates; drop composes its names before the transfer because dropping re-veils identification, #80) — superseding the v20 #63 per-item stream for these four verbs; **use, repair, and loot stay per-line** — each iteration is its own news (a swallow's effect and the stop-at-full check, a mend's chance outcome, a find's identity), and use never pre-announces a count. The count form is the deliberately plural-free first iteration; the natural-English pluralization subsystem is filed as #126 for a future version. **v23.3 brief 1 supersedes the amendment-5 rule for `use` only (#149/#151):** instant-restore healing consumables aggregate to one count-form line via the deficit-planned aggregate path (Section 4.3), every other `use` stays per-line (with the #149 merged sentence per item), and **repair and loot remain per-line** untouched.

**Settings standard (DD §10).** `Character.echo_mode` (new, default on) and `brief_mode` (field default flipped to off; existing rows untouched) landed in migration `0031`; `show_timestamps` unchanged. All three settings commands (`brief`, `echo`, `timestamps`) share one helper: six accepted words (`on off yes no true false`, any case); bare = the current-setting sentence (`brief room display is off.`); set = the `now` sentence (`command echo is now on.` / `output timestamps are now on.`) — stateless, idempotent, plain prose; invalid input answers the CLI `Usage: <cmd> [on|off]`. Echo is **pane-only**: the preference rides the connect-time `verbs` message and every status payload (like `show_timestamps`), and the client simply skips rendering `echo`-category lines when off — MC capture and every server behavior are untouched. Every command remains timestamped and echoed server-side as an event.

**The fourth setting: `plunder` (v24.29 brief 1, #235).** `plunder [on|off]` joins the family through the same `_cmd_setting` helper and inherits the whole standard unchanged — six words, bare-reports, the `Usage:` line in error-color, confirmations in `system`. Its subject/verb pair is `('plunder', 'is')`, producing `plunder is off.` and `plunder is now on.`: the other three name a *thing* ("brief room display", "command echo", "output timestamps") while plunder's subject is the setting's own name, because there is no separate noun for it — the command names the behavior directly. **A settings command is registered in five places**, and plunder occupies all of them: `COMMAND_TABLE` (args-taking), `DYING_ALLOWED`, the `HELP_SECTIONS` Settings block (alphabetical, between `echo` and `timestamps`), tab completion's settings branch, and GDD §9.1. It is deliberately **absent from `COMBAT_BLOCKED`** — all settings are legal in combat by the state-gating matrix, and flipping this one mid-fight governs that same fight, because the hook re-reads the value at combat end. The connect-time `verbs` payload derives from `COMMAND_TABLE`, so it needs no separate edit.

**Setter hygiene (v24.29 brief 1, #250).** `_set_echo_mode` carried a stray `self.character.show_timestamps = value` — the DB write was correct, but the in-memory line named the wrong field, so every `echo on`/`echo off` silently corrupted the cached `show_timestamps`. Latent in practice (the connect-time `verbs` payload reads the attribute before any command can dirty it, and `_status_payload` reads a fresh DB fetch), but wrong. The line was **deleted rather than corrected**: `cmd_echo` assigned the cached attribute itself after the setter returned, so the stray line was redundant as well as wrong.

**The family made uniform (v24.30 brief 1, #251).** The v24.29 deletion left the shape it found: three setters writing only the DB row with the cache maintained by the command, and `_set_plunder_mode` alone writing both. That is the defect #251 names — the cache was right only by the caller's cooperation, so **any direct call to a setter left `self.character` stale**, and `cmd_timestamps` was already making exactly such a call. **All four `_set_*` setters now write the DB row and the cached attribute**, `_set_plunder_mode`'s shape generalized, and each setter is therefore the **single writer** of its own setting; the caller-side assignments in `cmd_brief`, `cmd_echo` and `cmd_plunder` are removed with the same motion, because leaving them would preserve the very pattern the ruling kills (`cmd_brief` and `cmd_plunder` lose their emptied `if value is not None:` blocks; `cmd_echo` keeps its, for the fresh fetch and status payload). `cmd_timestamps` needed no edit — its direct setter call is now cache-correct by construction rather than by the accident of a later wholesale `get_character_fresh()` replacement. This is a **latent** class of bug removed, not a live one: every gameplay read of the four settings was already fresh, which is why the release carries zero player-visible behavior change. The #250 failure mode still cannot recur anywhere — every setter caches its own field and no other.

**The sweep is transport-agnostic (v24.29 brief 1, #235).** `_loot_sweep` used to *be* the sweep: a consumer method awaiting `self.output(...)` for every line and consumer-bound `@database_sync_to_async` helpers for every mutation. The plunder trigger fires from the **tick engine** — a different process, with no consumer, and per the ruling sometimes with no connected player at all — so the sweep could not stay somewhere that sends. It now lives in **`loot_utils.py`** (new module, in the style of `item_utils` / `combat_utils` / `effect_utils`): `sweep_corpses(character, room, lootable)` is **synchronous**, performs the mutations, and **returns** the lines rather than sending them, so each caller wraps it the way it already wraps ORM work. `_loot_sweep` is now purely the consumer's transport over it; the tick engine appends the same tuples to its round accumulators. The per-corpse ORM steps moved with it and the consumer's async wrappers delegate, so there is exactly one implementation of each and the two call paths cannot drift. The return is a **pair**, `(messages, room_lines)`: the corpse-disposal announcement (`… slowly disappears.`) was always a room broadcast rather than a personal line, so it cannot be a message bound for the looting character — each transport broadcasts it on its own channel. The move preserved the rights predicate, corpse ordering, message text, categories and order of operations exactly; the regression bar was the existing loot suite passing with no test edited, and it did.

**Tab completion (DD §8, #67/#96).** The completer offers exactly each command's pool at each position, literals included: grammar verbs complete through their resolver pools (`complete()` now accepts mixed-kind pools via per-candidate accessors — examine's union spans items, vendor entries, and NPCs, with player names merged by the consumer); `all` is offered only where the grammar accepts it; settings complete the six boolean words; `spend` completes `all` + stat names, then stat names after a quantity; `travel` completes revealed destination names (the sender-typed pool — revealed spheres only at a shard; v24.26, #30); `loot` completes `all` + corpse NPC names. `attune` completes the verb only — no noun pool (v24.26, #38).

**Help (DD §11, #58 adjacent).** `cmd_help` renders from the chart-derived `HELP_SECTIONS`: four type sections (key-color `…` headers), Kind-3 tables (`Command / Usage / Description`, muted headers), usage strings compiled from the chart cells in BASH notation (`<>` required, `[]` optional, `|` alternatives), authored example-free descriptions, and four shared bottom sections (Arguments, Quantities, Settings, Tab completion). `echo` is documented; the B3 commands (home/cancel/last/sudo) arrive with B3, admin-gated ones hidden from non-members. The v21 `HELP_COMMANDS` table and `<item selection>` section are retired.

**The B3 commands: home, cancel, last, sudo (v22 brief 3 — #57, #113, #88, #112).** Four chart rows went live to the DD's footnotes 2/9/12/18 plus the B3 design rulings recorded in the brief.

*The delayed-action registry (the standing template).* `SkylandConsumer.delayed_actions` — name → connection-bound `asyncio` task. A delayed action registers on start and deregisters in its task's `finally`; the registry is `cancel`'s candidate pool, its argument-completion pool, and dies silently with the connection (`disconnect` cancels every task — intent state dies with the intender). No tick-engine involvement, no DB countdown state. Home is the first resident; every future delayed action follows the pattern.

*home (#57; timings re-ruled in v23.4, #162).* A 10-second countdown narrated as atmosphere — authored fog-motif lines at t=0/≈3/≈7 (`HOME_CADENCE`; arrival at t=10), never a timer UI. Gates in order: combat (central, warn) → dying (central) → already at the effective home room (`You are already home.` — since v24.26 the comparison is against the attuned node's room, the Heart when no bond exists) → already counting → cooldown. Anything breaks it: the player's own movement or travel auto-cancels via `_dispatch` (its line prints, the move proceeds); combat entry of any kind interrupts with the violent voice — the player's own attack cancels in `cmd_attack`, and engine-side entries (respawn aggro, incoming attacks) are caught at each beat's combat checkpoint inside the task; `cancel` uses the DD §6 line. Completion runs travel's machinery pattern in home's own voice: departure witness to the origin room, relocation to the **effective home room** — resolved at completion time via `get_home_room` (`resolve_home_room`: the attuned node's room, the founding Heart node when no bond exists; v24.26 #38, design rule 7 — a bond changed mid-countdown lands at the *new* home; no destination is captured at initiation), RoomVisit, arrival line, full render + map, arrival witness to the destination room, then `home_last_completed` is set — **completion-only consumption**: interrupted countdowns never start the clock. Cooldown fields on Character (migration `0033`; timings re-ruled by migrations `0038`/`0039`): `home_cooldown_seconds` (default 300 since v23.4 — #162 shortened the cooldown 15m→5m, and its data migration `0039` reset **every** existing row to 300 unconditionally, admin-overridden values included; the per-player admin-override capability itself is unchanged) and `home_last_completed`; the refusal is a wry pool line ending in the exact parenthetical (`(10m cooldown rem.)` — ceiling minutes ≥1m, else ceiling seconds).

*cancel (#113).* Chart fn 12: bare with nothing running answers the ruled verbatim warn line; bare with one action cancels it; a named argument prefix-matches the registry. Allowed in every state including combat and dying — the escape hatch is never locked.

*Admin stealth gating (DD §12, fn 18) and the three admin commands.* The auth Group `admins.shyland` (idempotent data migration `0034`) gates `sudo`, `last`, and `mc` (`ADMIN_VERBS`): membership is checked live on every attempt — no session caching, revocation instant — and non-members receive the unknown-command line byte-identically (one shared `send_unknown_command`); help renders admin rows per-player, the connect-time verbs list omits admin verbs for non-members, and tab completion for `mc` is membership-gated (the pool never leaks). *sudo (#112):* echoes like any command and the game never responds in-line — no output by design; the arguments reach the MC stream as `cmd` records for an attached agent to read (v25.5 ships the door's `answer` delivery path — Section 4.22; the listening bot itself is 25.6, #262). *last (#88):* `Character.last_connect` is written at every websocket accept for all players; the command renders the Kind-3 `Last seen...` table (Character / Status / Last seen) — the composite character line, `Online`/`Offline` from the Redis presence keys, and the three ruled time forms (`never`, `since <ISO-8601 UTC>` online, bare stamp offline) — ordered online-by-recency, then offline-by-recency, then never. *mc (v25.4, #266 — GDD §9.1 fn 22):* `mc <status|kill|restore>`, the MC kill switch — prefix-matched closed subcommand vocabulary, dying-allowed and combat-legal, voices per the three-layer doctrine; mechanics in Section 4.21.

**Travel listing (interim, B4 deferral; rebuilt by B2 amendment 3).** The bare `travel` listing renders per-zone display blocks: the key-color opener (`The Obelisk offers passage to...` from a sphere; `The Shard reaches through the network to the spheres it answers to...` from a shard sender — v24.26, #30), then per zone a Kind-1 heading `Zone: <name>` (key-color key; the zone name in the zone's `theme_color` — the licensed hex exception, carried by a seg `x` field the client validates) and a Kind-3 table `Type / Destination / Description` (muted headers, value rows, `Sphere`/`Shard` capitalized) with identical column x-geometry across all blocks (`_table_lines`' `min_widths` seeds shared widths). Zones order by hardness via `TRAVEL_ZONE_ORDER` in `consumers.py` — the GDD danger ladder (Convergence/Sanctuary first, Verdant Reach/Beginner next); the single interim authority, a `danger_rank` field being explicitly out of scope. Within a zone the ruled interim sort holds: ascending straight-line map-space distance from the player. The Description column is `TravelNode.listing_description` (migration `0032`, seed-owned enforce-exact): the stone's one-line sentence harvested verbatim from its room's own prose — the standing convention is that every new node gets its one-liner at authoring time; seed-verify pins the five current sentences exactly. The traveling path is untouched; the travel redesign still belongs to a future zones-and-travel version.

**The sell refusal in the three-layer doctrine, and the no-leak rule (v23 brief 4, #138).** An artifact sell refusal is **world-declined — warn voice** (the vendor chooses not to buy; the command was well-formed), drawn from the generic `SELL_REFUSAL_*` pools. **The no-leak rule is standing law on vendor speech:** no vendor line may name or imply an item's rarity, tier, or true name — unidentified items veil all three, and speech must not lift the veil. The refusal pools are deliberately reason-free so they read correctly for any future non-sellable category; `npc_voice.SELL_REFUSAL_FALLBACK` exists as an unwired guard rail — if it ever appears in play, a new non-sellable category shipped without its own voice. **The consumable-guard responses (v23.1, #150)** join the same layer: the all-consumables refusal (`That's all consumables — name them ('sell all draught') if you mean to sell them.`) is **world-declined — warn voice** (the command was well-formed; the world narrows its default reach), and the skip note (`Your consumables stay in your pack — name them ('sell all draught') to sell them.`) is one warn-voice teaching line appended after an otherwise-successful guarded sale — both authored fixed lines, not pooled speech (they are renderings, and renderings stay stable).

*Test suites:* `tests/test_command_revamp.py` (the v22 conformance suite — chart deltas, response layers, gates, pools, sequences, settings, say/crit prose, name invariant, completion literals), plus `tests/test_command_grammar.py` (the v20 table, two cases updated to the chart: sell partial fulfillment, drop numeric-only) and `tests/test_dispatch_guard.py`.

---

### 4.15 UI layout: skeleton, location bar, fight feed, connection indicator (v20 brief 4 — #2, #1, #31)

**The ruled desktop skeleton (`game.html`).** Baseline 1000 px; on window widening the right pane keeps fixed width and only the left regions grow:

- **Left 2/3 (700 px baseline), stacked:** LOCATION BAR (top, one line of text high) → OUTPUT PANE (one unified scrolling pane for ALL output — zone/area/room text, who's/what's here, commands and results; there is no separate description pane) → COMMAND BAR (bottom; the send button lives inside it, and the connection indicator sits at its right end, before the button).
- **Right pane (fixed 300 px content width, full height):** player stats (top; the whole subsection turns red during combat) → fight info (middle; scrolling) → the map (bottom; the brief-1 component, unmodified — only its container placement was re-homed).

Implemented as CSS grid (`minmax(0,1fr) 300px` columns; location/output/command rows in column 1, the pane spanning all rows in column 2), the pane itself a flex column with the fight region as the flexing middle. **Phone stacking** (breakpoint 600 px): location bar → compact stats strip (fight info directly under it, only while combat is active) → output (fixed-height internal scroll) → the map below the output, reachable by scroll → command bar pinned last (`position: sticky; bottom: 0`). The right pane dissolves via `display: contents` so its children join the stack by `order`. The pre-brief-4 phone toggle-overlay and the old header row (`#room-name`, `#side-toggle`) are gone. The output pane's `role="log"` / `aria-live="polite"` semantics, categories, and scrollback are untouched by the restructure.

**Viewport-fit rule (Amendment 1, #73 — binding; closed #51).** The app fills the viewport exactly and **the page never scrolls** — only designated interior panes scroll themselves: the output pane and the fight-info region of the right pane. Implementation: the shell anchors to the `html→body` `height: 100%` chain with `overflow: hidden` on both (window-level overflow impossible by construction; `box-sizing: border-box` is universal so borders/padding stay inside the height math), and the right pane carries `overflow: hidden` (never scrolls as a whole, in either axis — the #51 fix). At the phone breakpoint the shell itself becomes the interior scroll surface (`height: 100%; overflow-y: auto`), preserving the ruled stacking (map below the output, reachable by scrolling the app; command bar sticky) with no window scrollbar.

**Zone-colored pane borders (#85 — v21 brief 1).** All five pane borders are **5 px solid, tinted with the current Zone's `theme_color` at ~0.75 alpha**, matching the room separator's style and color (the separator itself is 3 px since amendment 1, #97 — deliberately lighter): (1) `#loc-bar` border-bottom; (2) the full-height vertical divider — the border-right of `#loc-bar`, `#output-wrap`, AND `#cmd-bar`, styled identically so it reads as one line; (3) `#cmd-bar` border-top; (4) `#side-stats` border-bottom; (5) `#map-wrap` border-top. Mechanism: the client sets the CSS custom property `--zone-border` to `<zone_color>BF` (alpha appended in JS, format-validated) whenever a room render arrives — the same server-delivered `zone_color` the separator consumes; the pre-first-render fallback is `#CCCCCCBF`. **Combat precedence on border 4 is DEAD (v23 brief 3, #119):** the v20-era `border-bottom-color: var(--combat-accent)` override in `#side-stats.in-combat` — a collision of v20 brief 4's whole-subsection red with this very zone-border ruling, surviving unnoticed — is deleted. The combat state is background + name color only; border 4 stays zone-tinted through combat. **Doctrine (promoted, ruled):** pane borders belong to zone/area theming exclusively; combat state — and any other transient state — expresses through backgrounds and text, never through borders. `--combat-accent` survives (connection indicator). `#map-wrap` grows to 305 px tall (300 px content + the 5 px border under `border-box`) so the 300×300 SVG still fits; the right pane keeps its ruled fixed 300 px content width and the #73 viewport-exact no-scroll invariant holds. The phone layout applies the same treatment to the borders that exist there (`#side-stats`/`#side-fight` border-bottom, `#map-wrap`'s full border — the wrap becomes 310×310). The room separator is untouched and does not touch the pane border to its right (acknowledged and fine).

**Stats pane content (Amendment 1 — #71, #72).** The pane header is **`Character.name` verbatim** — byte-for-byte, no `text-transform` or case styling anywhere in its CSS/JS path — server-supplied as `character_name` in every state-sync payload (consumer and tick engine), never client-derived; it turns combat-red with the rest of the section. The V/A/L text lines are replaced by: **Vitality and Longevity ratio bars** (track in the pane's dark neutral at the fight panel's 8 px bar height; fills `var(--success-color)` — the loot-message green verbatim, full strength, per the v22 B2 amendment 2 repaint; numerals alongside in `var(--value-color)` — amendment 3 recolored the bar labels and every bar number, and the pane header name, from muted/`--text` to value-color, with the in-combat name red re-pointed to `var(--error)` — right-aligned, matching the `stats` command's values) and the **Acuity band gauge** — not a ratio: a fixed 0.0–2.0 track (uniform across Origins for v1), the character's Origin optimal band (`acuity_band_low`..`acuity_band_high`, already server-supplied in state sync) as a **solid `var(--success-color)` block** (amendment 2 ended the band's translucency era — no alpha anywhere on it), and current acuity as a `var(--say-color)` gold position tick (amendment 3: 16px tall × 4px wide, extending 4px above and below the 8px track) clamped to the track, numeral alongside (two decimals, matching `stats`). Numerals remain real text (the accessible values); tracks and gauge are decorative (`aria-hidden`). The first UI surface that teaches the band mechanic rather than reporting a number. **Amendment 3 (fight panel recolor):** the targeting arrow, enemy health-bar border, and fill read `var(--error)`; enemy names and the x/y counts read `var(--value-color)` — `--combat-accent` and `--text` survive with their remaining readers untouched.

**Location bar (#1).** Format `Zone: Area: Room` — the Area segment and its colon are omitted when the room has no Area. Colors are **server-delivered**: the state-sync (`status`) payload carries `zone_name`, `zone_color`, `area_name`, `area_color`, `room_name`, straight from the Section 4.1 `theme_color` fields — nothing is computed client-side. The room name renders in fixed near-white `#E8E4D8`; the colon separators in neutral chrome gray `#8A887F`. Overflow on the single line truncates the Area segment first (a very large `flex-shrink` on its span), then Room; Zone never truncates (`flex-shrink: 0`); per-segment `text-overflow: ellipsis`.

**Combat-red stats state (#2).** The `status` payload gains `in_combat` — a server-derived combat-session-membership boolean, present in every state sync (consumer `_status_payload` and tick-engine `_build_status`, both of which now run their combat-session lookup in a DB thread; async tick-engine call sites go through `_build_status_async`). The client toggles class `in-combat` on the stats section from it. Fresh statuses are pushed on every membership transition: engagement (attack, aggro move, aggro flee-arrival), flee, victory, death/respawn, and stale-session disengagement. Combat-red family base values (the v20 output palette derives its shades from this family later): background tint `#3A1212`, accent/border `#E24B4A`; stat text colors unchanged.

**Fight-info feed (#2).** New server-generated message to each involved player:

```json
{"type": "fight", "active": true,
 "enemies": [{"name": "the cave spider", "hp": 12, "hp_max": 20, "focused": true}]}
```

Sent on combat engagement (consumer, alongside the status refresh — and immediately on an attack-focus switch so the marker moves without waiting for the tick), on **every engine tick** while the player's session is active (tick engine, after round resolution on round ticks so hp is current), and once with `"active": false, "enemies": []` when combat ends for the player — victory, flee, death (rider on the `respawn` player-message), or disengagement (stale/emptied session close). `enemies` lists every living NPC in the player's combat session; `focused` marks the current attack-focus target resolved exactly as the tick engine resolves it (session focus if alive and present, else first); names use `npc_display_name` as combat messages render them today. Delivery from the tick engine rides the `player_message` event as a `fight` field (like `status`), stamped with its own `ts` in `send_to_player` and delivered as its own client message — fight messages are player-scoped, so a second character in the room never receives them. Client rendering (middle region): one row per enemy — focus marker (`»`), name, a horizontal hp bar (fill proportional to `hp/hp_max` in the combat-red accent), `hp/hp_max` numerals; the region scrolls on overflow and is completely empty when inactive. Server-side only; nothing trusted from the client.

**Connection indicator (#31).** Protocol: the client sends `{"type": "ping", "nonce": <int>}` every 10 s; `receive_json` routes it by type before any command parsing (and before the dying gate) and replies `{"type": "pong", "nonce": <same>, "ts": …}` — echo only, and only for a well-formed integer nonce (bools and non-ints are dropped, never reflected; no client data trusted or stored). RTT is computed client-side from the nonce round-trip. Placement: right end of the command bar, before the send button — a status dot plus latency readout (`42ms`). States: **green** healthy (fresh pong, RTT < 250 ms); **amber** degraded (RTT ≥ 250 ms or one missed pong — a ping still unanswered when the next fires); **red, pulsing** while the client attempts reconnection; **gray** disconnected/closed (quit/redirect). Brief 4 also introduced client auto-reconnect: on close (unless quitting — or, since v21.1, superseded: the `superseded` flag suppresses the placeholder and the reconnect loop and nothing else, no navigation; see the single-session enforcement subsection in Section 4.3) the client retries with doubling backoff (1 s → 10 s cap); a successful reconnect is a fresh consumer/connection (full-state resync per the v19 pattern, `seq` restarts at 1), and the indicator returns to green on the first pong. Accessibility: the indicator carries an updating `aria-label` ("Connected, 42 ms" / "Reconnecting" / "Disconnected") but is deliberately **not** an announcing live region — no screen-reader spam; the static "Connected to Shyland" line behavior in the output is unchanged.

**Tests.** `tests/test_ui_layout.py`: status-payload location fields with and without an Area, `in_combat` tracking session membership through create/deactivate, fight enemy rows (hp, focus flag and fallback, dead-NPC exclusion, positional same-name display), pong echo, malformed-nonce drop, and ping-while-dying.

---

### 4.16 Output palette, look sections, command echo, NPC article grammar, corpse messaging (v20 brief 5 — #13, #14, #39, #15, #24, #28)

**The output palette (#13 — authoritative).** All output colorization is client-side styling keyed on server-supplied **semantic categories**; the server never sends hex for message text (the sole exception remains the zone/area `theme_color` values riding location updates and the room-render payload — `zone_color` for the separator and pane borders, `area_color` for area prose — all straight from model fields). The palette lives in one CSS block in `game.html`:

| Role | Category / class | Color |
|---|---|---|
| Structural section headers (#39) | `.msg-section strong` | `var(--key-color)` = `#7FB3D5` |
| Room content (names, occupants, items) | `.room-content` | `var(--value-color)` = `#E8E4D8` |
| Room-render block container (amendment 4 — the dimmer parchment is dead) | `.msg-room-render` | `var(--value-color)` = `#E8E4D8` |
| Report keys (v21, #90–#92) | `.msg-key` | `var(--key-color)` = `#7FB3D5` |
| Report values (v21, #90–#92) | `.msg-value` | `var(--value-color)` = `#E8E4D8` |
| Plain-text reports (amendment 4 — one content voice) | `.msg-report` | `var(--value-color)` = `#E8E4D8` |
| Area prose (v21, #86) | inline, from `area_color` | Area `theme_color` (fallback value-color) |
| Room prose (v21, #86) | inline | `var(--value-color)` = `#E8E4D8` |
| Zone / Area names | inline, from `theme_color` | model value — identical in the location bar and output |
| Player's outgoing hit (and generic `combat`) | `combat-hit-out`, `combat` | `var(--hit-out-color)` = `#C4453F` |
| Player's outgoing critical | `combat-crit-out` | `var(--crit-out-color)` = `#E24B4A`, bold |
| Incoming hit (NPC hits the player) | `combat-hit-in` | `var(--hit-in-color)` = `#E0724A` |
| Incoming critical (v22, DD §2 — dormant until NPC crits occur; wired) | `combat-crit-in` | `var(--crit-in-color)` = `#F08A50`, bold |
| Your miss (v23 B5 amendment 1, #152 — your whiff is a warning) | `combat-miss-out` | `var(--warn-color)` = `#E8D44D` |
| Their miss on you (v23 B5 amendment 1, #152 — their whiff is good news) | `combat-miss-in` | `var(--success-color)` = `#4caf7d` |
| Miss, either direction — LEGACY, zero senders since #152 (rule retained) | `combat-miss` | `var(--muted)` = `#6b6b80` |
| Gains and good outcomes: kill / XP / level / death_message / pickups / heal-to-full / ALL loot lines incl. copper (v23 B5 amendment 1 moved the copper-loot line here from `success` for #124 parity) / "Combat has ended." | `reward` | `var(--loot-color)` = `var(--success-color)` = `#4caf7d` |
| Transactional success prose (v22, DD §6) | `success` | `var(--value-color)` = `#E8E4D8` |
| CLI error (v22, #121; amendment 1 #124 — the crit-out/Artifact red, deliberate reuse; amendment 2 re-unified agro-color with it) | `error` | `var(--error)` = `#E24B4A` |
| World declined (v22, DD §3 — new) | `warn` | `var(--warn-color)` = `#E8D44D` |
| Speech, player and NPC (v22, DD §13 — was `chat`) | `say` | `var(--say-color)` = `#f0c060` |
| The sudo bot's answers (v25.5, #281 — attributed `sudo: {text}`, stamped: answers are events) | `sudo` | `var(--sudo-color)` = `#E24B4A` (error-red's hex under a separable name — the documented reuse pattern; the two may diverge; door code and `.msg-sudo` use `--sudo-color`, never `--error`) |
| System / ambient / narration (presence, decay, effect ticks, dialogue greetings/departures/connectives) — v23 B5 amendment 1 (#152): lifted off muted because #147 put dialogue narration on `room` and muted made it nearly invisible mid-exchange | `system`, `room` | `var(--value-color)` = `#E8E4D8` |

**The color doctrine (v23 B5 amendment 1, #152 — ruled from play):** gold is speech, green is what went your way (their misses, all loot), yellow is your whiff, the reds are damage, value-color is the world, and muted is true chrome only (echo, guides, unknowns, empty cells). Stamping class is unaffected — the v20 B2 stamped/unstamped table does not move; these categories changed color and name, not whether they are events.
| Command echo | `echo` | `var(--muted)`, text carries the `> ` prefix; pane-suppressed when `echo` is off |
| Timestamp prefix | `.msg-ts` | `#6E6C64` |
| Chrome punctuation (header brackets/dashes) | `.room-chrome` | `#8A887F` |

**Combat-red family rule (binding):** the stats-panel combat state (`#E24B4A` accent, `#3A1212` tint, Section 4.15) and the combat text colors are **one family — never introduce a second red**. The category split is assigned at the send sites: the tick engine's player-attack branch emits `combat-miss-out` / `combat-hit-out` / `combat-crit-out`, its kill and level-up lines emit `reward`, the NPC-attack branch emits `combat-miss-in` / `combat-hit-in` / `combat-crit-in` (v22; miss directions split by v23 B5 amendment 1, #152) (attack direction is stated in the words too — "You hit…" / "The X hits you…" — so color conveys nothing the text doesn't); the consumer's commerce/pickup/loot confirmations emit `success`. Engagement, focus, aggro, flee, and fallen lines keep generic `combat`. Directional arrows on combat lines (`→`/`←`) were **struck during the design pass — do not implement** without a new ruling.

**Key/value palette vocabulary (v21 brief 1).** Two named CSS variables in `game.html`'s `:root` are THE v21 palette vocabulary — **`--key-color: #7FB3D5`** (the #39 structural-header blue) and **`--value-color: #E8E4D8`** (the room-content near-white). No new hex values were introduced: the variables name existing ones, and `.msg-section strong` / `.room-content` were refactored onto them with zero visual change. (`--loc-room` shares the value-color hex by coincidence of ruling and stays independently declared per #1's location-bar ruling.) Two message classes consume them: `.msg-key` and `.msg-value`.

**Structured key/value report form (v21 brief 1 — #90, #91, #92).** The `report` category has an optional structured form: a report message MAY carry a `lines` array instead of `text` — each entry `{'k': …}` (key-only line), `{'v': …}` (value-only line), `{'k': …, 'v': …}` (one line, key then value), or `{}` (blank line). The server helper is `SkylandConsumer.send_report_lines(lines)` (stamps `ts` like every send). The client renders each entry as one output line — `k` in `.msg-key`, `v` in `.msg-value`, concatenated — and **every `v` portion passes through `appendFlaggedText()`, so item flag blocks keep their rarity/chrome colorization inside value-colored lines** (binding design rule). Adopted by **`help`** (amendment 1 — see Section 4.14) and three state commands: **`inv`** (three key headers `Equipment:` / `Inventory (N/M items):` / `Wallet:`, blank lines between sections, every other line a value), **`stats`** (key header `Character Stats:`, then the value Player line `Player: <name> - Level <N> <Origin> <Archetype>` — `origin` joined via `get_character_fresh`'s `select_related`; everything under the header is value-colored including stat labels — a subkey-color was explicitly deferred; `spend` with no args reuses `_send_stats` and inherits the form; **v22 brief 5 (#100): each of the six stat rows shows base with the gear parenthetical from `gear_stat_bonus` — `Strength     (STR): 25 (+3)` — rendered only when the gear sum is nonzero, signed, in the same value-color; no new palette entries. Amendment 1 adds the Armor row after the six stat rows — `Armor: 13 (blocks 21%)` — TAV from `total_armor_value` with the percentage derived live as `round(100 × TAV/(TAV + ARMOR_MITIGATION_K))`, never a hardcoded copy; naked renders `Armor: 0` with no parenthetical (zeros never hidden, nothing blocked to report); one equipped-set query (`get_stats_gear_context`) feeds both the parentheticals and the row; amendment 2 separates the row from the six stats with one blank line — it reads as its own group. Declined by ruling: no persistent armor readout in the right pane — the stats row and the per-hit receipts cover the need**), and **`wallet`** (one `{'k': 'Wallet:', 'v': ' <contents>'}` line, content unchanged). `report` messages carrying only `text` render exactly as before (who, examine, vendor list, travel listing, brief query are NOT converted; help joined the structured form in amendment 1), and `report` remains unstamped.

**Rarity scale — flag block only.** The client colorizes the #48 flag block inside any message text (`appendFlaggedText()`, a presentational span-wrapper over the unchanged composed text): Common `#9C9A90`, Uncommon `#5FA8D3`, Rare `#B387E8`, Epic `#f0c060` (the say-color gold — deliberate reuse, amendment 1 #124), Legendary `#E0724A`, Artifact `#E24B4A`; brackets, commas, and `Bound`/`Unbound` render `#9C9A90` (bind word renamed in v21 brief 1, #60 — the client's `FLAG_RE` changed in lockstep). The flag block keeps this colorization in every context, including inside v21's value-colored report lines. Composition itself (Section 4.6) is untouched.

**Look-command output sections (#14/#39; amendment 1 #77/#78).** `look` and room entry render in this exact order — prose first, `Exits:`, `Who's here?`, `What's here?`, then the zone-colored separator — with a blank line between sections and **empty sections omitted entirely**. **There is no bracket header** (amendment 1, #77): the render begins with the description prose; place identity lives in the location bar alone (the transcript argument for a header is void because the pane clears on every room entry, ruled to stay that way). The room render is **one structured `room-render` message**: `area_text` / `area_color` / `room_text` (v21 brief 1, #86 — the prose travels as separate fields; the old concatenated `text` field is gone), `exits` (comma list, one line after its label), `players`, `who`/`what` string arrays, and `zone_color` — the current `Zone.theme_color`, riding the render itself so a cross-zone move colors the separator correctly (the status payload that also carries it arrives after the render). **Prose colorization (#86, superseding the prose-color portion of #1's D1 narrowing):** the client renders `area_text` (long form only; None in brief mode) as its own block in the Area's `area_color` (fallback value-color), a blank line, then `room_text` in `var(--value-color)` — each prose level matches its location-bar segment color; the old fixed `.msg-room-render` prose color no longer applies to these blocks (the class remains as the block container). The Zone `description` field remains unrendered by explicit ruling. **The separator (#78; height re-ruled in v21 brief 1 amendment 1, #97):** the client closes every room render with a solid 3 px bar (rounded ends 1.5 px — reduced from 5 px so it reads lighter than the 5 px pane borders), `zone_color` fill at ~0.75 opacity, margins bringing the divider to at least one text line's height — once per render, after the final room-block line, `aria-hidden` (decorative), part of the unstamped room-render block; it appears nowhere else. `Who's here?` lists living non-fixture NPCs, one **introduction-composed, sentence-capitalized bare noun phrase** per line — no `is here.` suffix since v21 brief 1 (#55) (`A black bear`, `Morra`, `One of the Matron's brood` — introductions per amendment 1, #79); **`What's here?` is THE things/items section**: fixture NPCs (same introduction composition, same bare form), corpse lines (the capitalized corpse display name alone — no `lies here.` suffix, #55), and ground items as composed item lines (identical lines collapsed to `xN`) — the brief-3 interim `On the ground:` section is absorbed; exactly one items section exists. Every structural section-header label, present and future, renders `#7FB3D5` (`Exits:`, `Who's here?`, `What's here?`, `Players here:`, and any later label); labels and only labels use that color. The Verdant Shard was flagged `is_fixture=True` in the seed so it renders under `What's here?` per the brief's ruled example. Brief mode is unchanged; `look` always long (standing rule).

**Aggro-room arrival (#81 — v21 brief 1).** Entering a room with aggressive NPCs renders the room **exactly like any other entry, in BOTH arrival paths** (cardinal movement and flee-into-aggro): first the full room render (`send_room_description(destination, entering=True, …)` — pane clear, area/room prose, exits, who's-here listing the attackers, separator; the flee path fetches the fully-related room via `get_current_room()` first), then the snarl lines with **`introduction=False`** — the who's-here section just introduced the NPC, so the snarl is a second mention and takes the definite article (`The cave centipede snarls and moves to attack!`) — then `start_combat` / `send_fight` / `send_status_refresh`, so the combat-red status is the final status the client receives (the render sends its own status payload earlier), and `send_map()` last. Before v21 the aggro branch skipped the render entirely, leaving the previous room's description on screen. The ambush reads as: you see the room, and it sees you.

**v22 brief 2 — three-layer categories, named palette completions, Kinds, say and crit prose (DD §§2/3/6/9/13; #121, #54, #98).** The palette gains three named variables — `--warn-color: #E8D44D` (world declined), `--say-color: #f0c060` (the named form of the old `--chat`, which is deleted), `--loot-color: #4caf7d` (the named form of the reward green) — and the chrome gray `#8A887F` is deleted from the file (its users joined `var(--muted)`, including the location-bar separators). `.msg-error` reads `var(--error)` — the hard-coded amber is dead (#121); `.msg-warn` is new. **Speech** is `Name: message` in say-color for players and NPCs alike — the `[say] ` prefix is dead at both sites; bare `say` prompts; the speaker still receives their own broadcast (double vision is intentional; echo-off is the remedy). **Crit prose (#54):** the `[Critical]` bracket is dead at both tick-engine sites — on the authored-flavor paths the word lives in the damage clause (`… for a critical 28 damage!`); the weapon path's prose already conformed; NPC crits emit the new `combat-crit-in`. **Information output standards (DD §9):** three Kinds — Kind 1 `Key: Value`, Kind 2 key header + indented values, Kind 3 key header + table (muted column headers, value rows); the header punctuation law (colon = value on the line, ellipsis = structure below); headers uniformly key-color with embedded counts (`Inventory (12/250)...`). Report lines gained a third form — `{'segs': [{'t', 'c'}]}` — segments naming a palette voice (key/value/muted/error/warn/say/reward/rar-*/flag-chrome), used by the Kind-3 tables for muted headers, band-colored durability (derived from the mechanical durability band: no penalty → value, penalty → say, broken → error), and rarity-colored words. `inv` renders the Equipment paper-doll (all 14 slot rows always, anatomical `SLOT_ORDER` head→feet with two Ring rows, sentence-case labels, muted `-` empties, `Slot/Name/Details` columns), the Inventory table (`Slot/Name/Quantity/Details`, flat alphabetical), and the shared wallet line — one renderer, byte-identical between `wallet` and inv's Wallet section. `list` renders `Slot / Name / Details / Price` (amendment 1 #123 — no Quantity column; Details is rarity only; free group first with muted `free`), `who` is one line (`Players online (3): …`, #98), `stats` gains a blank line before Unspent, `look` is untouched. **Amendment 1 (#123/#124/#120):** the Slot cell on the vendor list and the Inventory table names the item's equip slot (sentence-case label; muted `-` when slotless — the paper-doll was always populated); `--success-color` is loot-color's general-purpose alias and the four combat colors carry named variables (`--hit-out-color`/`--crit-out-color`/`--hit-in-color`/`--crit-in-color`); the dead `--bar-v`/`--bar-a`/`--bar-l`/`--combat` declarations are deleted; `apps/shyland/version.py` holds `SHYLAND_VERSION` (currently `22.0-DEV`) — help always ends with a blank line and the Kind-1 `Version:` line, and the version-closeout ritual bumps the constant to the release stamp alongside the GDD and architecture stamps (point releases bump it on main).

**Chart-as-license and the palette conformance test (v22 B2 amendment 4 — standing law).** The color chart is not a description of the colors in use — it is the license to use them; a color literal not on the chart or the documented chrome list is a defect by definition. Enforcement: `tests/test_b2_amendment4.py` extracts every color literal from `game.html` (value-position hex forms and rgb()/rgba(), `#RGB` normalized to `#RRGGBB`, case-insensitive) and asserts **set equality** against its `ALLOWED_COLORS` constant — a new color appearing fails, and a licensed color disappearing fails, so every palette change is a deliberate two-place edit (CSS + allowlist) traceable in one diff. The allowlist's Chrome group (backgrounds, borders, `--text`, `--accent`, connection dots, timestamp prefix, zone-color fallbacks, and the dead `--room`/`--system` declarations) is annotated `pending chart ruling` and queued for the design chat. The amendment also killed the report parchment: `.msg-report` and the `.msg-room-render` container render `var(--value-color)` — one content voice.

**Command echo (#15).** Every command the player submits echoes into their own transcript as `> <the command as typed>` (category `echo`) **before** its result — including unrecognized commands and dying-gate refusals (the echo fires in `receive_json` ahead of the gate, right after the empty-input check). It is a **displayed-prefix category**: stamped, carrying and displaying the timestamp prefix exactly as output categories do, governed by `timestamps on|off` identically. It is a transcript of the player, not game output — sent only via `send_output` to the submitting connection, never re-broadcast. `ping`/`complete` protocol messages are not commands and do not echo.

**NPC article grammar (#24 — migration 0029).** `NpcDefinition` gained two fields: `article` (`CharField(max_length=8, default='the', blank=True)` — blank for proper nouns: Morra, Aldric, Mother Tansy, VND-9, the checkpoint menders/traders) and `plural_phrase` (`CharField(max_length=64, blank=True)` — used **verbatim** as the composed reference for group-phrase names: `one of the Matron's brood`). `name` is now **article-free by authoring law, enforced in the seed verify** (no NpcDefinition name begins with `a`/`an`/`the`; companion checks pin the proper-noun roster to blank articles and the Silk Matron/brood field shape). One helper composes **every player-visible NPC reference**: `combat_utils.npc_display(npc, capitalize=False)` — `plural_phrase` if set, else `article + name`, else bare `name`; `capitalize=True` uppercases only the first character for sentence-initial use; it accepts an `NpcInstance` or an `NpcDefinition`. `npc_display_name` (the ordinal helper) is rebuilt on it — ordinal between article and name (`the second cave spider`); proper nouns and phrase names never take an ordinal. **Templates never prepend their own articles.** Call sites composed through the helper(s): all tick-engine combat lines (hit/miss/crit/kill/focus-shift/slain broadcasts), aggro engagement lines (`The Silk Matron snarls and moves to attack!`), room occupant lines, corpse lines, dialogue attributions and connectives, kibitz, pity-repair fallback, vendor `list`, repair narration, `examine`, targeting messages, the fight-info feed, and the grammar resolver's `_NpcAccessor` (both `tokens` — so `attack the silk matron` matches — and `base_name`, so disambiguation lists read composed). The unarmed message pools were rewritten `{attacker}`-driven (no hardcoded subjects/articles; the vale/flats/ridge species pools now share one template text and compose the right name per attacker), and `get_unarmed_message`'s hard fallback no longer prepends `The`. `Corpse.npc_name_snapshot` now stores the **composed reference** at death (`the Silk Matron`, `one of the Matron's brood`), so corpse lines stay grammatical even after definition deletion. Seed data pass: 17 seeded names rewritten article-free (Obelisk, Primordial Sphere, Verdant Sphere/Obelisk/Shard, the six bosses, the six brood-style minions — the last carrying `plural_phrase`); the acceptance strings render exactly (`You land a critical hit on one of the Matron's brood for 64 damage.` / `You hit the Silk Matron for 22 damage.` / `The Silk Matron snarls and moves to attack!`). **Rider:** `Area.theme_color` for Spinner's Hollow authored `#C8CBB8` (pale web-green), replacing the `#CCCCCC` default, with a seed-verify color assertion alongside the others.

**Indefinite introductions (amendment 1, #79 — migration 0030).** `NpcDefinition.indefinite_article` (`CharField(max_length=8, default='a', blank=True)`) supplies the article for **first-presentation contexts** — exactly two template families: room occupant lines and aggro-engagement lines — via `npc_display(npc, capitalize=True, introduction=True)`. A **blank** `indefinite_article` means "no indefinite introduction": proper nouns, bosses, and unique landmarks (the Obelisks, the Spheres) fall back to their definite/bare composition, which keeps the #24 acceptance strings byte-identical (`The Silk Matron snarls and moves to attack!`) while commons introduce indefinite (`A giant cave spider snarls and moves to attack!`). `plural_phrase` names use the phrase verbatim, capitalized at sentence start. Every other context keeps the definite article, unchanged. Data pass: commons default `'a'`, the elder cave trio carry `'an'`, proper nouns/bosses/landmarks/phrase-named minions blank; the seed verify asserts non-null `indefinite_article` on every definition plus elder/boss/common pins, and the no-leading-article name law is unchanged.

**Corpse decay and empty-loot messaging (#28).** A corpse-decay line is **not sent to any player currently in an active combat session** — dropped, not queued or deferred; non-fighting players in the room still see it (`system` gray). Mechanism: the tick engine passes the room's fighting-character pks as `exclude_pks` on the decay broadcast, and the consumer's `room_message` handler drops the message for excluded members (a third exclusion mechanism alongside `exclude` channel-name and `exclude_pk`). Decay timing and loot mechanics are unchanged. Empty-corpse responses: looting a corpse that **never had loot to give** (definition has no loot table and no coin capacity — normal-tier NPCs drop nothing by design) answers `The <npc> carried nothing worth taking.`; a corpse **this player emptied** keeps the distinct, accurate `You've already taken everything from the <npc>.` — both composed via `npc_display` (snapshot fallback if the definition is gone); the old `… is already empty.` wording is removed. A loot that just took the coin drop raises no complaint at all. The disposal broadcast (`The corpse of … slowly disappears.`) now uppercases only its first character instead of `str.capitalize()`, which had lowercased proper nouns.

**Filed for future design (amendment 1, issue #80 — no v20 code):** item identification visibility ("knowledge by holding") — drop's `is_identified = False` re-veil is deliberate but the system it fed was never built; the ruled direction (examine reveals without pickup, pickup unlocks, drop re-veils) is captured unmilestoned in #80. **Shipped in v24.11 brief 1 — see the identification rule in Section 6.**

**Tests.** `tests/test_output_messaging.py`: `npc_display` composition (article/indefinite/proper/phrase/capitalize/definition-input), the amendment-1 introduction context (common/an/blank-fallback/proper/phrase), ordinal interaction, unarmed-fallback article law, and grammar matching/disambiguation over composed references. `tests/test_dispatch_guard.py` asserts echo-then-result ordering for both crashing and unknown commands. The 30-case grammar table is untouched.
