### 4.17 NPC voice pools (`apps/shyland/npc_voice.py`) — v23 brief 4, #40 code half

The home of every transactional and reactive flavor pool: each line that used to be a single hardcoded string lives here as a module-level list, selected by **`pick(pool, **fields)`** — plain `random.choice` plus `{placeholder}` substitution, the kibitz-style pattern that already existed. No per-player last-line state is tracked: the lines are low-frequency enough that consecutive repeats are acceptable, and stateful selection would be novel architecture the #40 ruling excluded. (The seed-data dialogue engine — `DialogueEntry` and friends, Section 4.1 — is a separate system; Brief 5's content lands there, not here.)

**Pool inventory:** `SELL_REFUSAL_SINGLE`/`_PARTIAL`/`_NONE` (+ the unwired `SELL_REFUSAL_FALLBACK` guard rail), `SELL_SINGLE`/`SELL_BULK`, `SELL_WORTHLESS_SINGLE`/`_TRAILING`, `BUY_SINGLE`/`BUY_BULK`, `SOLD_OUT`, `PITY_REPAIR_LINES` (per-NPC-slug pools for all seven repairers: morra, pella, ferwick, repairbot-prime, maro-the-mender, tavik-the-mender, old-brammel) + `PITY_REPAIR_FALLBACK` (future repairers), `REPAIR_SUCCESS/FAIL/POOR_BULK/_SINGLE`, `KIBITZ_LINES`, `AGGRO_ENGAGE` (all three engagement sites — consumer walk-in, consumer flee-in, tick-engine respawn — in lockstep; the respawn site keeps its #79 indefinite-introduction name composition; a guard test pins the retired literal to this module alone).

**The render rule (v23 brief 5, #147):** `SPEECH_ENTRY_TYPES` + `dialogue_line(entry_type, npc_name, text)` make this module the single home of the speech-vs-narration decision for delivered dialogue — keyword responses take the `Name: ` attribution and the say color; greeting and departed entries return verbatim at category `room`. The tuple mirrors `DialogueEntry.ENTRY_KEYWORD` under a lockstep test.

**The NO-LEAK RULE (standing, #138):** no line in this module may name or imply an item's rarity, tier, or true name — enforced by a standing test over the refusal pools. **The ruled exceptions (speech gets pooled; renderings stay stable):** the vendor list caption, the nothing-left-for-sale line, and the repair sweep summary are report/system-category renderings and stay single-stringed; `NpcDefinition.death_message` stays a scalar `TextField` (cut from #40's scope, 2026-07-24). Tests: `tests/test_npc_voice.py` — pool membership at the wired sites, the no-leak invariant, pity coverage, aggro lockstep, substitution completeness, and the #146 field-removal guard.

### 4.18 The read-only verification family (`verification.py`, `management/commands/verify_*.py`) — v24.29 brief 1, #249 Part 2

The in-container half of `make verify` / `make verify-prod`. Part 1 (shipped on main, `28db69e`) added the Make targets and the guard hook: `verify` runs one command against the current target after `crosscheck-env`; `verify-prod` is a posture-setting target in `deploy-prod`'s exact contract (pins its own `DOCKER_HOST`, refuses an ambient one, flips posture, runs, restores dev resting posture), operator-authorized and bare-only. Both gate `VERIFY` to the `verify_*` name. They shipped live but **inert** — no command existed to run. This release supplies the base class and the first command.

**`verification.py` — `VerificationCommand`.** A `BaseCommand` subclass that subclasses extend by implementing `verify()` rather than overriding `handle()`. Three contract points:

- **Nothing is written.** `handle()` runs the body inside `transaction.atomic()` and then raises a private sentinel to force the rollback, so any write the body performs — accidental or otherwise — is discarded rather than committed. This is the **runtime backstop** behind the `verify_*` name gate the Makefile enforces, and it is what makes pointing one of these at production safe.
- **Exit code is the outcome signal:** 0 clean, nonzero for findings or error, via `CommandError` — Django's own nonzero-exit mechanism, which exits 1 under `run_from_argv` and raises under `call_command`, so `make` and the test suite both see the failure. A failure is loud through the Make target rather than buried in stdout.
- **Findings are reported, never repaired.** No verification command may mutate state, even to "fix" what it finds. The rollback enforces this at runtime; it is also a review rule. A finding is information — the answer to it is a design ruling, not a write.

`verify()` writes its human-readable report to `self.stdout` and returns the findings: empty (or `None`) is clean.

**Deliberately Shyland-scoped**, not platform-shared: a shared home would be platform shared surface under CLAUDE.md Rule 2 and would need its own stop-and-flag, and Shyland is the only consumer today. It is a module, not a management command — `management/commands/` holds commands only.

**`verify_ladder`** — the first member, and the settlement of a debt. Its spec is verbatim V24.28 Brief 1 §7 step 8: count `ItemInstance` rows on a ladder definition whose `mk_tier` falls outside that definition's rung; expected 0. That survey went to production **unverified** because no session had a sanctioned path to run a read-only check there, which is what filed #248 and made this target exist. Ladder membership is `definition.tier_material_mk_min is not null` — a null minimum means not on the ladder at all, so the freebie kit's `suppress_mk_suffix` does not enrol it. A mismatch is `mk_tier` below the minimum, or above `tier_material_mk_max` **where that maximum is non-null**: sphaerium's null maximum is unbounded above and can never mismatch upward — null is not zero here. Clean output is one line reporting zero mismatches out of the ladder-row total; on findings it adds the offending definition slugs with their `mk_tier` values and exits nonzero. Dev result at this commit: **0 mismatches out of 8 ladder rows**.

#249 remains open for Part 3 — retiring the Instructions v33 interim rule that routes read-only prod inspection to the operator, now that a session path exists.

### 4.19 The MC sink (`mc.py`, `management/commands/run_mc_persister.py`, `MCEvent`) — v25.1 brief 1, #37/#271

**The emit helper and the discipline.** `apps/shyland/mc.py` is the single creation-level choke point for Monitoring and Command capture (GDD §10.11). It is standalone by design — game code imports `mc`, never the reverse. `mc_emit(kind, *, actor_id, actor_name, room_id, audience, data)` builds a flat record (`actor_id`/`room_id` as strings, `''` when none; `audience` and `data` JSON-encoded) and `XADD`s it to the stream; **the entire body sits in `try/except`** — a sink failure of any kind (Redis down, serialization error) drops the record and never raises into game code, logging at most one warning per 60 seconds (module-level throttle). No caller ever uses a return value. The choke-point discipline is structural and grep-enforced: in `consumers.py`, `channel_layer.group_send` may appear only inside the tapped wrapper `mc_group_send` (which derives the audience, emits, then performs the send with the payload unchanged); in `run_tick_engine.py`, only inside the two tapped outbound helpers `send_to_player` and `broadcast_to_room`.

**Kinds and the chrome boundary.** Four kinds: `cmd` (every submitted command, accepted or rejected — `{raw, verb, args}`, no outcome field, because rejections are themselves the stream-adjacent `out` records that follow — plus the `complete` *request* as `{'complete': text}`), `out` (every outbound creation event of every client type), `connect`, and `disconnect` (`{'code': code}`). Excluded as protocol chrome (ruled 2026-08-18): the `ping`/`pong` keepalive, the connect-time `verbs` list, and the `complete` *response*. Unattributable pre-entry traffic (no-character connect, unauthenticated close) emits nothing — there is no actor to attribute; the deleted-while-connected redirect (a known `character_pk`) IS captured. The single-session `token` is stripped from the superseded event's record, and no credential ever enters a record.

**Audience.** A field on every record, resolved at fan-out time: personal events carry `[character_pk]`; room broadcasts carry `mc.resolve_room_audience(room_id, exclude_pks)` — characters whose `current_room_id` matches, filtered to live presence keys (`MGET` on `shyland:online:{pk}`, the same mechanics as the ticker's `_online_character_pks`), minus excludes, sorted. All three exclude semantics are honored (`exclude` == own channel → own pk; `exclude_pk`; `exclude_pks`). A resolver failure yields `[]`, never an exception.

**The hot tier.** Redis Stream `mc:events` on db 0 — shared with the channel layer and the presence keys; the `mc:*` namespace collides with neither. Bounded by `XADD maxlen=settings.MC_STREAM_MAXLEN` (env-overridable, default 250000 since v25.2 — #33 ruling 8 raised the 100000 the sink shipped with for the combat family's volume; `approximate=True`). Volumeless by ruling: Redis persistence stays off — Postgres is the durability tier. The async client is lazy and module-level, built from `settings.REDIS_HOST`, and rebinds if the running event loop changes (redis.asyncio pools bind to their creating loop; Daphne and the ticker each live on one loop, test runs create many).

**The persister.** `run_mc_persister` — a sync loop with a sync `redis.Redis` client (a management command doing ORM writes needs no async), running as the `mc-persister` container (same image, `ticker`-mirrored compose block). It ensures consumer group `persister` (`XGROUP CREATE ... 0 MKSTREAM`, `BUSYGROUP` swallowed), then loops: `XAUTOCLAIM` of entries pending idle >60s (crash recovery) ahead of `XREADGROUP GROUP persister worker-1 COUNT 500 BLOCK 2000` on `>` (`BLOCK_MS` 5000 → 2000 in v25.4, #277 — the b812afb egress fix mirrored: a server-side BLOCK equal to redis-py ≥ 8's default 5s client `socket_timeout` is a coin-flip race every idle cycle; test-pinned at 2000, asserted < 5000). Batches map to `MCEvent` rows and land via `bulk_create(ignore_conflicts=True)` — the unique `stream_id` makes replay idempotent — with `XACK` only after a successful write; on DB failure it backs off and retries the same batch without acking. A malformed entry never crashes the loop: whatever parses is stored (raw fields into `data`, kind `malformed`), one warning, acked. SIGTERM is graceful — the in-flight batch finishes and the process exits 0.

**The durable record.** `MCEvent` (migration `0050`): `stream_id` (unique), `ts` (derived from the stream id's millisecond prefix, timezone-aware UTC, indexed), `kind`, `actor_id`/`room_id` as **loose `BigIntegerField`s plus denormalized `actor_name`** — deliberately no FKs into live tables (reseeds delete rows; the record survives), `audience`/`data` JSON. Composite indexes on `(kind, ts)`, `(actor_id, ts)`, `(room_id, ts)`. The admin registration is **read-only** — `has_add_permission`/`has_change_permission`/`has_delete_permission` all return `False` (append-only truth; nothing edits history) — introducing the repo's read-only-admin pattern, with kind filter, actor/data search, and a `ts` date hierarchy.

**#271 / #272.** No presence client hardcodes its endpoint anymore: `REDIS_HOST` became a settings constant (`base.py`), the consumer's presence URL builds from it, and the channel-layer and defender reads re-point at the same constant — zero behavior change at any current deployment; the MC client uses the constant from birth (#271). The second hardcode site found during implementation — the ticker's `_online_character_pks` presence reader — was filed as emergent #272 on operator direction and fixed in the same session: it builds from the constant too. Grep truth: no literal `redis://redis` endpoint remains anywhere in the app.

**The combat family (v25.2 brief 1, #33; GDD §10.11).** Seven kinds instrument combat at the internals — what the engine rolls, including what no player ever sees. Envelope discipline (ruling 6 on #33): every combat record's `data` carries `session` (the CombatSession pk) as the encounter join key; `audience=[]` always — these are observability records, addressed to no one; `room_id` = the session's room. Player-acted records carry the character as actor; NPC-acted records carry `actor_id=None` + `actor_name` = the NPC definition name, with instance/definition pks in `data`; session-scoped records (`combat_start`, `combat_join`, `combat_round`, `combat_flee`, `combat_death`, `combat_end`) carry the session's character.

- **`combat_start`** — one per `CombatSession` creation, from any site: `{session, room, zone, area?, origin: 'attack'|'aggro'|'flee_aggro'|'respawn_aggro', first_attacker, character, npcs}`. The character snapshot: `{id, name, level, archetype, origin, stats (effective six), gear_bonus (six), tav, vitality: [cur, max], acuity: [cur, baseline, band_low, band_high], longevity: [cur, max]}`; the NPC snapshot: `{instance, definition, slug, name, combat_tier, mk_tier, level, stats (dex/str/per/int), vitality: [cur, max]}`. Built by `combat_snapshot_character` / `combat_snapshot_npc` in `combat_utils` — not `mc.py`; the import direction is doctrine.
- **`combat_join`** — NPCs added to an already-active session: `{session, room, origin, npcs}` (snapshots of the newly added only; a join adding nothing new emits nothing).
- **`combat_round`** — one per resolved round, emitted after ordering is decided, before `execute_actions`: `{session, round (= tick_counter // COMBAT_ROUND_TICKS), basis}` — round 1 carries `basis='first_attacker'` + `first_attacker` (round 1 rolls no initiative); later rounds `basis='initiative'` + `character_roll {dex, per, die, total}`, `npc_rolls [{instance, dex, per, die, total}]`, `npc_avg`, `order ('character_first'|'npcs_first')`.
- **`combat_action`** — one per resolved attack action (skipped dead-target/dying-attacker continues emit nothing). Player→NPC: `{session, round, target {instance, definition, mk_tier}, focus, to_hit {die, attack_total, defense, margin, result, crit_chance?, crit_die?}}` (crit fields only when the hit landed and the crit roll ran) + on non-miss `damage {weapons [{instance, definition, slot, roll, stat, durability, factor, term}] | unarmed_base, stat_bonus, acuity_mod, hit_multiplier, raw, final}`, `gear_bonus {total, procs [{stat, value, chance, fired, rolled?}], flat}` (a graze never rolls the pool — the dict is honestly empty), `lifesteal` (when > 0 and the heal ran), `target_vitality [before, after]`, and on a kill `kill: true, xp, level_ups`. NPC→player: `{session, round, attacker {instance, definition, mk_tier}, to_hit {…}}` + on non-miss `damage {base_roll, str_basis [low, high], hit_multiplier, raw, pre_mitigation, tav, final}`, `effects [{name, chance, fired}]` (every `NpcEffect` candidate, fired or not; absent on the fall path — the effect pass never runs there), `target_vitality`, `target_fell`.
- **`combat_flee`** — one per rolled flee contest (the pre-contest refusals are already `cmd` records): `{session, character, dex, die, total, npc_avg_per, success}` + on a won contest `destination` (room pk) and `direction`, or `blocked: 'nowhere_to_run'` when the contest won but no exit existed.
- **`combat_death`** — two phases. The fall (from `execute_actions`, appended immediately after the killing action record): `{session, round, phase: 'fall', character, killer {instance, definition, mk_tier}}`. The death (from the dying loop, after `execute_death`): `{phase: 'death', character, home_room?, broken_items, sessions_closed}`.
- **`combat_end`** — one per session close, at every end site, outcome↔reason fixed: kill→win, sibling_kill→win, death→loss, flee→flee, stale/self_heal/empty/flee_empty→disengage: `{session, outcome, reason, rounds, duration_secs, npcs_remaining [{instance, vitality [cur, max]}]}` — `npcs_remaining` captured **before** `release_session_npcs` resets anything (empty on win closes). **`wipe`, GDD §10.11's fifth outcome word, is defined but never emitted:** while sessions hold exactly one character a wipe is indistinguishable from a loss; it becomes distinct only if multi-character sessions ship (#220's territory).

**The detailed-variant pattern and the equivalence guarantee.** Six roll helpers gained `<name>_detailed(...) → (existing_return_value, detail_dict)` forms — `roll_initiative`, `resolve_hit`, `calculate_damage`, `composite_weapon_term`, `roll_gear_bonus_damage`, `apply_npc_effects`. The original body moved into the detailed form; the plain name is a one-line delegation that discards the detail, so every existing caller is untouched. **Identical random calls in identical order is the hard contract**, pinned by seeded equivalence tests asserting both the return value and `random.getstate()` after the call — same value, same random-stream consumption, byte-identical player output. Combat paths call only the detailed forms (grep-enforced: zero plain-name call sites remain in `run_tick_engine.py`); the flee contest's previously-anonymous inline d20 got a name — the same single roll.

**Emission sites and the internals-first ordering contract (ruling 7).** A round's internals emit before the round's player-facing sends, in the exact order actions resolved; at every other site, emit-before-send. Sync code under `database_sync_to_async` never awaits — ready-to-emit record dicts accumulate and their async callers emit them first: `execute_actions` returns a 5-tuple ending in `mc_records` (flushed before the message/status/room flush), `start_combat` returns `(session, records)` with an `origin` parameter (its three callers — walk-in aggro, the attack command, flee-into-aggro — emit immediately after it returns), the ticker closers return their `combat_end` record (`close_session(reason)` for stale/empty, `self_heal_close`, the kill-path and sibling closes append inline), `end_combat_session(reason)` returns the flee/flee_empty close, `execute_death` returns the death-phase record plus one loss close per session it closed (the dying loop emits all before its sends), and the ticker's `engage` returns the respawn-engagement record (emitted before the engagement broadcasts).

**Volume.** Measured on the dev stack at ship time: a three-round kill fight produced 10 combat records in 9.7s ≈ 1.03 records/sec, against v25.1's non-combat baseline of 24 records/40s (0.6/sec) — the combat data point GDD §10.11's retention posture was waiting for, and the basis for ruling 8's `MC_STREAM_MAXLEN` raise to 250000.

---

### 4.20 The MC egress (`mc_consumer.py`) — v25.3 brief 1, #267; the agent door — v25.5 brief 1, #281; the attach singleton — v25.10 brief 1, #304

**The endpoint.** `wss://<host>/ws/shyland/mc/` → `MCEgressConsumer` (`AsyncJsonWebsocketConsumer`), registered as one line in `game_mvc/routing.py` (an operator-approved shared-surface touch; nginx needed nothing — the existing `location /ws/` block proxies by prefix). Through it, remote agents attach to the MC event stream — session auth, hot-window replay, then live tailing — and, since v25.5 (#281), speak the **query** and **action** vocabularies of the agent door (the frame contract below; the machinery in Section 4.22). **The v25.3 read-only law narrowed to the tail** (ruled supersession): the tail itself still causes no game action, but query/action frames read and mutate the world — every one on the record. The endpoint is gated by the MC kill switch (#266/#281 — the kill-switch gates below and Section 4.21). Module constants: `MC_PROTOCOL = 2` (1 → 2 at v25.5), `MAX_FRAME_ID_LEN = 64`, `REPLAY_BATCH = 500` (matching the persister's `BATCH_COUNT`), `LIVE_BLOCK_MS = 2000` — deliberately inside the reused client's 5s socket read cap (redis-py ≥ 8 defaults `socket_timeout=5`): a server-side BLOCK equal to the client-side read cap is a coin-flip race every idle cycle, and the loser tears down the connection (found by operator playtest — the live tail died after ~18 idle 5s/5s cycles).

**The gate.** Access = live `agents.shyland` Group membership at connect, checked on the `check_shyland_admin` shape (`@database_sync_to_async`, `groups.filter(...).exists()`, no session caching — revocation is instant). The group is created by data migration `0051` (the 0034 pattern: idempotent `get_or_create`, no members seeded); agent service accounts are provisioned operationally by the operator, never by code, seed, or migration. Three connect outcomes: unauthenticated → handshake rejected (`close()` before accept — a custom code cannot be delivered pre-accept in Channels); authenticated non-member → accept-then-close with **4403** (so the code reaches the client); member → accept, `hello`, stream on request. **The gate is the group, not a character:** agent accounts have no `Character`, the consumer never touches the character table, and nothing routes to the character creator.

**The wire protocol** (all frames JSON objects with a `type` field). Server → client: `hello {protocol}` immediately on accept; `event {id, kind, actor_id, actor_name, room_id, audience, data}` — one per stream entry, replay and live alike, in stream order; `gap {requested, oldest}` — once, before replay, when the attach cursor predates the window (`oldest` null = window empty); `pong` (nonce echoed verbatim when the ping carried one); `result {id, ok, data | error [, detail]}` — one per query/action frame (v25.5); `error {error: "unknown-frame"}` (the ruled v25.5 supersession of v25.3's `read-only` string). Client → server: `attach` (bare = live from now) / `attach {after}` (replay everything after the id — exclusive — then live) / `ping` / `query {id, q, params}` / `action {id, act, params}` (v25.5 — `id` a client string ≤64 chars, echoed verbatim; missing/non-string/over-long ⇒ `result` with `id: null`, `error: "bad-frame"`; unknown kinds ⇒ `unknown-query`/`unknown-action`; malformed params ⇒ `bad-params`; an unexpected handler failure ⇒ `internal`, the frame refused, the connection surviving). **The vocabulary is closed:** anything else, a second `attach` included, draws the error frame and is otherwise ignored — the connection stays open. Query/action frames check the kill switch fresh **before** processing, per frame — killed ⇒ close 4503, same as the tail sever. `_handle_request` emits one `agent_query`/`agent_action` MC record after every processed frame (the §6 envelope discipline: `actor_name` = the agent's username, `actor_id`/`room_id`/`audience` empty; `data = {q|act, params, ok[, error][, result]}` — `result` on successful actions only), fire-and-forget. Field decoding lives in the module-level `entry_to_frame(stream_id, fields)` (unit-testable without a socket): bytes→str, empty-string `actor_id`/`room_id` → null else int, `audience`/`data` json-decoded — **a field that fails to parse passes through as its raw string; a malformed entry never kills the connection.**

**Cursors and gaps.** Agents own their cursors — server reads are stateless `XRANGE`/`XREAD`; consumer groups remain the persister's mechanism alone, and no server-side per-agent state exists. Stream-id comparison parses both dash-separated parts numerically, never as strings. Resume from an id older than the window's oldest (trim and Redis restart are the same symptom) announces one `gap` frame, then replays from oldest — **gaps are announced, never silent**; deep replay is never served on this socket (Postgres holds history). Replay runs in `XRANGE` batches of `REPLAY_BATCH` with exclusive `(`-prefixed starts. **The live tail reads from a concrete id, never `XREAD $`:** the `$` cursor silently drops entries landing between one XREAD returning and the next call, and a task-deferred snapshot would misclassify events emitted immediately after attach as history — so the bare attach's "now" (the stream's tail id, `XREVRANGE ... COUNT 1`, `0-0` when empty) is resolved inside attach handling, making a ping/pong fence after attach a hard delivery guarantee. The tail runs as an asyncio task on the consumer, cancelled on disconnect.

**Boundaries.** Backpressure never reaches the game: `mc_emit` is untouched, the consumer holds zero references to it (grep-enforced), and egress reads never block or slow emission — a slow agent is Daphne/nginx's problem. Egress connections are **not** captured as stream events (they are not in-world events; the `connect`/`disconnect` kinds are player presence): attach and detach get `shyland.mc` logger lines carrying the agent username, and fleet observability belongs to #268's arc. **`mc._get_client` is deliberately reused, not renamed:** the consumer calls it as a module attribute, so every existing and new `mock.patch.object(mc, '_get_client', ...)` test patch governs egress reads too — zero existing patch sites moved (the V25.2 D1 lesson, avoided by construction).

**The kill-switch gates (v25.4 brief 1, #266).** Three enforcement points, all reading through the module-level `switch_killed()` helper (`database_sync_to_async` over `MCKillSwitch.is_killed`; **any read exception returns killed — fail closed**; the consumer's no-character-table law is untouched — `MCKillSwitch` is not the character table). **Connect:** after the `check_shyland_agent()` pass and before accept — order deliberate, so a non-member sees 4403 whether or not the switch is engaged (the switch leaks nothing to non-members); a killed member draws accept-then-close **4503** (the 4403 pattern — the code must reach the client). Close-code vocabulary: **4403** = not authorized, **4503** = killed — killed is not not-authorized. **Replay:** the same check per batch iteration in `_replay` — a kill during a long catch-up severs without waiting for replay to finish (`None` back to `_stream` marks the connection already closed, so the live tail never starts). **Live:** the check at the top of every `_live` iteration — the loop wakes at least every `LIVE_BLOCK_MS` (2s), so a hung or rogue agent is severed within ~2s without its cooperation. Cost: one indexed single-row read per wake per agent (single-digit agents) — deliberately uncached per the ruling. Section 4.21 holds the switch itself.

**The attach singleton (v25.10 brief 1, #304).** One agent account = one attached connection — hard reject, **never takeover**. The module-level `ATTACHED` registry (agent username → `channel_name`) is **in-process by design**, deliberately not Redis: the registry must die with the process that owns the connections it guards, making a stale lock structurally impossible (the `django` container runs a single Daphne process — no worker fan-out). Gate order in `connect` is fixed: auth → membership (4403) → kill switch (4503) → **singleton (4409)** — a killed door reports killed, not conflict; a non-member sees 4403 either way (the leak law). A duplicate is accepted, sent `{'type': 'error', 'error': 'already-attached', 'detail': 'Another connection for <username> is already attached.'}`, closed 4409, and logged (`egress attach refused — already attached`) — **without** claiming and without setting `_agent`; the winner claims **before** accepting. `disconnect` releases under the guarded-presence-delete discipline: the entry is deleted only when `ATTACHED.get(self._agent) == self.channel_name` — a rejected duplicate's teardown cannot disturb the holder's claim. Freed-slot guarantee (verified, recorded on #304): a clean stop or `kill -9` of the holder closes its socket at the OS level → Channels fires `disconnect` immediately → slot freed; a truly half-dead peer is reaped by Daphne's default WS ping (20s interval / 30s timeout) within ~50s; a Django restart destroys registry and connections together. No other consumer behavior changed — tail/query/action handling and the 4403/4503 paths are byte-identical.

---

### 4.21 The MC kill switch (`MCKillSwitch`, `mc_emit_sync`, the `mc` command) — v25.4 brief 1, #266/#277

**The model.** `MCKillSwitch` (migration `0052`, schema-only — no data migration, no seeded row): a database singleton — row pk=1 always, **absent row = alive** (the designed default state), fields `killed` (`BooleanField`, default False), `flipped_at` (nullable `DateTimeField`), `flipped_by` (`CharField(64)`). Postgres is the only store whose state survives restarts and reseeds — which is why the switch lives there and nowhere else. **Config, not history:** editable surfaces are sanctioned (the `mc` command, the shell helper, the Django admin); the append-only rule governs `MCEvent`, not this row. `is_killed()` is a fresh single-row read on every call — no cache, no TTL, no module-level state, no cross-process invalidation story, by ruling — and **raises on DB failure**: enforcement sites catch and treat any error as killed (fail closed; the game degrading to "no AI" is the shipped game).

**The flip choke point.** `MCKillSwitch.flip(killed, *, by, surface, actor_id=None)` — every surface routes here, and it is the documented shell helper (via `make shell`: `MCKillSwitch.flip(True, by='<operator>', surface='shell')`). `get_or_create(pk=1)`, no-change short-circuit returning False, then the write (`update_fields`) and **one `mc_kill` record per actual state change** — `data={'killed': bool, 'surface': str}`, the flipper as `actor_name`, the flipping character's pk as `actor_id` on the command surface; **a no-change flip emits nothing** (there is no state change to record). The emit rides **`mc_emit_sync`** in `mc.py` — `mc_emit`'s sync twin for sync creation sites (shell, Django admin, the command's ORM path): byte-compatible record shape (the persister maps it with zero changes), the same whole-body fire-and-forget guard and throttled warning, a short-lived sync `redis.Redis` client per call (flips are rare by definition). The import direction holds: `models.py` imports from `mc` inside `flip` (function-level — migration-time imports stay clean); `mc.py` imports no game code.

**The `mc` admin command.** `mc <status|kill|restore>` — `admins.shyland`-gated with **the fn-18 stealth machinery extended, never reimplemented**: `'mc'` in `ADMIN_VERBS` alone buys the central `_dispatch` gate (byte-identical unknown-command response for non-members) and the connect-time verb-list subtraction; the `COMMAND_TABLE` row, the `DYING_ALLOWED` entry (allowed while dying; absent from `COMBAT_BLOCKED`, so allowed in combat — GDD §9.1), the members-only help row (the 4-element `HELP_SECTIONS` form, alphabetical between `loot` and `pickup`), and a membership-gated completion branch (`kill`/`restore`/`status` for members, nothing for non-members — completion never leaks the pool) complete the six touch points. `cmd_mc` prefix-matches a closed subcommand vocabulary (first letters distinct — ambiguity impossible; renderings never vary). Voices per the three-layer doctrine: the usage line = CLI error (error); already-in-state = world declined (warn); an actual flip = world answered (success); `status` = an unstamped Kind-1 key/value report line.

**The Django admin.** `MCKillSwitchAdmin` is **editable** — the editable-config exception beside the read-only `MCEvent` pattern (config, not history). `save_model` routes through `MCKillSwitch.flip` and deliberately does not call `super()` — `flip` performs the write, keeping the no-change-no-emit rule intact even from the admin form; `surface='django-admin'`, `by` = the admin username. Add permission only while no row exists; delete disabled.

**The boundary law.** **Capture never checks the switch:** no `mc_emit` call site, consumer tap, ticker tap, or the persister has any switch awareness (grep-enforced: zero `is_killed`/`MCKillSwitch` hits in `run_mc_persister.py` and `run_tick_engine.py`). The persister is monitoring infrastructure inside the trust boundary, not an actor — its only v25.4 change is **#277**: `BLOCK_MS` 5000 → 2000, mirroring the egress constant's b812afb fix (a server-side BLOCK equal to redis-py ≥ 8's default 5s client `socket_timeout` is a coin-flip race every idle cycle; 2s block, 5s cap, no race), with both block constants pinned at 2000 and asserted < 5000 by `test_mc_kill_switch.py`. Enforcement is egress-only (Section 4.20's kill-switch gates); the switch shipped **before** the first actor by design. **v25.5 (#281) extends the coverage over the door:** every `query`/`action` frame reads the switch fresh before processing (the same `switch_killed()` helper, fail closed) — killed ⇒ close 4503 on the frame, both frame types tested. Capture still never checks the switch (the boundary law above is untouched).

---

### 4.22 The agent door (`mc_door.py`) — v25.5 brief 1, #281/#273; granular item control — v25.7 brief 1, #287/#288/#289/#293; memory, rooms, record search, report — v25.8 brief 1, #294/#290/#300/#296; structural receipts — v25.9 brief 1, #302; the report family — v25.10 brief 1, #306; the durability posture — v25.12 brief 1, #311/#314

**The module.** All query/action machinery behind `MCEgressConsumer`'s protocol-2 dispatch: sync DB functions called via `database_sync_to_async`, the audited send helper, the artifact builder, and the narration senders. **Import direction (ruled):** `mc_door` may import from `consumers`/`item_utils`/`models`/`mc` and their kin; nothing imports `mc_door` except `mc_consumer`. Refusals raise `DoorError(code, detail)` — the consumer maps them onto the `result` frame. Character-name params resolve `iexact`; no match ⇒ `not-found`. Presence reads use the same `shyland:online:{pk}` keys as everything else, quiet-False on failure (offline is the norm, never an error).

**The audited send.** `audited_send(group, event, *, agent_name, room_id)` is the door's delivery choke point, mirroring `mc_group_send`'s discipline (Section 4.3): emit the `out` record first — audience = the target pks, resolved at fan-out time honoring `exclude_pk`/`exclude_pks` via `mc.resolve_room_audience` — then `channel_layer.group_send` with the payload unchanged. **No bare `group_send` anywhere in door code.** The door has no channel name, so room broadcasts exclude the affected player **by pk** (`room_message` has honored delivery-side `exclude_pk` since v20).

**Queries** (§4.1 of the brief, table-authoritative): `commands` — the connect-time derivation exactly (`set(DIRECTIONS) | set(COMMAND_TABLE)`), `ADMIN_VERBS` listed separately (agents see the full vocabulary; the split lets a bot model stealth); `who_online` — presence keys, pks parsed from key names, names via `parse_presence_name`; `where_is` / `character` — the `{id, name, area, zone}` room shape, `character` adding base + effective stats (via `combat_utils.effective_stats`), bars, copper, unspent points; `items` — definition catalog, `icontains` filter, name-ordered, capped 50 with a `truncated` flag; `is_admin` — live `admins.shyland` membership (#273's query surface, never cached); `inventory` (v25.7, #293) — the uncapped owner-wide roster (`char.inventory.select_related('definition').order_by('pk')`): every instance the character owns, equipped included and flagged (`is_equipped`/`equipped_slot`), each row `{id, slug, name, item_type, mk_tier, rarity, durability_current, is_broken, is_soulbound, is_equipped, equipped_slot}` — a state report, deliberately unlike the player `inventory` command's protective pools; no cap, no truncation flag (`ITEMS_CAP` stays a catalog concern); `item` (v25.7, #293/#289) — one instance by `item_id` at full fidelity: the roster fields plus rolled stats, the damage pair, and the curse/identification **true state** (`is_cursed`, `curse_identified`, `is_identified`, `is_unidentifiable` exactly as stored — the no-leak rule governs world speech, which queries never touch; the definition's real name renders despite any mystery veil), with holder context (`owner {id, name}` or null; `room` via the standard room shape or null).

**Actions.** `answer` (#273's delivery gate): target must be a live `admins.shyland` member (`not-admin` otherwise — authoritative regardless of what the bot concluded); online delivery is `sudo: {text}` as category `sudo` through the audited send; offline is `{delivered: false}` with `ok: true` — silence is the norm. `gift`: definition by slug, rarity from `RARITY_CHOICES` minus artifact (`artifact-requires-create`), `generate_item_instance(..., gift=True)` — the first production caller of the soulbind-on-gift path; the Mk-mismatch guard's `ValueError` surfaces as `invalid-item` with its message; **capacity deliberately unchecked** (an admin gift lands regardless of carry state — recorded design point); online recipients get the DD §6 giving line (`An admin has given you {item_ref(item)}.`, category `reward`). `create_artifact`: the §5.4 spec validated key-by-key (strict — unknown keys refused; rarity-word names refused at runtime per the #22 authoring law; mystery pair required together with `is_unidentifiable`), then the hand-authored `ItemDefinition` (`scaling_base`/`scaling_factor` 0.0 — required floats, meaningless for hand-authored stats; `suppress_mk_suffix` False; slug slugified, collisions ⇒ `name-taken` case-insensitively) + the artifact `ItemInstance` (never through `generate_item_instance` — forbidden for artifacts; born soulbound to the recipient; unidentifiable artifacts born unidentified), atomically — `effect` wiring deliberately excluded from the day-one spec. `strip`: snapshot written **before** any unequip, `_unequip_blocked_reason` deliberately bypassed (admin tool; the #275 over-capacity state accepted knowingly, ruled 2026-08-22), one `rescale_bars_for_gear` after the loop. `dress`: restores each surviving, still-owned, unequipped snapshot entry to its exact slot — byte-consistent with `equip_item` (re-soulbinds) — one rescale after the loop, `{restored, missing}` reported, **the snapshot always consumed** (a `finally` sets it null whatever the outcome). `move`: exactly one of `to_name`/`to_room_id`; refuses `in-combat` (no landmines in the combat model); **online targets**: the DB move, then the `cmd_move` sentence shapes to both rooms (pk-excluded) and the `moved` `player_message` (`An admin moved you to a new room.`, operator-authored verbatim, category `system`) — the consumer's respawn-modeled `moved` branch re-seats groups, **records the visit** (deferred to it by the door so a first visit announces zone completion exactly like a walked arrival), renders the room, sends the map; **offline targets**: DB update + visit (`record_room_visit_sync`) only, no broadcasts. Strip/dress narration rides a `refresh_status` event — a sibling consumer branch re-syncs the status pane from DB truth.

**The item actions (v25.7 — #287/#288/#289).** Shared addressing on every write kind: params `name` + `item_id`; `_owned_item` fetches and validates inside the handler's single `transaction.atomic()` block — missing instance ⇒ `not-found`, owned by anyone else or unowned ⇒ `not-owner` with the detail naming the actual state (the other owner's name, or "no character (room floor or corpse)"). **No string-based item resolution exists on any write path** — the bot resolves via `inventory` first; a stale id is a refusal, never a guess. Narration goes through `_send_player_line` only when the target is online (category `system`; offline mutates silently); `item_ref` is composed **before** any deletion. `remove_item` (#287): destruction, never transfer — the `_strip` posture (no combat gate, no protective guards). If `active_curse` is set, **the curse ends with the item** (operator-ruled): the death-teardown pattern applied to the one effect — `component_instances.filter(is_active=True).update(is_active=False, removed_by='item-removed')`, then the `EffectInstance` the same way. Artifact instances (`rarity == ARTIFACT`) delete their **definition** — the CASCADE takes the instance and the unique name/slug free for re-authoring (the freed-name law); ordinary instances delete alone, the shared definition surviving. Bar-law rescale when the target was equipped; `An admin has taken {item_ref} from you.` with `refresh_status` riding only then; result `{item_id, definition_removed}`. `edit_item` (#289): **raw-set whitelists** — the admin's values land exactly, nothing re-rolls or re-derives, `item_type` deliberately not editable. Instance-side (any owned instance, any rarity): `mk_tier` (int ≥ 1), `rarity` (any `RARITY_CHOICES` key), `rolled_primary_stats`/`rolled_secondary_stats` (through `_validate_stat_entries`, the artifact-creation bounds — floors on primary only), `damage_midpoint`/`damage_spread` (float ≥ 0 or null; both-or-neither non-null after the edit), `durability_current` (integral 0–100 since v25.12 — #314, see the durability-posture paragraph below; maintaining `is_broken == (durability == 0)`). Definition-side **only when the instance is an artifact** (an ordinary definition is a shared template — `not-artifact`): `name` (re-running the unique-name law excluding self — `name__iexact` + regenerated-slug checks, `name-taken` on collision, rarity-word names refused), `description`, `base_value`, `valid_slots`, `is_two_handed`, `armor_base`, `mystery_name`/`mystery_description` (the creation coupling judged on the post-edit state), `genre_tag` — all validated by the `_validate_artifact_spec` rules. **Any key outside the applicable whitelists refuses the whole request** (`bad-params` naming the key; the atomic block guarantees no partial application). Equipped-target edits rescale bars; narration composed after the edit (a rename shows the new name): `An admin has altered {item_ref}.`; result `{item_id, changed, definition_changed}`. `equip_item` (#288): target must not already be equipped (`already-equipped`); an explicit `slot` must be in `valid_slots` (`bad-slot` listing them). Candidates come from **`item_utils.equip_candidates`** — the player's own slot-resolution algorithm, lifted verbatim from `cmd_equip` (with `SLOT_CAPACITY` moving alongside; `cmd_equip` consumes the helper, player behavior byte-identical, the existing player-side equip tests passing unmodified). A displacement-free candidate wins outright; otherwise the minimal-displacement candidates are deduped by displaced-set — more than one distinct set refuses with **`ambiguous`**, the detail naming each option as slot + item + id (structured for the bot to relay the choice or retry with an explicit `slot`); one distinct set proceeds even when it holds two items (a two-hander displacing both hands is one option, not an ambiguity). **Structural rules always hold** (valid slots, capacity, two-hander geometry); **protective guards yield** (the `_strip` precedent: a cursed occupant comes off, capacity is ignored). Displaced items are unequipped flags-only; the target equips with the re-soulbind (`is_soulbound=True`, `soulbound_to` — byte-consistent with `equip_item`/`_dress`); **one** rescale per action; `outfit_snapshot` never read, written, or consumed. `An admin has equipped {item_ref} on you.` + `refresh_status`; result `{item_id, slot, displaced}`. `unequip_item` (#288): target must be equipped (`not-equipped`); flags off, one rescale, **no protective guards** (cursed comes off; over-capacity accepted — the #275 acceptance); curse *effects* untouched — unequip is not removal; snapshot untouched. `An admin has unequipped {item_ref}; it is in your inventory.` + `refresh_status`; result `{item_id}`.

**The memory store (v25.8 — #294).** `AgentMemory` (`models.py`, migration `0054`): generic per-bot durable storage — direct `User` FK `agent` (`related_name='agent_memories'`; agent accounts are Users, never a Character FK), `kind` (`waypoint`/`bundle` via `KIND_CHOICES` — kinds own their payload shapes, validated by the door at teach time, never by the model), `name` (≤ 60), JSON `data`, `SET_NULL` `taught_by` (`related_name='taught_agent_memories'` — audit, not ownership), `created_at` (indexed) / `updated_at`; CI-unique `(agent, kind, Lower(name))` (`agentmemory_unique_agent_kind_name_ci` — the Character-name precedent) plus an `(agent, created_at)` index. Cap constants live beside `MAX_ANSWER_LEN` in the door, not on the model: `MEMORY_MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES` 4096, `MEMORY_MAX_ROWS_PER_AGENT` 262,144 (= 1 GiB / 4 KiB, operator-ruled), `MEMORY_MAX_NAME_LEN` 60, `MEMORY_MAX_BUNDLE_LINES` 50, `MEMORY_LIST_CAP` 50. **The verbs** — every one scoped to the requesting agent's own store (`_agent_user` by username; one bot never reads or forgets another's): `remember` — kind-aware validation (`_validate_memory_data`: waypoint `data` exactly `{'room_id': <int>}` with the room existing — **the room PK and nothing else**, no stored path, nothing that can go stale; bundle `data` exactly `{'lines': [[slug, mk_tier, rarity, quantity], …]}`, 1–50 lines, every slug resolving, mk/rarity/quantity under the gift laws, artifact rarity refused — bundles replay `gift` and `gift` refuses artifacts), serialized-size and row-count caps as **distinct legible errors** (`payload-too-large`, `memory-full` — checked on create only, a replace never grows the store; `too-many-lines`), then a CI upsert by (agent, kind, name) inside `transaction.atomic()` returning `{'id', 'result': 'created'|'replaced'}` — the replace takes the new casing, data, and `taught_by` (the teaching admin's character name resolved `iexact` to their User; absent or unresolvable ⇒ null). `forget` — **by PK only, never by name**: read-before-delete, `{'forgotten': {id, kind, name}}`, `not-found` on a bad or foreign id. `memories` — optional `kind`/`name`-substring/`since`/`until` (`_parse_when`: ISO-8601, naive read as UTC) filters, newest-first by `created_at`, capped `MEMORY_LIST_CAP`, each row `{id, kind, name, summary}` with the summary **rendered live**: waypoints resolve their room now through one `in_bulk` joined query (`Zone: Area: Room`, area omitted when absent; `(room no longer exists)` when dangling), bundles report `<N> lines`. `memory` — the full row by id (`data`, `taught_by` username or null, ISO stamps); bundle `data` rendered with labeled fields — the stored form is positional, the rendered detail is legible. **Bundle replay is not a door feature** (ruled): the bot replays a bundle as N ordinary `gift` calls — fresh generation each time.

**Rooms and the origin receipt (v25.8 — #290).** `rooms`: CI substring against `Room.name` (required), optional CI zone-substring narrowing, ordered zone name then room name, capped `LIST_CAP` (50 — the §3 preamble cap shared with `events`), `_room_dict` rows; duplicate names across zones are simply multiple rows. `a_move`'s result grew `from_room` — the origin `Room` captured from the resolved character before the move (already `select_related` through `_character_by_name`), through `_room_dict`, null when the character stood nowhere. No other move behavior changed.

**Record search (v25.8 — #300).** The record is the journal. `events`: time-windowed search over `MCEvent` — defaults `until` = now, `since` = `until` − 24h (`EVENT_DEFAULT_WINDOW`); filters `kind` (exact), `actor` (int ⇒ `actor_id`, string ⇒ `actor_name__iexact`), `room_id`, and `text` (CI substring against the JSON-serialized `data` payload). **When `text` is present the span must be ≤ 7 days** (`EVENT_TEXT_WINDOW_MAX`) — `bad-params` with the legible walk-backwards message otherwise. The query filters the `ts` range and indexed fields first (the composite `(kind, ts)`/`(actor_id, ts)`/`(room_id, ts)` indexes are the point), orders newest-first, then applies `text` inside that bounded iterator walk, breaking at the cap; rows are `{stream_id, ts, kind, actor_name, room_id, gist}` with `gist` = the serialized `data` truncated to 120 chars (`EVENT_GIST_LEN`). `event`: the full record by `stream_id` — `{stream_id, ts, kind, actor_id, actor_name, room_id, audience, data}`, `not-found` on a miss.

**The rendered report (v25.8 — #296).** `report`: params `to` (the requesting admin — the `a_answer` resolution and **identical admin gate**: `_resolve_character(key='to')` + `_is_admin` ⇒ `not-admin`), `character` (the target), `kind` (must be `'inventory'`, the only v1 kind — `REPORT_KINDS`; unknown ⇒ `bad-params`). Offline recipient ⇒ `ok: true, delivered: false`, never an error (the `a_answer` posture). Delivery, in order, **privately to the recipient's pane only** (`player_{pk}` through the audited send — never a room broadcast): (1) the door-composed leader `sudo: <Name> (<N> items total)` in the `sudo` category, N counted live from total owned instances — the model never writes this line; (2) the equipped section then (3) the carried section, composed by `item_utils.equipment_doll_lines` and `item_utils.inventory_table_lines` — **the same code the player's bare `equip` and `inv` render through** (see 4.6's v25.8 extraction) — with the two fetches mirroring the player paths and the carry numbers from `carry_capacity`, each section one structured-lines `player_message` in the player `report` category. The consumer's `player_message` handler gained the lines branch (an `event['lines']` payload renders to the client exactly as `send_report_lines` sends its own — same category, same seg-form shape). Result `{'delivered', 'item_count'}`. **`a_answer` is untouched** — its cap and voice rules stand.

**The report family (v25.10 — #306).** `REPORT_KINDS = ('inventory', 'waypoints', 'memories', 'memory')`; `a_report` is kind-dispatched. Common to every kind: `to` resolved and admin-gated exactly as before; unknown kind ⇒ the `bad-params` naming all four kinds; offline recipient ⇒ `ok: true, delivered: false`, nothing sent. **`inventory`** is the v25.8 report **byte-identical** (`_report_inventory` holds the unchanged sequence; the pre-existing tests pass with zero edits). **`waypoints`/`memories`**: the **calling agent's** store (`_agent_user(agent_name)` — one bot never reports another's rows), `-created_at`, capped `MEMORY_LIST_CAP` (50) with the store's **true total counted before the slice** (`_memory_report_rows` — the `_memories_payload` query shapes with a `count()` ahead of the window, one `in_bulk` joined live room query). Leader (via `_send_player_line`, category `sudo`, the inventory-report pattern): `sudo: {total} waypoints` / `sudo: {total} memories`, with ` (newest {N} shown)` appended when total > rows shown; **when total is 0 the leader alone is the report** (no lines frame). Lines (one `player_message`, category `report`, `lines` list — each entry a value-voice dict `{'v': text}` per the client report-lines contract, which renders `k`/`v`/`segs` dict entries and renders a bare string as a blank line; `_value_lines` wraps): `#{id} {name} — {path}` per waypoint row and `#{id} {kind} {name} — {summary}` per memories row — paths rendered live per the `_memory_summary` semantics (`Zone: Area: Room`, area omitted when absent; `(room no longer exists)` when dangling; bundle summary `{N} lines`). Results `{'delivered', 'count': total}`. **`memory`**: integer `id` required (`_require_int`); the calling agent's row or `not-found` in the `q_memory` message form (`_memory_report_detail` — the `_memory_payload` row with the waypoint path resolved live and the positional bundle lines decoded legible). Leader `sudo: {kind} '{name}' (id {id})`; lines — waypoint `where: {path}`, bundle one `{quantity}× {slug} Mk {mk_tier} {rarity}` line per stored line, then `taught by {username}` (`taught by (unknown)` when null) and `created {ISO-8601} / updated {ISO-8601}`. Result `{'delivered', 'id'}`. Delivery shares one tail (`_deliver_report`: presence check, sudo leader, one lines frame when lines exist). **Machinery composes, the game renders, model prose is commentary** (the standing doctrine, GDD §10.11) — nothing about these reports is model-formatted.

**Structural receipts (v25.9 — #302).** `a_move` grew a third mutually exclusive destination key, **`waypoint`** — exactly-one-of-three (`to_name`/`to_room_id`/`waypoint`, the `bad-params` message naming all three). Resolution is **lookup-and-act atomic in the door**: `_waypoint_row` scopes to the calling agent's own store exactly as the memory verbs do (`_agent_user(agent_name)`, kind waypoint, `name__iexact`) — one bot never resolves through another's waypoints, and the bot never handles a room id for a taught place. No memory of the name ⇒ `not-found` naming the waypoint; the stored `{room_id}` resolving to no room ⇒ `not-found` naming both the waypoint and the vanished room id (the legible-refusal law). Everything downstream — combat refusal, origin capture, online/offline handling, arrival narration, MC records — is the standing `a_move` path; the result gains `'waypoint'` (the row's cased name) only when the waypoint form was used, the `room`/`from_room` shape untouched. `a_answer` grew machinery-only **`receipts`**: an optional list of 1–20 non-empty strings ≤ 200 chars each (`MAX_RECEIPTS` 20, `MAX_RECEIPT_LEN` 200, beside `MAX_ANSWER_LEN`; every violation a distinct `bad-params` naming the rule broken); `text` is optional when receipts are present (the 1–2000 rule unchanged when given; neither present ⇒ `bad-params` via the same text check). Online delivery: the `sudo: {text}` line exactly as before (skipped when no text), then **one `sudo did: {receipt}` line per receipt in list order**, all category `sudo`, each through `_send_player_line` → `audited_send` — receipts appear in the MC record like all door output, and the door-composed `sudo did:` prefix means model text can never occupy line-start position on a receipt line. Offline (`delivered: false`, nothing sent, never an error) and the result shape are unchanged. No new kinds, no client change, no kill-switch change — both shapes ride existing choke points.

**The durability posture (v25.12 — #311/#314).** `_create_artifact` authors `takes_durability_loss=False, durability_table=[]` on every definition it creates — **artifacts don't wear** (the model-default pair was the incoherent posture behind #311's say-voice, fully-penalized pristine artifacts), and `_validate_artifact_spec`'s vocabulary gains no durability keys. `ARTIFACT_DEFINITION_EDIT_KEYS` grows `takes_durability_loss` and `durability_table`: the type checks are door-side (`bad-params` — boolean and list respectively, naming the rule), the posture itself is judged **on post-edit state** through `models.durability_posture_violations` (the mystery-coupling structure — apply, then validate the pair), any violation ⇒ `bad-params` carrying the violation text; whole-request refusal and the artifact-only gate come free from `_edit_item`'s existing structure — nothing is duplicated. **#314:** the `durability_current` instance edit is integral-only — an `int`, or a float whose value is integral (`value.is_integer()`; JSON clients may deliver `42.0`) — because every game path wears and repairs in whole points and a fractional value matches no seed band, silently drawing the 1.0 fallback penalty while rendering as a rounded whole; fractional ⇒ `bad-params` naming the integral rule, with the existing bool/range refusals and the `is_broken == (durability == 0)` derivation unchanged.

**Boundaries.** Agents never hold a DB or Redis credential; Redis and Postgres continue to publish no ports; nothing in the door touches `SkylandConsumer.connect`; no game mechanic depends on anything arriving through the door (additive, never load-bearing); MC emits remain fire-and-forget. Agent accounts (`agent-<name>`) never own a `Character` — provisioned operationally, never by code, seed, or migration.

### 4.23 The sudo bot (`agents/sudo_bot.py`) — v25.6 brief 1, #262; v25.7 brief 1, #292 + the item tools; v25.8 brief 1, the memory/record tools + #299 target-scoped state; v25.9 brief 1, #302 the typed receipts ledger; v25.10 brief 1, #301 `file_issue` + #304/#305 truthful delivery; v25.11 brief 1, #308 the zero-tool bounce; v25.12 brief 1, the durability-vocabulary mirror

**The home.** The repo's top-level `agents/` directory (formalized #279) is the bot-side world: scripts are committed (`sudo_bot.py`, the `mc_test_agent.py`/`mc_door_agent.py` throwaway drivers, `requirements.txt` — `requests`, `websockets`, `anthropic`); everything runtime is gitignored (`.secrets/` for credential files, `venvs/` recreated per the requirements file's own instructions, `*.log`, `*.pid`, the conversation file). Nothing under `agents/` enters the Django image, ever — the bot is a remote client of the game, exactly like a player's browser, and its only game-facing surface is the door (Section 4.22).

**Process shape.** One file, asyncio, single instance by pidfile. A reader task routes inbound frames — `result` frames to per-request futures matched by id (`sudo-N`, the serial-per-connection contract makes this trivially safe), `event` frames to the worker's queue; everything else (`pong`, `error`, `gap`) is quietly ignored. The worker consumes tail events one at a time and reacts only to `cmd` records with `verb == 'sudo'` and non-empty `args`; a pinger keeps idle connections warm (30s app-level ping over websockets' own keepalive). The run loop owns the lifecycle: login (CSRF GET → credential POST → cookie; password from a `.secrets/` file, never an argument, never logged) → connect → `hello` verification (`protocol != 2` is a refuse-to-run `FatalError`, not a retry) → `query commands` → system prompt build → `attach` → serve. `cmd_run` normalizes `--url` once at config time — `rstrip('/')` (v25.7, #292: a trailing slash composed a double-slash login POST whose 302 masqueraded as a credential failure; one rstrip covers the login and WebSocket URLs), and the refused-login warning appends the response's `Location` header when present (the double-slash tell). Subcommands `run`/`status`/`stop` work through the pidfile; SIGTERM is a clean close (conversations saved, pidfile removed). Logs are UTC (`Z`-suffixed — project convention), append-only, with every pre-flight refusal logged (the documented `nohup` launch line swallows stderr). Model requests run in a thread (`asyncio.to_thread`) so the stream and pings never stall behind the API.

**The brain.** `Brain.respond(system, history, tools) → BrainTurn` (tool calls and/or text, plus the provider-shaped raw content echoed back into history) is the whole provider contract. `ClaudeBrain` — the official `anthropic` SDK; model, max-tokens from config (`claude-sonnet-5` / 5000 by default; no temperature or other sampling parameters — Sonnet 5 rejects non-default values); logs `usage.input_tokens`/`output_tokens` per request. `StubBrain` — deterministic canned behavior (any request → `where_is` on its last word → one-line answer), the no-model, no-key end-to-end proof. `ollama` is reserved: a future brain drops in by configuration, nothing else changes. **The tool loop lives in the bot, not the brain** (cap 8 iterations): tools presented = the door vocabulary minus `answer` and `is_admin` (delivery and the pre-check are bot machinery, never model choices); schemas mirror `mc_door`'s param shapes exactly (enum values from the model choices — item types, genre tags, rarities, slot codes); door refusals come back as error tool-results the model turns into polite replies — never a crash, never a pane error. The v25.7 item vocabulary (#287/#288/#289/#293) rides the same generics: six new tool schemas (`inventory`, `item`, `remove_item`, `edit_item`, `equip_item`, `unequip_item` — `_execute_tool` needed no change, verified generic), and the system prompt carries the two item laws — **resolve items through `inventory` (and `item` where detail matters) before proposing any item write, never guessing or reusing an id**, and **destructive/mutating actions act only on an explicitly admin-named target** (ambiguous resolution — several matching items, an ambiguous equip displacement — is relayed to the admin, never picked).

**Conversations.** Bot-side only, by ruling: keyed by admin character name, each thread ≤20 exchanges (request + final answer as plain text — a deliberately provider-neutral shape any future bot can reuse), 600s quiet expiry checked at the next request (an expired thread starts fresh with no comment — indistinguishable from the bot never answering), persisted to a gitignored local JSON file so a restart doesn't drop a live artifact Q&A. Every request turn is stamped `[Name]`, so the model always knows who is talking and first-person requests resolve against the requester; the prompt requires a fresh query for location/status answers (never stale conversation memory) and a `where_is` before every `move` (belt and suspenders since v25.8 — the door's move receipt now carries `from_room` itself, #290).

**The v25.8 tools and standing orders (#294/#290/#300/#296/#299).** Eight tools join the catalog and routing sets — `memories`, `memory`, `remember`, `forget`, `rooms`, `events`, `event`, `report` — same `{name, description, input_schema}` shape, schemas mirroring the door's param shapes (memory kinds enumerated; `events.actor` typed int-or-string; `report.kind` pinned to `inventory`). The system prompt's new paragraph: **taught facts go in durable memory, not conversation** (a taught place is a waypoint; items handed out together are a bundle; bundle replay is ordinary `gift` calls, fresh generation each time); `forget` takes a memory id looked up through `memories` first, never guessed; **"what happened" questions are answered from the durable record** — `events`/`event` with time windows walking backwards from now, not from conversation memory; inventory questions for admins prefer the `report` action (the game renders the report into their pane itself) over hand-written rosters. **Target-scoped state (#299):** `PIDFILE`/`CONVO_FILE` constants became `pidfile(target)`/`convo_file(target)` — `.sudo_bot.<target>.pid` and `.sudo_bot_conversations.<target>.json` — with a required `--target {dev,prod}` on `run`, `status`, and `stop`; one checkout can host a dev-facing and a prod-facing bot side by side, and a dev stop is incapable of touching the prod bot's pidfile by construction. No state-file migration: stale unsuffixed files are left in place, ignored; the operator restarts bots after deploy (the standing action).

**The typed receipts ledger and action log (v25.9 — #302).** The bot never invents a value it should have read from the database — enforced by machinery at `_execute_tool`, the single choke point, never asked of the model (the v25.8 prompt standing orders stand verbatim as defense-in-depth). Per sudo request — one request = one turn, the standing orders' "current turn" made literal — `_handle_sudo` creates a **`ReceiptLedger`** (four id-spaces: **room** int, **item** int instance ids, **memory** int, **stream** str) and an **`ActionLog`**, threaded through the tool loop. **Checked arguments** (`CHECKED_ARGS`): before the door is called, `move.to_room_id` (room), the five item-write `item_id`s (item), `memory`/`forget` `id` (memory), and `event.stream_id` (stream) must each be present in the matching space or the call returns an error tool_result `{'error': 'unreceipted-id', 'detail': "'<arg>' <value> is not from a lookup this turn — look it up first."}` with the door never called — the model recovers inside the loop by looking the value up; `move` with `waypoint` or `to_name` has no checked argument (nothing to invent). **Harvest** (`HARVEST`): a per-tool result-path map (`room.id`, `rooms[].id`, `items[].id`, `memories[].id`, `events[].stream_id`, `forgotten.id`, waypoint `data.room_id`, the item-write `item_id` echoes, …) applied to **successful results only**, missing keys skipped silently (a field the door stops sending must not crash the bot). The map is deliberately not a generic integer sweep — **the typing is the point**: v25.8's playtest failure passed a receipted integer (a memory's own id) into the wrong id-space, and an untyped ledger would have licensed it. The `ActionLog` appends one composed receipt per successful action except `answer` — bot machinery from params + door result, never model text — with **identical (slug, mk_tier, rarity, to) gifts aggregated to a single `×N` receipt** (ids omitted when aggregated; per-instance ids live in the MC record — without this a 50-line bundle replay would flood the bound); composed receipts are truncated to the door's 200-char bound (ellipsis) and capped at 20, oldest kept, overflow dropped with a log line. `_deliver(actor, text, receipts)` includes `receipts` when non-empty, and **delivery happens when there is text OR receipts** — a silent-model turn whose actions succeeded delivers receipts-only (the admin always sees what was actually done; model prose is commentary, receipts are the record); a turn with neither stays silent exactly as before. Schema/prompt tightening rides along: the `move` schema carries `waypoint` ("for taught places, always prefer this over to_room_id") and exactly-one-of-three; every checked argument's description states the "from a tool result in this turn; unreceipted ids are refused" contract; the prompt's waypoint clause passes the name to `move` directly (never resolving a room id bot-side), and one added sentence — the game renders `sudo did:` lines for every action automatically; never enumerate your own receipts.

**Truthful delivery and the filing bot (v25.10 — #301/#304/#305/#306).** **The runtime relocation (#304):** `RUNTIME_DIR = Path.home() / '.shyland'` — `pidfile(target)`, `convo_file(target)`, and the `--log` default all derive from it (`sudo_bot.<target>.pid`, `sudo_bot_conversations.<target>.json`, `sudo_bot.log` — undotted, operator-directed: state in a dedicated runtime directory needs no hiding; `cmd_run` creates the directory before the pidfile write); code and `agents/.secrets/` stay checkout-scoped. No state migration — old per-checkout files are abandoned in place (conversations expire in 600s; pidfiles are transient; logs are history), pruned by the operator. `CLOSE_MEANINGS` gains **4409** ("refused: another connection for this account is already attached") — no behavior change: the existing capped backoff is the correct response, the refused bot waits patiently for the holder to die, then attaches (the 25.9 two-bots incident shape, now failing safe behind the door's singleton). **The store remembers what the game confirmed (#305):** `_handle_sudo` computes `receipts = actions.receipts()` **before** the record call; the stored answer folds the receipt lines in as plain text — one `[did: {receipt}]` line per receipt, newline-joined after the text (text absent ⇒ the `[did: …]` lines alone; neither text nor receipts ⇒ the empty answer exactly as before); `record()`'s signature and the `{'q','a'}` exchange shape are unchanged. The `[did: …]` form belongs to machinery, enforced in two layers: a would-be final answer carrying a `[did:]` mark while the turn's `ActionLog` holds **no receipts** is a machine-detected fabrication (the mark is the lie's signature — observed live: the model pattern-completes 'Sent — …' answers from replayed report history without acting) and is **bounced back into the tool loop** with a corrective message — the model performs the real call or drops the claim (bounded by `TOOL_LOOP_CAP`); and any model-written `[did:]` line surviving to delivery is **stripped before delivery and storage** (the `sudo:` double-prefix-guard precedent, logged when either layer fires). A prompt sentence in the receipts-not-intentions paragraph closes the poisoned-history hole: earlier turns' answers carry `[did: …]` lines for every game-confirmed action — **a past answer claiming an action without one performed nothing**, and is never a license to skip a tool call now (the v25.9 standing orders stand beneath it — defense-in-depth). **Filing (#301):** `BOT_ACTIONS = frozenset({'file_issue'})` — bot-local actions; `_execute_tool` (its signature now threading `actor_name`) runs them **before** the door round trip, so they never become door frames (**the door grows no filing vocabulary; the game box holds no GitHub credentials**); their successes join the `ActionLog` with **no ledger harvest** — issue numbers belong to no door id-space, deliberately. `_file_issue`: missing/empty/non-string `title` or `body` ⇒ `bad-params` naming the field (the model finishes the draft in its loop); no token ⇒ `not-configured` ("no GitHub token on this bot host; filing is disabled") — a legible, model-relayed refusal; otherwise `asyncio.to_thread(requests.post, …)` to the GitHub issues API (`GITHUB_REPO = 'KnightOfNight/games-mvc'`, `GITHUB_API`, `GITHUB_ASSIGNEE = 'KnightOfNight'`, `GITHUB_TIMEOUT = 15`) with the drafted body + the provenance footer `_Filed via sudo (in-game) by {actor_name}, {UTC ISO-8601}Z._` and the assignee applied by machinery — **title + body only, no labels** (thin is doctrine; triage fattens). HTTP 201 ⇒ `{'number', 'url', 'title'}` from the response; anything else ⇒ `{'error': 'github', 'detail': 'HTTP {status}'}` with the response body never echoed into the detail (a bounded log excerpt instead); attempt and outcome logged, no secrets ever. The receipt — `filed issue #{number}: {url}`, the API response's values, never model text — rides the turn's `answer` delivery and persists with the stored answer (#305): **the confirm gate is behavioral by design this release** (the tool description and a prompt paragraph: gather Q&A style like artifact work, thin is correct, read the complete draft back verbatim, file only on an explicit yes, never restate a number from memory, decline plainly when filing is disabled); **the filing's honesty is structural**. Token custody: `cmd_run` reads `GITHUB_TOKEN` from the environment into config (injected by botctl from `agents/.secrets/github-token.<name>`); the startup log says only `filing=enabled|disabled`. **The report tools (#306):** the `report` schema grows the four kinds (`character` moves out of schema-required — the door validates authoritatively; integer `id` added for the `memory` kind), `_compose_receipt`'s report branch goes kind-aware (`report ({kind})[ on {character}] to {to}: {status}` — ` on {character}` only when passed, never `on None`), and the prefer-report standing order extends over the bot's own store: listings and memory detail render deterministically in the game; the `memories`/`memory` *queries* serve the bot's own next step, not the admin's eyes.

**The zero-tool bounce (v25.11 — #308).** The unmarked sibling of v25.10's marked fabrication: delivery-claim prose on a turn with zero tool calls carries no `[did:]` mark, no receipts, and no machine-detectable signature — observed live twice in the 25.10 playtest ("Sent — full memory list delivered to your pane.", nothing delivered, an immediate retry always working). The fix is signature-free and closes the whole class without classifying prose: two per-request locals beside the ledger (`ran_tool`, set on the tool-execution branch whenever calls execute — success or error result alike; `bounced`, the single-shot latch) and one gate in the no-tool-calls branch, evaluated **after** the receiptless-`[did:]` gate (which is unchanged, loop-capped and multi-fire; a zero-tool `[did:]` answer necessarily has no receipts, so the existing gate owns it). **Condition:** a nonempty would-be final answer + no tool call executed at any point this turn + not already bounced ⇒ append the answer as the assistant turn plus a corrective `[game]`-register user message ("Your answer made no tool call this turn. If the request asks you to see or do something, do it now with a tool call — machinery reports only what it confirms. If it was just conversation, give your answer again — your next message is delivered to the admin's pane, so speak to them directly, never about this exchange." — the audience clause operator-ruled in the 25.11 playtest: the first wording drew redo answers addressed to the machinery, meta-commentary the single-shot delivery then shipped to the pane), log `zero-tool answer for {actor} — one corrective redo`, and `continue`; the redo's answer is delivered without a second bounce, subject only to the existing gates. **Silence is deliberately exempt** — a no-text, no-tool turn stays sanctioned exactly as before; the bounce consumes one `TOOL_LOOP_CAP` iteration and cap exhaustion falls through to the strip guard and delivery path unchanged. The ruled cost, accepted explicitly: every genuinely conversational turn draws exactly one extra model round trip.

**Reconnect and the kill switch.** The session cookie is obtained once and reused across every reconnect (#284's bot-side mitigation); the only re-login path is a refused handshake. All disconnects — 4503 included — feed one quiet retry loop with capped exponential backoff (2s base, 60s cap, reset on successful attach) and exactly one log line per attempt cycle: killed is an expected, indefinite state, not an error, and recovery after `mc restore` is unaided. No pane output exists for any of this — silence is never an error, in every failure mode.

**Boundaries.** The model's tool calls are proposals; the bot is the executor; the server validates and records every effect (Section 4.22's audited machinery) — three independent layers between the model and the world. The bot holds no capability outside the door vocabulary: no ops powers, no shell, no DB, no Redis, by design and by construction. `agent-sudo` is an ordinary Django account in `agents.shyland` with no `Character`, provisioned operationally per environment (dev in-session; production by the operator in the closeout tail's deploy window).

### 4.24 The bot manager (`agents/botctl.py`) — v25.7 brief 1, #295; v25.8 brief 1, #299 target-scoped paths; v25.10 brief 1, #304 the central runtime directory + #301 token injection

**The tool.** One command to start, stop, restart, status, or tail an agent bot against a named target: `botctl.py <prod|dev> <start|stop|restart|status|tail> [--bot NAME]` (default `sudo`). **Stdlib-only and system-python-3.9 compatible** — the manager itself needs no venv; it is the tool that reports a missing one. List-argv throughout, never `shell=True`; executable with a `python3` shebang; its own printed progress lines carry UTC-Z stamps (project convention). It replaces the operator's `$HOME` wrapper scripts (`sudo-bot.sh`/`test-sudo-bot.sh`), which the operator retires personally — sessions never touch files outside the repo.

**Self-location (the law).** The repo root derives from `Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent` — **the copy you run is the checkout it manages**: the main checkout for prod, the current version worktree for dev. No baked-in absolute paths anywhere.

**Targets.** One documented constants block: `prod` → `https://games.magrathea.com`; `dev` → `https://emma.private.magrathea.com`. **Neither target rides `--insecure`** (v25.10, operator-directed: the renewed wildcard cert is live on dev, so both stacks verify TLS fully; the per-target flag mechanism remains for any future target that genuinely needs it). URLs are rstripped again at use (belt and suspenders with #292's bot-side normalization).

**Per-(bot, target) derivation (v25.8, #299).** `BotPaths(name, target)` derives the module (`agents/<name>_bot.py`), the **target-suffixed** log (`agents/<name>_bot.<target>.log`), and the key file (`agents/.secrets/anthropic-api-key.<name>` — the key is per bot, not per target); the bot-owned pid/conversation paths (`.<name>_bot.<target>.pid`, `.<name>_bot_conversations.<target>.json`) are listed on the object as the one map for humans debugging state files. Missing module or key file ⇒ clear error, nonzero exit. Missing venv (`agents/venvs/mc-agent/bin/python`) ⇒ a clear error naming the exact fix (`python3 -m venv … && pip install -r agents/requirements.txt`) — **never an auto-install**.

**The verbs.** `start`: reads the key and places it in the **child environment only** (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` — never argv, never echoed, never logged; the committed file contains no secret values), spawns the venv python on the bot module's `run` subcommand — passing `--target` since v25.8 — detached (`start_new_session=True`, stdout/stderr → the log file, cwd = repo root), then polls the bot's own `status` subcommand (bounded, ~10 s, no blind sleeps) and finishes with a short log tail — exit 0 only when status reports running. `stop`: invokes the bot's `stop` for the named target, polls `status` until down (bounded), tails, propagates failure nonzero — **a dev stop cannot touch the prod bot** (distinct pidfiles by construction). `restart`: stop-then-start, each gated on its poll (a not-running bot goes straight to start). `status`: pure pass-through — the bot's own target-scoped line and exit code are the answer. `tail`: follows the target's log (`tail -f`). Proven on the dev cycle with honest exit codes: status-down 1, start 0, status-up 0, stop 0, status-down 1; the v25.8 coexistence check re-proved the family target-scoped (dev running while prod status reports not-running and prod stop is a no-op).

**The central runtime directory and the token (v25.10 — #304/#301).** `RUNTIME_DIR = Path.home() / '.shyland'` — `BotPaths` now derives the log (`~/.shyland/<name>_bot.<target>.log`) and the bot-owned pid/convo paths (`{name}_bot.{target}.pid`, `{name}_bot_conversations.{target}.json` — undotted, operator-directed) under it, generalized on `{name}`; `cmd_start` creates the directory before opening the log. **`module` and `key_file` stay checkout-scoped** — code belongs to the checkout it ships in, secrets stay under `agents/.secrets/`. Every checkout's manager now sees the same state files, so a same-target bot running from another checkout is visible; for a bot started by *old* code (its pidfile in the old per-checkout location, invisible to new botctl), the door's 4409 attach singleton is the structural backstop — the new bot logs `already attached` and retries instead of double-acting. No state migration: old per-checkout files abandoned in place, pruned by the operator. `BotPaths` also gains `github_token_file` (`agents/.secrets/github-token.<name>`, optional — #301): when the file exists and is non-empty, `cmd_start` injects its stripped contents as `GITHUB_TOKEN` into the **child environment only** (the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` custody pattern — never argv, never echoed, never logged); an absent token file is not an error — the bot refuses filing legibly at call time instead.

## 5. Data Flow Diagrams

*(unchanged from v15)*
